### [1] [Cohomology of $\mathfrak{psl}(n|n)$ in trivial and in adjoint representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22598)
*Thiago Oliveira Ferreira*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the cohomology of the Lie superalgebras 𝔭𝔰𝔩(n|n) (trivial and adjoint coefficients) and 𝔰𝔩(m|n) (trivial coefficients) using Hochschild–Serre spectral sequences, corrects a prior result in the literature, and provides explicit low-degree cohomology groups for small n; results have applications in AdS/CFT via 𝔭𝔰𝔲(2,2|4).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the cohomology of projective special linear Lie superalgebras — especially 𝔭𝔰𝔩(n|n) — due to their relevance in theoretical physics (e.g., AdS/CFT correspondence via the real form 𝔭𝔰𝔲(n|n)), and to resolve a discrepancy in previously reported cohomology of 𝔰𝔩(m|n).

Method: Systematic computation via Hochschild–Serre spectral sequences associated with the natural filtration of the Lie superalgebras; includes analysis of the spectral sequence convergence and explicit determination of differentials and extensions.

Result: Full cohomology of 𝔭𝔰𝔩(n|n) in trivial and adjoint coefficients; cohomology of 𝔰𝔩(m|n) in trivial coefficients (correcting prior literature); explicit low-degree cohomology groups for n ≤ 4; identification of a misstatement in earlier work on 𝔰𝔩(m|n) cohomology.

Conclusion: The cohomological structure of 𝔭𝔰𝔩(n|n) is fully determined and shown to be nontrivial in multiple degrees; the correction to 𝔰𝔩(m|n) cohomology underscores the necessity of careful spectral sequence analysis; results provide foundational data for physical applications involving superconformal symmetry and holography.

Abstract: We compute the cohomology of the projective special linear Lie superalgebra $\mathfrak{psl}(n|n)$ over a field of characteristic zero in trivial and in adjoint coefficients in full detail through its associated Hochschild--Serre spectral sequences. Applications in physics exist for its real form, the projective special unitary Lie superalgebra $\mathfrak{psu}(n|n)$ --- in particular, $\mathfrak{psu}(2,2|4)$ for AdS/CFT. We also find the cohomology of $\mathfrak{sl}(m|n)$ in trivial coefficients along the way and we point out a discrepancy with a previous statement in the literature. We present the explicit cohomology groups of low degree for $n\leqslant4$.

</details>


### [2] [Distributional Fractional Taylor Series and Interpretation of the Fractal and Number-Theoretic Explicit Formulas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22759)
*Michel L. Lapidus,Matthew Overduin*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a distributional fractal Taylor's formula and exact fractal Taylor series for generalized fractal strings, expressing residues in explicit formulas via fractional distributional derivatives of the Dirac delta applied to logarithmically modified test functions, under languidity or strong languidity assumptions on the geometric zeta function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Lapidus and van Frankenhuijsen's fractal explicit formulas by constructing a distributional fractal Taylor expansion that encodes geometric and spectral information of fractal strings via their complex dimensions.

Method: Uses distributional calculus and Mellin transform theory; rewrites residue contributions in fractal explicit formulas as fractional-order distributional derivatives $Y_ω$ of the Dirac δ-distribution acting on test functions $φ(x)\ln^{k-1}(x)$, leveraging Laurent coefficients of $ζ_η$ at poles $ω$ and $Γ(ω)$.

Result: Derives a distributional fractal Taylor's formula with error term under languidity, and an exact (error-free) version under strong languidity; expresses each residue term explicitly using $Y_ω[φ(x)\ln^{k-1}(x)]$, Laurent coefficients, and gamma values.

Conclusion: Provides a rigorous distributional framework for fractal Taylor expansions tied to complex dimensions, advancing the characterization of fractal geometry through analytic properties of geometric zeta functions.

Abstract: Motivated by the work of M. L. Lapidus and M. van Frankenhuisjen on fractal explicit formulas for generalized fractal strings, we develop in this paper a distributional fractal Taylor's formula with error term and an exact distributional fractal Taylor series representation for generalized fractal strings. The explicit formulas that the aforementioned authors developed provide a way of expressing a generalized fractal string $η$ in terms of the underlying fractal complex dimensions, under suitable growth assumptions on its geometric zeta function, $ζ_η$, known as the languidity assumptions. This involves a sum indexed by the complex dimensions of the generalized fractal string, summing over all residues of $ζ_η$, multiplied by the Mellin transform of a tempered or Schwartz test function $φ$, denoted by $\widetildeφ$. Under languidity assumptions, there is an error term present. Under stronger assumptions, that is, in the case of strong languidity, no error term is present and the resulting fractal explicit formula is said to be exact. In this paper, we express the residue term in the fractional distributional explicit formulas as the fractional $(-ω)^{\mathrm{th}}$-ordered distributional derivative of the Dirac $δ$ distribution, denoted by $Y_ω$, applied to modified test functions of the form $φ(x) \ln^{k-1}(x)$, where $k$ runs from $1$ to the multiplicity of the pole $ω$ of $ζ_η$. The coefficients in this sum are written in terms of the coefficients in the principal part of the Laurent expansion at $ω$ of the geometric zeta function $ζ_η$, multiplied by the gamma function evaluated at $ω$. The results obtained in this paper contribute to the broader program of characterizing fractals in terms of fractal Taylor series expansions involving their underlying fractal complex dimensions.

</details>


### [3] [Holomorphic Conformal Cartan Geometry: Conformal Gravity, Twistors, and Ambitwistors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22861)
*Chen-Hsu Chien*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper unifies 4D conformal gravity, twistor theory, and ambitwistor theory under holomorphic conformal Cartan geometry, using spinorial formalism to derive Weyl–Bach gravity, recover twistor incidence relations and chiral integrability (ASD/SD), and realize ambitwistor space as the parameter space of complex null geodesics encoding both chiralities of Weyl curvature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a unified, gauge-theoretic geometric framework connecting four-dimensional conformal gravity, twistor theory, and ambitwistor theory—beyond ad hoc constructions—by leveraging holomorphic conformal Cartan geometry and its spinorial realization.

Method: Develops holomorphic conformal Cartan geometry using the spinorial representation of SO(4,ℂ) ≅ SL(4,ℂ)/ℤ₂; constructs the normal sl(4,ℂ)-valued Cartan connection, its curvature, and tractor bundles; restricts to the real Lorentzian slice with Weyl gauge fixing; builds local/dual twistor bundles as spin tractor bundles; uses invariant pairing to define ambitwistor space; analyzes parallel transport, incidence relations, and chiral integrability conditions (α- and β-surface equations).

Result: 1) Quadratic curvature action on Lorentzian slice yields Weyl–Bach conformal gravity; 2) Local twistor bundles encode flat-space incidence and enforce anti-self-dual (α) or self-dual (β) constraints in curved backgrounds; 3) Ambitwistor space—defined via canonical pairing of twistors and dual twistors—parameterizes complex null geodesics and symmetrically incorporates both left- and right-handed Weyl curvatures.

Conclusion: Holomorphic conformal Cartan geometry provides a natural, intrinsic gauge-theoretic foundation unifying conformal gravity, twistor theory, and ambitwistor theory—revealing their structural interdependence through tractor bundles, chiral integrability, and null-geodesic geometry.

Abstract: Four-dimensional conformal gravity, twistor theory, and ambitwistor theory are reviewed within the unified framework of holomorphic conformal Cartan geometry. Starting from the spinorial representation of the geometry, the normal $\mathfrak{sl}(4,\mathbb{C})$-valued Cartan connection, its curvature $2$-form, and the associated tractor bundles are systematically formulated. Evaluated on the real Lorentzian slice, the quadratic curvature action is shown to reproduce four-dimensional Weyl--Bach conformal gravity upon fixing a Weyl gauge. Local twistor and dual twistor bundles are constructed as associated spin tractor bundles, illustrating how parallel transport yields the classical incidence relations in flat space and chiral integrability constraints in curved backgrounds: anti-self-duality for $α$-surfaces and self-duality for $β$-surfaces. Furthermore, by pairing local twistors and dual twistors via an invariant canonical pairing, ambitwistor space is shown to parameterize complex null geodesics and symmetrically accommodate both left- and right-handed Weyl curvatures. This framework establishes a direct gauge-theoretic bridge linking conformal Cartan geometry to twistor and ambitwistor theories.

</details>


### [4] [Higher-order and non-geometric symmetries and Noether's theorem in k-cosymplectic Hamiltonian field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23034)
*Manuel de León,Xuefeng Zhao*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces generalized infinitesimal symmetries in k-cosymplectic Hamiltonian field theory, defines two new symmetry classes—higher-order general infinitesimal Cartan symmetries and good non-geometric symmetries—and proves corresponding Noether-type theorems yielding conserved quantities; it extends these results to k-symplectic manifolds and provides illustrative examples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically generalize symmetries and conservation laws beyond standard Cartan symmetries in geometric field theory, particularly addressing higher-order and non-geometric symmetries in k-cosymplectic and k-symplectic Hamiltonian frameworks.

Method: Introduces generalized infinitesimal symmetries by relaxing the standard symmetry condition; defines two novel symmetry classes; derives Noether-type theorems for exact k-cosymplectic Hamiltonian systems; extends all constructions analogously to k-symplectic manifolds; validates via concrete examples.

Result: Explicit Noether-type theorems yielding conserved quantities for both new symmetry classes in k-cosymplectic and k-symplectic settings; unification of symmetry analysis across both geometric frameworks; demonstration of existence and applicability through examples.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides a coherent, geometrically intrinsic treatment of generalized symmetries and associated conservation laws in classical field theories, significantly extending prior results on Cartan symmetries and bridging higher-order and non-geometric symmetry concepts.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate higher-order symmetries and non-geometric symmetries within the framework of $k$-cosymplectic Hamiltonian field theory. We introduce the concept of generalized infinitesimal symmetry, which relaxes the standard symmetry condition, and then define two new classes of symmetries: higher-order general infinitesimal Cartan symmetries and good non-geometric symmetries. For each of these symmetry classes, we establish a Noether-type theorem that provides explicit conserved quantities for exact $k$-cosymplectic Hamiltonian systems. These results extend and generalize previous work on standard Cartan symmetries in the $k$-cosymplectic setting. Furthermore, we extend all of these results in a parallel fashion to Hamiltonian systems on $k$-symplectic manifolds, demonstrating the unifying nature of our approach. Concrete examples are provided to illustrate the existence and applicability of the proposed symmetries. Our framework offers a systematic geometric treatment of generalized symmetries and their associated conservation laws in classical field theories.

</details>


### [5] [Long time stability of low--frequency packets of modes at finite total energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23119)
*Dario Bambusi,Antonio Ponno*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves energy localization in the FPUT α-model over long times by leveraging the proximity to the integrable Toda lattice, using a recombined Flaschka integral—rather than action-angle variables—to control high-frequency mode excitation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish long-time energy confinement within a low-wave-number packet in the Fermi–Pasta–Ulam–Tsingou (FPUT) α-model, addressing the lack of rigorous control on energy transfer in weakly nonlinear lattices.

Method: Constructs a modified Flaschka integral of the Toda lattice whose quadratic part serves as an adiabatic invariant for the FPUT system; higher-order terms are shown to be uniformly small; employs comparison with the Toda flow and energy-based estimates to bound mode coupling.

Result: For initial energy E concentrated in Fourier modes with wave number ≤ θ_*, no significant energy flows to modes with wave number > θ_* up to time scales ~ E^{−(1−ε/2)}, provided θ_* and ε are small.

Conclusion: The FPUT α-model exhibits approximate integrability on long but finite timescales, manifested as spectral energy localization, due to its closeness to the Toda lattice—quantified via controlled Flaschka integrals rather than canonical action-angle variables.

Abstract: We consider the FPUT $α$-model with $N$ particles and initial data with small total energy $E$, independent of $N$. In particular, we prove that if the energy is initially given to Fourier modes of wave number $πk/N\leq θ_*$, then it does not flow to modes of wave number larger than $θ_*$ up to long times of order $E^{-(1-ε/2)}$, $θ_*$ and $ε$ being small numbers. The proof relies on the closeness of the Toda lattice to the FPUT system. Unlike previous approaches, we do not make use of the action-angle variables of the Toda system, but we exploit the Flaschka integrals of the latter. Actually, we find a suitable recombination of the Flaschka integrals whose quadratic part is used to control the packet, the higher order part fulfilling very good estimates.

</details>


### [6] [Six-wave scattering in Hamiltonian Dysthe equations: Euler matching and integrability obstructions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23175)
*Alex J. Sutherland,Solomon C. Yim*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes six-wave interactions in Hamiltonian Dysthe equations and compares them with the deep-water Euler Hamiltonian, showing that nonzero scattering occurs on open resonance sets when cubic and mean-flow coefficients are nonzero; it identifies conditions for vanishing six-wave coefficients and proves that physical Dysthe models cannot support an infinite inverse-scattering hierarchy due to Zakharov–Schulman constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the extent to which Hamiltonian Dysthe equations capture the full six-wave resonant dynamics of the Euler equations for deep-water waves, and to characterize when higher-order resonant interactions vanish or persist under dispersion deformations.

Method: Analytical computation of effective six-wave interaction coefficients in spatial/temporal Hamiltonian Dysthe equations; comparison with the degree-six term of the Euler Hamiltonian; asymptotic analysis of resonance persistence under small cubic dispersion corrections; classification of coefficient vanishing conditions; application of the Zakharov–Schulman condition to constrain quadratic leading symbols.

Result: A uniform asymptotic formula for the six-wave coefficient $W_6$ under dispersion deformation; identification of nine-quartic-vertex origin of leading Euler six-wave term; classification of coefficient sets where $W_6$ vanishes identically; proof that only wave action, momentum, and linear energy symbols satisfy the Zakharov–Schulman condition for $C^3$ quadratic symbols; inclusion of NLS, Chen–Lee–Liu, Calogero–Moser DNLS, and Hirota equations in cancellation sets.

Conclusion: Physical Dysthe models are fundamentally limited in their capacity to support infinite integrable hierarchies due to structural constraints from the Zakharov–Schulman condition; generic six-wave scattering is present unless specific coefficient cancellations occur, linking model fidelity to exact Euler resonances.

Abstract: We compute the effective six-wave interaction in spatial and temporal Hamiltonian Dysthe equations and compare it with the complete degree-six coefficient of the deep-water Euler Hamiltonian. On every compact subset $K$ of a generic quadratic-dispersion resonance patch, exact resonances persist under a small cubic correction to the dispersion, and \[ W_6\bigl(Φ_{λ,\varepsilon}(z)\bigr) =\varepsilon\frac{4BD}{aL(z)}+O_K(\varepsilon^2) \] uniformly in the resonance point $z$ and the deformation parameter $λ$. Here $L$ is the spectral diameter and $B,D$ are the local cubic and mean-flow coefficients. Thus $BD\ne0$ gives nonzero scattering on relatively open resonance sets. At the Craig--Guyenne--Sulem temporal coefficients, the corresponding exact Euler resonances satisfy \[ \fracμ{4}W_6^{\rm E}\bigl(1+μΨ_μ(z)\bigr) =-\frac{8}{L(z)}+O_K(μ), \] where $μ$ is the relative bandwidth. The leading term comes from nine contractions of quartic vertices; every other connected degree-six contribution is uniformly bounded. We classify the coefficient values for which the generic six-wave coefficient vanishes identically; in the temporal case, this also gives cancellation on every non-permutation four-wave resonance. Outside these sets, the Zakharov--Schulman condition restricts every regular $C^3$ quadratic leading symbol to $\operatorname{span}\{1,k,ω(k)\}$, the span of the symbols of wave action, momentum, and linear energy. Consequently the physical Dysthe models cannot support an inverse-scattering hierarchy with infinitely many linearly independent regular $C^3$ quadratic leading symbols. The cancellation sets contain known nonlinear Schrödinger, mixed Chen--Lee--Liu, Calogero--Moser derivative nonlinear Schrödinger, and Hirota representatives.

</details>


### [7] [Routing and memory in the three-dimensional mirror model on K-lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23244)
*Jian Gu,Paul Schaeffer,Qin Hao*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates delocalization in the 3D Manhattan mirror (K-lattice) model via numerical experiments, revealing suppressed transmission, non-uniform channel usage, inverse-square-root decay of memory remainder, diminishing continuation losses, and finite routing cycles—suggesting candidate mechanisms for delocalization but not proving an infinite-volume delocalized phase.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe whether the three-dimensional Manhattan mirror model (K-lattice) supports a delocalized phase, by analyzing transmission, reflection, memory effects, and routing structure beyond standard Anderson-type localization diagnostics.

Method: Numerical observables analysis including: (1) conditional comparison preserving transmission/reflection aperture sets; (2) spatial suppression analysis of nearby transmitting channels; (3) return-age-resolved decomposition of repeated vertex contributions; (4) annular geometry experiments to quantify continuation losses and history dependence; (5) topological analysis of auxiliary contact graphs and routing cycles in periodic configurations.

Result: Observed statistically significant transmission deficit relative to conditional baseline; suppression of adjacent transmitting channels; signed old-memory remainder norm decaying as ~1/√t; decreasing continuation losses in annuli; failure to replicate initial history dependence; periodic configuration yields connected contact graph but only finite routing cycles.

Conclusion: The results identify concrete quantitative signatures—reflection routing constraints and adaptively selected revisit charges—that could inform a rigorous proof of delocalization, yet they do not establish existence of an infinite-volume delocalized phase.

Abstract: We study numerical observables relevant to delocalization in the three-dimensional Manhattan mirror model, also known as the K-lattice. Our principal experiment separates the locations of transmitting boundary channels from the routing between them. We derive an exact conditional comparison that preserves all transmission and reflection aperture sets, and find a statistically resolved deficit of physical transmission relative to this comparison. Nearby transmitting channels are themselves suppressed relative to a uniform subset with the same cardinality. A second experiment resolves the contribution of repeated vertices by their return age. The squared norm of the signed old-memory remainder is consistent with an inverse-square-root decay over the measured range. Annular experiments quantify decreasing continuation losses, while an initially apparent history dependence fails independent replication. Finally, a periodic configuration has a connected auxiliary contact graph but only finite routing cycles. These results identify estimates on reflection routing and adaptively selected revisit charges that may assist a rigorous proof. They do not establish an infinite-volume delocalized phase.

</details>


### [8] [Resurgence and Complex Geodesics for the Heisenberg Heat Kernel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23608)
*Xinxing Tang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes geodesic actions and heat-kernel resurgence on isotropic Heisenberg groups, deriving exact Borel–Écalle resurgent structures, Stokes matrices, alien calculus properties, and asymptotics—revealing dimensional independence, resonance-dependent confluence, and endpoint-specific summability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the resurgent structure of heat kernels on isotropic Heisenberg groups—specifically how complex geodesic actions govern Borel singularities, Stokes phenomena, and large-order asymptotics—extending resurgent analysis to sub-Riemannian geometric settings with nontrivial nilpotent symmetry.

Method: Analytic continuation of minimizing-saddle Borel germs; construction of directional Stokes operators; explicit computation of positive-ray Stokes matrices and their logarithms; uniform asymptotic analysis near Borel singularities (including two-term large-order expansions); identification of resurgent sectors via Fourier contour selection; classification of trajectories and action spectra at isotropic endpoints.

Result: Exact characterization of finite Borel singularities as complex action differences; explicit Stokes matrix and logarithm for nonresonant endpoints; strict containment of primitive alien support within Borel singular support; uniform two-term large-order asymptotics near vertical axis; $A_2$ confluence formulas for cubic resonances; exact positive Borel summation at horizontal endpoints; signed vertical action lattice as limiting spectrum at nonzero isotropic complex endpoints; dimensional independence of action and Stokes structures on $H^n$.

Conclusion: The resurgence of the heat kernel on isotropic Heisenberg groups is fully governed by geodesic action geometry, exhibiting rich but structured Stokes phenomena, resonance-sensitive confluence, and endpoint-dependent summability—unifying sub-Riemannian dynamics, spectral asymptotics, and Écalle’s alien calculus in a dimension-agnostic framework.

Abstract: We study complex geodesic actions and heat-kernel resurgence on isotropic Heisenberg groups. For real endpoints with $r>0,y\ne0$, the nonzero finite singularities reached by analytic continuation of the minimizing-saddle Borel germ are exactly the finite complex action differences. For nonresonant endpoints, we construct row-finite directional Stokes operators and compute the positive-ray Stokes matrix and its logarithm explicitly; the primitive alien support can be strictly smaller than the full Borel singular support.
  Near the vertical axis, we obtain uniform two-term large-order asymptotics from the nearest Borel singularity. Cubic resonances admit uniform $A_2$ confluence formulas. We also identify the combination of resurgent sectors selected by the original Fourier contour. Positive Borel summation is exact at horizontal endpoints. At nonzero isotropic complex endpoints, no finite normal trajectories exist, but the signed vertical action lattice is the complete limiting spectrum. The action and Stokes structures are independent of the dimension on isotropic $H^n$.

</details>


### [9] [Local conservation laws and symmetries of three-dimensional incompressible Euler flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23772)
*Alexey Shevyakov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies all smooth local conservation laws and finite-order local symmetries (and adjoint symmetries) for the 3D incompressible Euler equations, showing that only classical and time-generalized momentum laws—and their associated multipliers—exhaust the possibilities modulo trivial currents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To fully characterize the structure of conservation laws and symmetries of the 3D incompressible Euler equations—a fundamental PDE system in fluid dynamics—with rigorous account of arbitrary finite-order derivatives, explicit spacetime dependence, and equivalence up to trivial divergences.

Method: Systematic analysis using determining equations for adjoint symmetries; verification of multiplier identities; exploitation of highest-derivative response to reduce symmetry problems to first order; and use of correspondence between adjoint symmetries, conservation-law multipliers, and divergence-equivalence classes of currents.

Result: Complete classification: (i) only mass, momentum, angular momentum, kinetic energy, helicity, and time-dependent momentum conservation laws exist (up to trivial currents); (ii) all symmetry characteristics arise from point transformations; (iii) all adjoint symmetries admit multiplier representatives whose product with Euler equations is an exact divergence; (iv) these multipliers precisely correspond to restrictions of conservation-law multipliers to solutions.

Conclusion: The 3D incompressible Euler system admits no hidden or higher-order local conservation laws or symmetries beyond those generated by classical and time-generalized point symmetries; its conservation structure is completely determined by its variational and geometric origins.

Abstract: We classify all smooth local conservation laws of three-dimensional homogeneous incompressible Euler flow in velocity and pressure, allowing explicit time and position and derivatives of arbitrary finite order. Modulo currents vanishing on solutions and currents with identically zero divergence, the classical mass, momentum, angular-momentum, kinetic-energy and helicity balances, together with generalised time-dependent momentum, exhaust the classification. We also classify all smooth finite-order local symmetry characteristics and adjoint symmetries: differential expressions satisfying the linearised Euler equations and their formal adjoint on solutions, respectively. Every symmetry characteristic is equivalent on solutions to one induced by a point transformation. Every adjoint symmetry has a multiplier representative, whose product with the Euler equations is identically a divergence. These representatives exhaust the restrictions of all local conservation-law multipliers to solutions. The conservation-law proof has three stages: classification of adjoint symmetries using their determining equations, verification of explicit multiplier identities, and the correspondence between multiplier restrictions and current classes. A common analysis of highest-derivative responses reduces both symmetry problems to order at most one.

</details>


### [10] [Sum of Three Squares and Particle in a Cubic Box - Degeneracies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23794)
*S. Pratik Khastgir*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives a closed-form, exact formula for the degeneracy of energy eigenvalues of a quantum particle in a 3D cubic infinite well — equivalently, the number of representations of a positive integer as a sum of three natural-number squares — based on empirical pattern analysis of the first five million cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve the long-standing number-theoretic problem of counting representations of a positive integer as a sum of three natural number squares, motivated by quantum mechanical degeneracy in a 3D infinite cubic potential well.

Method: Empirical pattern recognition and induction: generated degeneracy data for the first 5 million integers using Mathematica, analyzed sequences, inferred structural regularities (e.g., dependence on prime factorization modulo 8), and synthesized a closed-form formula without formal proof.

Result: A closed-form, exact expression for the degeneracy d(n), i.e., the number of ordered triples (l,m,n) ∈ ℕ³ such that l² + m² + n² = N, derived from observed arithmetic patterns tied to the prime factorization of N.

Conclusion: The degeneracy problem admits an exact, computable closed-form solution grounded in empirical number-theoretic regularities; while lacking rigorous proof, the formula is extensively validated numerically up to N = 5×10⁶.

Abstract: We address the problem of determining the degeneracy of a particular energy eigenvalue corresponding to a quantum particle trapped in a cubic box. In other words, we aim to find the number of ways a specific positive integer can be written as the sum of three natural number squares. This is a classic problem in number theory. We provide a closed-form, exact solution to the problem. We do not claim to provide proofs, as we have none; instead, we build up the formulae starting from simple cases where the answer could be deduced by studying the list of degeneracies. We present ample examples that could be verified against the degeneracy calculated numerically via the brute-force method. We arrived at the solution by meticulously studying the list of degeneracies for the first five million numbers, generated by Mathematica$^{\circledR}$.

</details>


### [11] [Quantum modularity in refined topological recursion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23864)
*Veronica Fantini,Kento Osuga*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates Borel summability and quantum modularity of the free energy from refined topological recursion on a Weber-type spectral curve, confirming expected analytic properties (e.g., paired Stokes rays, identification with Barnes double gamma), and—unexpectedly—establishing that Stokes constants generate Jacobi quantum modular functions, suggesting broader applicability of quantum modularity in refined topological recursion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the analytic structure (Borel summability) and arithmetic-geometric properties (quantum modularity) of free energies arising from refined topological recursion, particularly on the Weber-type refined spectral curve, and to explore potential universality of quantum modularity in this framework.

Method: Rigorous analysis of Borel–Laplace resummation of the asymptotic free energy series; explicit computation and classification of Stokes phenomena; derivation and modular transformation properties of generating functions of Stokes constants; comparison with known special functions (Barnes double gamma, Jacobi forms).

Result: 1. Borel summability holds with symmetric Stokes rays; Borel–Laplace sum equals the Barnes double gamma function. 2. Generating functions of Stokes constants along each ray are Jacobi quantum modular forms. 3. Evidence that quantum modularity is intrinsic to refined topological recursion beyond the Weber case.

Conclusion: Quantum modularity is not accidental but likely a structural feature of refined topological recursion; the Weber-type curve serves as a prototypical example where analytic (Borel) and arithmetic (quantum modular) structures coexist and interplay canonically.

Abstract: In this paper we study the Borel summability and quantum modularity of the free energy computed by refined topological recursion on the Weber-type refined spectral curve. On the one hand, we verify expected properties, namely, Stokes rays come in a pair and the Borel-Laplace sum of the free energy coincides with the Barnes double gamma function. Somewhat unexpectedly, on the other hand, we prove that the generating function of the Stokes constants along each ray is a Jacobi quantum modular function. Our proof suggests that quantum modularity may be a general feature of refined topological recursion, applicable beyond the Weber-type refined spectral curve.

</details>


### [12] [Recursive construction of spectral basis and its application to matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24418)
*A. Acus,A. Dargys*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a recursive formula to construct a generalized spectral basis, demonstrated via symbolic computation involving non-diagonalizable matrices in electromagnetic wave propagation in anisotropic media; the method extends to multivectors in Clifford algebra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable symbolic computation with non-diagonalizable matrices—particularly relevant in physical models like electromagnetic wave propagation in anisotropic media—and to generalize spectral methods beyond diagonalizable operators.

Method: Derivation and proof of a recursive formula for constructing a generalized spectral basis; application to symbolic computation with non-diagonalizable matrices and extension to Clifford algebra multivectors.

Result: A working recursive construction of generalized spectral basis is established; successfully applied to electromagnetic wave propagation modeling and shown to be extensible to Clifford algebra multivectors.

Conclusion: The recursive generalized spectral basis construction provides a robust algebraic framework for symbolic manipulation of non-diagonalizable linear operators, with broad applicability across mathematical physics and geometric algebra.

Abstract: We prove a recursive formula for construction of generalized spectral basis. Example of application is presented for symbolic computation with non-diagonalizable matrix that describes electromagnetic wave propagation in anisotropic media. The method is general enough and can be applied, for example, to multivectors of the Clifford algebra.

</details>


### [13] [The Hadamard parametrix on globally hyperbolic spacetimes with Robin boundary conditions: Fundamental solutions and Hadamard states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24465)
*Beatrice Costeri,Claudio Dappiaggi,Benito Alberto Juárez-Aubry*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the Klein-Gordon equation with Robin boundary conditions on globally hyperbolic spacetimes with timelike boundaries, establishing fundamental solutions, characterizing their wavefront sets via propagation of singularities, and constructing Robin-Hadamard two-point correlation functions—extending the Hadamard condition to include boundary reflections—and proving their existence on static and deformed backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend quantum field theory in curved spacetime to manifolds with timelike boundaries by rigorously defining physically admissible (Hadamard) states for scalar fields subject to Robin boundary conditions—a setting relevant for Casimir effects, AdS/CFT, and black hole physics with boundaries.

Method: Microlocal analysis: construction of advanced/retarded fundamental solutions; application of Melrose–Sjöstrand propagation of singularities theorem under infinitesimal convexity and finite boundary reflections; synthesis of a local Robin-Hadamard parametrix incorporating reflected null geodesics and Robin-matching transport equations; equivalence proof between local parametrix and global wavefront set condition (Radzikowski-type theorem); ground-state deformation argument for existence.

Result: Existence/uniqueness of fundamental solutions; full characterization of their wavefront sets in the interior; definition and construction of Robin-Hadamard two-point functions satisfying both local (parametrix-based) and global (wavefront set) criteria; equivalence of these formulations; existence proof for such states on static and adiabatically deformed spacetimes.

Conclusion: The paper provides a rigorous microlocal framework for Hadamard states of scalar fields on globally hyperbolic spacetimes with timelike boundaries and Robin conditions, unifying geometric, analytic, and quantum-field-theoretic perspectives—thus enabling well-defined renormalization and physical interpretation in bounded relativistic settings.

Abstract: On a $d$-dimensional, $d\geq 2$, globally hyperbolic spacetime with a timelike boundary $(\mathcal{M},g)$, we investigate the Klein-Gordon equation with Robin boundary conditions. First of all, we prove existence and uniqueness of the advanced and retarded fundamental solutions, discussing in addition their structural properties. Secondly, under the hypothesis of infinitesimal convexity of $\partial\mathcal{M}$ and of local finiteness of boundary reflections for broken null bicharacteristics, we characterize their wavefront set in the interior $\mathring{\mathcal{M}}$, using the propagation of singularities theorem of Melrose and Sjöstrand.
  In the second part of the paper, we introduce a notion of Robin-Hadamard two-point correlation function as a positive distribution on $\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}$ with prescribed antisymmetric part and singular structure, the latter being characterized in terms of its wavefront set. We complement this definition with a local Hadamard form. Up to a smooth remainder, this consists of the standard directed Hadamard parametrix together with additional contributions associated with the reflected null rays. Their coefficients are determined by transport equations supplemented by Robin matching conditions at the boundary. The construction applies to an arbitrary, but finite number of reflections. We prove that this local formulation is equivalent to the wavefront set condition, thereby establishing a counterpart of Radzikowski's theorem in the presence of a timelike boundary. Finally, starting from the ground state on suitable static backgrounds and using a deformation argument, we prove existence of Robin-Hadamard two-point correlation functions on the class of spacetimes considered.

</details>


### [14] [Payoffs and perception mediate environmental feedback in an N-player trust game with Q-learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24493)
*Ruqiang Guo,Zhaoyi Hu,Fangfang Wang,Linjie Liu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper integrates reinforcement learning with eco-evolutionary dynamics to study how endogenous environmental feedback—mediated by both material payoffs and perceived environmental states—affects trust evolution in a hierarchical two-population trust game; it finds that environmental feedback amplifies learning-driven trust decline at high-return regimes but promotes trust near perception-tier boundaries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how endogenous environmental feedback—arising from collective behavior—modulates trust development via both material incentives (payoff changes) and perceived states, bridging reinforcement learning and eco-evolutionary modeling gaps.

Method: Coupled heterogeneous tabular Q-learning agents in a fixed-role, two-population hierarchical trust game with a centered endogenous environment; conducted controlled comparisons between fixed-payoff baselines and feedback branches initialized from identical learned states; varied payoff multiplication factor and observed environmental tier.

Result: Increasing payoff multiplication raised trust in fixed-payoff games (driven by risk-seeking investors); environmental feedback promoted trust near perception-tier boundaries but reinforced trust decline at high-return operating points; payoff feedback intensified the learning-driven trust decline, exacerbated by stronger drive and longer horizons.

Conclusion: Endogenous environmental feedback embeds past collective outcomes into future incentives and perceptions, qualitatively altering trust dynamics beyond standard RL predictions—shifting from purely incentive-driven adaptation to state-dependent, history-embedded behavioral evolution.

Abstract: Trust develops through learning, while collective behavior can alter the environment in which later decisions are made. Reinforcement-learning models describe adaptation, and eco-evolutionary models describe behavior-environment feedback, but how an endogenous environment changes trust through material incentives and perceived states remains unclear. We couple a fixed-role, two-population hierarchical trust game populated by heterogeneous tabular $Q$-learning agents to a centered endogenous environment. For the main feedback comparisons, we initialize the fixed-payoff baseline and feedback branches from the same learned state to control for prior learning. We then compare responses when payoff parameters or the observed environmental tier vary. As the payoff multiplication factor increased, successful trust in the fixed-payoff game crossed from a low to a higher finite-horizon level, led by risk-seeking investors. Environmental feedback promoted trust near perception-tier boundaries and reinforced its decline at the high-return operating point. At the high-return operating point, payoff feedback reinforced the learning-driven decline in trust. Stronger drive and longer horizons deepened this negative effect. Compared with a fixed-payoff trust game, environmental feedback carries past collective outcomes into future payoffs and observed states.

</details>


### [15] [Proactive Incentive Regulation in Multi-Agent Systems with Environmental Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24506)
*Xinyang Cao,Shijia Hua,Linjie Liu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a multi-agent environmental feedback game to study how the timing of punitive incentives—applied either in resource-abundant or resource-scarce states—affects cooperation dynamics; it finds that early (abundant-state) penalties induce bistability and enable full cooperation, whereas late (scarce-state) penalties cause complex, unstable dynamics that impede cooperation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the insufficient understanding of *when* to implement punitive incentives in environmental feedback systems—specifically, how incentive timing under varying resource conditions affects the emergence and stability of cooperation among rational agents.

Method: Develops a coupled multi-agent environmental feedback game model with state-dependent incentive intensities; conducts theoretical analysis—including bifurcation analysis, stability analysis, and phase-plane methods—to characterize system dynamics under abundant vs. scarce resource regimes.

Result: In resource-abundant states, penalties induce a bistable regime enabling full cooperation; in resource-scarce states, penalties generate interior equilibria, heteroclinic cycles, and Hopf bifurcations—leading to oscillatory or unstable cooperation. Timing critically determines whether incentives stabilize or destabilize cooperation.

Conclusion: Proactive incentive implementation during favorable (abundant) environmental conditions is more effective for sustaining cooperation than reactive enforcement after degradation—highlighting 'timing' as a fundamental design principle for environmental governance mechanisms.

Abstract: In environmental feedback systems, self-interested behaviors of rational agents often undermine cooperation and environmental sustainability. Although punitive incentive mechanisms are widely recognized as effective in addressing such social dilemmas, the timing of their implementation under different environmental conditions remains insufficiently understood. In this paper, we develop a multi-agent environmental feedback game framework with coupled incentive intensities across resource states. We investigate how system dynamics vary with incentive intensity under both resource-abundant and resource-scarce conditions. Theoretical analysis shows that imposing penalties in resource-abundant states can transform the system from a tragedy of the commons into a bistable regime that supports full cooperation. In contrast, imposing penalties in resource-scarce states leads to complex dynamical behaviors, including interior stability, heteroclinic cycles, and Hopf bifurcations, which hinder the emergence of stable cooperation. These results highlight the importance of timing in incentive regulation. Proactive intervention under favorable environmental conditions is more effective in sustaining cooperation than delayed enforcement after environmental degradation.

</details>


### [16] [Batalin-Vilkovisky algebras from Ševera bicomplexes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24781)
*Eugenia Boffo,Domenico Fiorenza*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces Ševera bicomplexes—bicomplexes with differential operators constrained by order—and shows that under suitable conditions, the second-page differential of their spectral sequence yields a Batalin–Vilkovisky (BV) operator; when $E_2$ is rank-1 free over a graded commutative algebra $A$, this induces Gerstenhaber and BV algebra structures on $A$, generalizing Ševera’s BV structure on odd symplectic manifolds, all interpreted via Cartan calculus.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize the construction of Batalin–Vilkovisky algebra structures—particularly Ševera’s geometric example on odd symplectic manifolds—using homological algebra tools (bicomplexes, spectral sequences) and differential operator constraints, while framing them intrinsically via Cartan calculus.

Method: Defines Ševera bicomplexes $(M,d_0,d_1)$ where $d_i$ are Grothendieck differential operators of order $\leq i$ w.r.t. an $A$-module structure on $M$, and $d_0$ admits a contracting homotopy $K$ of matching order; analyzes the associated spectral sequence, identifies $d_2$ as a BV operator on $E_2$, and derives induced Gerstenhaber/BV structures when $E_2 \cong A$ as a free rank-1 module; interprets all structures using Cartan calculus formalism.

Result: Under suitable assumptions (e.g., existence of compatible contracting homotopy, freeness of $E_2$), the $E_2$-page differential $d_2$ is a BV operator; if $E_2$ is a free rank-1 $A$-module, then $A$ acquires canonically a Gerstenhaber algebra structure, enhanced to a BV algebra upon choice of a $d_2$-closed generator; this recovers Ševera’s BV structure on $C^\infty$ of odd symplectic manifolds.

Conclusion: Ševera bicomplexes provide an abstract homological framework for generating BV algebras from constrained bicomplexes, linking spectral sequence geometry, differential operator theory, and Cartan calculus; the approach suggests a pathway toward derived Cartan calculus.

Abstract: A Ševera bicomplex is a bicomplex $(M,d_{0},d_{1})$ whose differentials $d_{i}$ are Grothendieck differential operators of order $\leq i$ with respect to a given $A$-module structure on $M$, where $A$ is a graded commutative algebra. One also requires that $(M,d_{0})$ admits a contracting homotopy $K$ that is a differential operator of the same order as $d_{0}$. Under suitable assumptions, the differential $d_{2}$ of the second page of the spectral sequence associated with $(M,d_{0},d_{1})$ is a Batalin-Vilkovisky operator on $E_{2}$, so that when $E_{2}$ is a free rank 1 $A$-module, the graded commutative algebra structure of $A$ is enhanced to a Gerstenhaber algebra structure, and the choice of a $d_{2}$-closed basis element for $E_{2}$ further enhances this to a Batalin-Vilkovisky algebra structure. The prototypical example of this construction is Ševera's description of the Batalin--Vilkovisky algebra structure on the algebra of smooth functions of an odd symplectic manifold. Throughout the whole article we look at Batalin-Vilkovisky algebras through the lenses of Cartan calculus. Indications of a generalization to derived Cartan calculus are briefly discussed in the concluding section.

</details>


### [17] [Constraints for Physical Gauge Groups Coming from the Causal Action Principle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24888)
*Felix Finster,Niels Gresnigt*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores constraints on local gauge groups in causal fermion systems via the causal action principle, revealing a systematic link between Lie and Clifford algebras through quadratic (bilinear logarithmic) terms in gauge potentials, starting from chiral U(N)×U(N) structures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To derive and understand physical constraints on admissible local gauge groups in the framework of causal fermion systems, grounded in the causal action principle.

Method: Analytical derivation using the causal action principle; systematic algebraic analysis of bilinear logarithmic terms in the Lagrangian; exploration of chiral gauge potentials for U(N)×U(N) and their reduction to physically relevant subgroups.

Result: Quadratic constraints on gauge potentials induce an algebraic correspondence between Lie algebra structures (governing gauge symmetries) and Clifford algebra structures (encoding spacetime and spinor geometry); explicit examples demonstrate how standard model–like groups emerge.

Conclusion: The causal action principle inherently couples gauge symmetry (Lie algebra) and quantum geometry (Clifford algebra), suggesting a deep unification mechanism where gauge groups are not freely chosen but constrained—and partially determined—by the underlying causal fermion structure.

Abstract: The constraints for the effective local gauge groups stemming from the causal action principle for causal fermion systems are reviewed. The constraint which are quadratic in the gauge potentials (coming from the so-called bilinear logarithmic terms) are shown to make a connection between the structures of Lie algebras and Clifford algebras. This connection is worked out systematically starting from general chiral potentials corresponding to the gauge group $\mathrm{U}(N) \times \mathrm{U}(N)$. The general results are illustrated in various examples.

</details>


### [18] [Don't mind the gap: Absolutely Continuous Edge Spectrum in a Mobility Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25000)
*Simon Becker,Mengxuan Yang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a rigorous link between bulk topological invariants and the absolutely continuous edge spectrum for 2D lattice and magnetic Schrödinger Hamiltonians in a mobility gap, proving that a nonzero bulk index forces the entire mobility gap to lie in the absolutely continuous spectrum of the half-space operator, with multiplicity bounded below by the index magnitude.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously connect bulk topological invariants (e.g., Chern number) to the nature of edge spectrum—specifically absolute continuity—in systems where the bulk may be localized (mobility gap), beyond the traditional spectral gap assumption.

Method: Functional analytic methods including spectral theory of self-adjoint operators, localization estimates, boundary condition analysis, and an auxiliary-space cut construction; leverages assumptions of dynamical or Anderson localization in the bulk and suitable boundary regularity.

Result: If the bulk topological index is nonzero and constant over a mobility gap, then that entire interval lies in the absolutely continuous spectrum of the half-space (edge) Hamiltonian; moreover, the absolutely continuous spectral multiplicity is ≥ |index| almost everywhere in the interval.

Conclusion: Topological protection of edge states manifests as robust absolute continuity—not just existence—of the edge spectrum across the full mobility gap, generalizing previous results from spectral gaps to more physically realistic localized bulk regimes.

Abstract: We study the edge spectrum of two-dimensional lattice Hamiltonians and magnetic Schrödinger operators in a mobility gap, an energy interval in which the bulk spectrum is potentially localized rather than necessarily absent. Under suitable localization and boundary assumptions, we prove that a constant nonzero bulk topological index implies that the entire interval belongs to the absolutely continuous spectrum of the corresponding half-space operator. The absolutely continuous spectral multiplicity is bounded below by the absolute value of the index at almost every energy in the interval. The first version of the auxiliary-space cut construction in Section 2.3 was generated using ChatGPT 6.

</details>

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First measurement of W boson transverse helicity fractions in the forward region (0 ≤ y_W ≤ 5) at √s = 13 TeV using LHCb, based on W→μν_μ decays and template fitting of muon p_T and η; results show strong rapidity dependence and agree with NLO SM predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe electroweak dynamics and parton distribution functions (PDFs) in the forward region—where gluon and sea-quark contributions dominate—and provide the first test of W boson transverse helicity fractions outside central rapidity.

Method: Template fit to muon transverse momentum (p_T) and pseudorapidity (η) distributions from W→μν_μ decays in pp collisions at √s = 13 TeV; data correspond to 5.1 fb⁻¹ integrated luminosity collected by LHCb; helicity fractions extracted differentially in W rapidity y_W.

Result: Transverse helicity fractions measured differentially in 0 ≤ y_W ≤ 5; observed strong rapidity dependence; statistical and systematic uncertainties quantified; agreement with next-to-leading-order (NLO) Standard Model predictions confirmed.

Conclusion: This is the first determination of W boson transverse helicity fractions in the forward region; results validate NLO SM calculations and constrain PDFs—particularly sea quarks and gluons—at low-x and high-|y|.

Abstract: The transverse helicity fractions of $W$ bosons are measured as a function of the $W$ boson rapidity, $y_{W}$, in the range $0 \leq y_{W} \leq 5$ using $W\toμν_μ$ decays in $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV recorded by the LHCb experiment and corresponding to an integrated luminosity of $5.1$ fb$^{-1}$. The fractions are extracted from a template fit to the muon transverse momentum and pseudorapidity. The results show a strong rapidity dependence and agree with next-to-leading-order Standard Model predictions, providing the first determination of the transverse helicity fractions of $W$ bosons in the forward region.

</details>


### [20] [Benchmarking Nucleon Production in Hadron Interactions Relevant for GeV-scale Neutrino Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22662)
*Richard Diurba,Steve Dytman,Matthew King,Yinrui Liu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how hadron-nucleus scattering models in neutrino event generators (e.g., GENIE with Bertini Cascade and INCL++) predict nucleon production from common final-state hadrons (nucleons, pions), finds simple parametric forms (Gaussian/exponential) for nucleon multiplicity in mesonless interactions, and provides reweighting tools to mitigate model uncertainties in visible energy and nucleon counts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neutrino experiments depend on accurate modeling of intranuclear hadronic cascades to reconstruct neutrino energy and identify interaction channels; discrepancies among hadron-nucleus scattering models introduce significant systematic uncertainties in final-state observables.

Method: Systematic comparison of nucleon production predictions from GENIE’s embedded hadronic models (Geant4 Bertini Cascade and INCL++) for nucleon and pion scattering on common target nuclei; empirical fitting of nucleon multiplicity distributions for mesonless interactions; development of reweighting tools to quantify and correct model-induced variations in visible energy and nucleon multiplicities.

Result: Nucleon multiplicities from mesonless hadron–nucleus interactions follow simple Gaussian (for low-multiplicity regimes) and exponential decay (for high-multiplicity tails) functional forms across studied nuclei; quantified spread between Bertini and INCL++ predictions; validated reweighting tools that adjust simulated events to match alternative model outputs in both visible energy and nucleon count distributions.

Conclusion: Parametric descriptions of nucleon production enable efficient uncertainty quantification and mitigation in neutrino oscillation and cross-section analyses; the provided reweighting framework improves robustness against hadronic model dependence without requiring full recomputation of event samples.

Abstract: In neutrino scattering experiments, the emitted final state particles are essential for identifying interaction channels and reconstructing neutrino energies. During intranuclear propagation, neutrino-induced hadrons can interact with surrounding nucleons, altering both the number of final state particles and detectable energy depositions. As a result, neutrino experiments rely on hadron-nucleus scattering models to simulate these nuclear effects. This paper explores nucleon production predicted by hadron scattering models for common hadrons in neutrino experiments, specifically nucleons and pions. We investigate these models with simulations from the \textsc{GENIE} neutrino event generator, which includes implementations of \textsc{Geant4} Bertini Cascade and \textsc{INCL++}. For popular target nuclei, we find that the number of emitted nucleons for mesonless hadron interactions can be parameterized by simple Gaussian and exponential decay functions. We present these relationships and provide reweighting tools for addressing the hadron scattering model spread in both visible energy and final state nucleon multiplicities.

</details>


### [21] [Mixture Density Networks for Neutrino Reconstruction at Hadron Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22979)
*Seungjin Yang,Jason S. H. Lee,Junghwan Goh*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: MONSTER is a mixture density network using a Transformer-based event encoder to reconstruct neutrino momentum in semileptonic tt̄ events at hadron colliders, achieving improved accuracy (5–6% lower momentum residual percentiles), reduced RMSE (2–9%), lower bias, and 2–8.5× faster inference versus νflows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neutrino longitudinal momentum is unobserved at hadron colliders, causing intrinsic ambiguity in momentum reconstruction—especially critical in semileptonic tt̄ events for precision physics and new physics searches.

Method: MONSTER employs a mixture density network (MDN) that models the conditional distribution of neutrino three-momentum as a multivariate normal mixture, parameterized by a Transformer-based encoder over reconstructed event objects; it provides a closed-form, sampling-free point estimate via mode extraction from the predicted density.

Result: On the νflows public benchmark, MONSTER reduces the 68th percentile of the three-momentum residual by 5% and the 95th by 6%; per-component RMSE decreases by 2–9% at comparable bias; inference is 2–8.5× faster than νflows depending on hardware and batch size.

Conclusion: Mixture density networks—exemplified by MONSTER—are a competitive, efficient, and accurate alternative to normalizing flows for neutrino momentum reconstruction in collider environments.

Abstract: Neutrino momentum reconstruction at hadron colliders is intrinsically ambiguous because the longitudinal momentum is not directly observed. We study this problem in semileptonic $t\bar{t}$ events using \monster{} (Mixture of Neutrino Solutions with Transformer Event Representation), a mixture density network that predicts a multivariate normal mixture for the conditional distribution of neutrino momentum from reconstructed event objects. The model uses a Transformer-based event encoder and yields a sampling-free point estimate of the neutrino momentum from the closed-form density. On the public benchmark introduced with \nuflows, \monster{} reduces the 68th percentile of the three-momentum residual by 5\% and the 95th percentile by 6\% relative to the empirical-mode \nuflows{} baseline, with per-variable root-mean-square errors smaller by 2 to 9\% at comparable bias, while running 2 to 8.5 times faster at inference, depending on the device and batch size. These results indicate that mixture density networks are a competitive alternative to normalizing flows for neutrino reconstruction.

</details>


### [22] [Detailed investigation on the Geant4 simulation of alpha particles in liquid scintillator detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23793)
*Shiwen Xu,Shubing Liu,Zeyuan Yu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper validates Geant4 simulations of alpha-particle energy deposition in LAB-based liquid scintillator for calibration of large detectors, identifying delta-electron fluctuations as the dominant source of intrinsic resolution and quantifying dependencies on step size, production threshold, Birks constant, and electromagnetic physics models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Alpha particles from ^214Po, ^212Po, and ^210Po are increasingly used to calibrate large liquid scintillator detectors (e.g., SNO+), but Geant4 simulations of their energy deposition lack detailed validation—particularly concerning quenching and low-energy electron transport.

Method: The authors simulate 5.3 MeV alpha particles in a 4 m cube of linear alkylbenzene (LAB)-based liquid scintillator using Geant4 11.4.2 with Birks quenching applied per step. They systematically vary step size, production threshold, Birks constant (k_B), and electromagnetic physics models (Standard, Penelope, Livermore). Geant4-DNA is employed to resolve keV-scale electron track structure, including tortuosity and curvature.

Result: Delta-electron-associated fluctuations dominate simulated response variance: secondary-track quenched-response contributes 101–112% of total variance, with negative primary–secondary covariance reducing overall variance. Simulated quenching curves agree with SNO+ data for k_B = 0.007–0.010 g cm⁻² MeV⁻¹ (χ²/ndf ≈ 0.14–0.17). Geant4-DNA reveals strong electron-track curvature, with tortuosity decreasing from ~6 at 200 eV to ~1.6 at 10 keV. Production threshold impacts L/E more strongly than EM model choice: Penelope and Livermore agree within ~3%, while Standard yields higher L/E—especially at low thresholds.

Conclusion: Delta-electron fluctuations constitute the dominant contribution to intrinsic energy resolution in alpha-calibration simulations under tested conditions. Production threshold is the most sensitive simulation parameter; electromagnetic model choice (Penelope/Livermore vs. Standard) has secondary impact. Geant4-DNA reveals non-straight electron tracks at sub-keV energies, implying limitations of continuous-slowing-down approximation in scintillation modeling.

Abstract: Purpose: Alpha particles from $^{214}$Po, $^{212}$Po, and $^{210}$Po are increasingly used to calibrate large liquid scintillator detectors, but Geant4 simulations of their energy deposition require detailed validation.
  Methods: We simulate alpha particles in a 4 m cube of LAB-based liquid scintillator with Geant4 11.4.2 and apply Birks quenching per step. We study step-size, production-threshold, Birks-constant, and electromagnetic-model dependence, and use Geant4-DNA to examine keV-scale electron track structure.
  Results: Delta-electron-associated fluctuations dominate the simulated response. Across the tested explicit-delta settings, the variance of the quenched-response contribution from secondary tracks is 1.01--1.12 times the total variance; a negative primary--secondary covariance reduces the total. The simulated quenching-curve shape is compatible with SNO+ data within current uncertainties, with $χ^2/\mathrm{ndf}\simeq0.14$--$0.17$ for $k_B=0.007$--$0.010$ g cm$^{-2}$ MeV$^{-1}$. Geant4-DNA gives strong electron-track curvature, with tortuosity decreasing from about 6 at 200 eV to 1.6 at 10 keV.
  Conclusion: Delta-electron fluctuations are the dominant contribution to the intrinsic resolution in the tested configurations. The production threshold has a larger effect than the electromagnetic model: Penelope and Livermore agree within about 3% in L/E, whereas Standard gives higher L/E, especially at low thresholds.

</details>


### [23] [Jevons' Paradox and Fast Generative Simulation for HEP: Why Realistic Benchmarking is Essential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24306)
*Thorsten Buss,Henry Day-Hall,Frank Gaede,Gregor Kasieczka,Katja Krüger,Anatolii Korol,Thomas Madlener,Peter McKeown,Martina Mozzanica,Lorenzo Valente*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper discusses how fast generative models can drastically improve calorimeter simulation efficiency in high-energy physics (HEP), while cautioning against Jevons’ paradox and emphasizing realistic efficiency metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Calorimeter simulation is computationally expensive and energy-intensive in HEP; future detectors with finer granularity will exacerbate this bottleneck, demanding scalable, efficient simulation methods.

Method: Analysis and conceptual discussion of fast generative models for calorimeter simulation, with critical evaluation of efficiency definitions and implications of Jevons’ paradox.

Result: Fast generative models enable ~100× speedup in calorimeter simulation, but true efficiency gains require careful metric design to avoid rebound effects (e.g., increased simulation volume offsetting energy savings).

Conclusion: Adopting fast generative models is essential for future HEP analyses, but 'true' efficiency must account for systemic impacts—especially energy use and statistical sufficiency—not just wall-clock speed.

Abstract: Simulation is a major computational expense in HEP, and calorimeter simulation in particular drives the overall energy cost of our physics analyses. Future detectors will contain more finely grained calorimeters than ever, and their data analyses will demand unprecedented simulated statistics. Fast generative models redefine what is possible, producing simulations 100 times more efficiently. This article addresses Jevons' paradox in our field and considers the importance of realistic metrics in achieving "true" efficiency.

</details>


### [24] [The detector system of the SHiP/NA67 experiment at CERN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24378)
*Matei Climescu,on behalf of the SHiP Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: SHiP/NA67 is a CERN experiment targeting GeV-scale feebly interacting particles and all-flavour neutrino physics using the HI-ECN3 beam; its initial detector configuration—featuring veto systems, precision timing/trackers/calorimeters—is being optimized for commissioning and first physics runs in 2032–2033.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To discover feebly interacting, GeV-scale new particles beyond the Standard Model and to conduct comprehensive, high-precision all-flavour neutrino interaction measurements.

Method: Design and optimization of a multi-subsystem detector at the HI-ECN3 beamline, including high-timing-resolution veto detectors, a lightweight straw tracker, high-spatial-precision calorimeters for new-particle searches, and high-granularity, compact calorimeters for neutrino reconstruction.

Result: Ongoing optimization of the initial detector configuration for commissioning and first physics runs (2032–2033); no experimental results reported yet.

Conclusion: The SHiP/NA67 experiment represents a major effort in intensity-frontier particle physics, uniquely combining sensitivity to hidden-sector particles and high-statistics, flavor-resolved neutrino scattering studies via a purpose-built, hybrid-detector design.

Abstract: The SHiP/NA67 experiment aims to search for feebly interacting GeV-scale new particles and to perform all-flavour neutrino-physics measurements at the HI-ECN3 beam facility at the CERN SPS. The collaboration is currently optimising the experiment's initial configuration for the commissioning and first physics runs of 2032-2033. The detector subsystems comprise a few large-area instruments: for new-particle searches, highly sophisticated veto detectors, high timing-resolution detectors, a lightweight straw tracker system, and high-spatial-precision calorimeters; and, for neutrino reconstruction, small transverse-size, high-granularity calorimeters.

</details>


### [25] [mkFit for track fitting with the CMS Phase-2 detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24659)
*Leonardo Giannini,Emmanouil Vourliotis*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: mkFit, a parallelized Kalman filter-based track reconstruction algorithm adopted by CMS for LHC Run 3, achieves 3.5× speedup in track building; this work extends mkFit to track fitting for the CMS High-Level Trigger, showing promising physics and computational performance in Phase-2 simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Track fitting has become a bottleneck after mkFit accelerated track building; further speedup is needed for HL-LHC conditions, motivating extension of mkFit to track fitting.

Method: Extension of the mkFit framework—originally designed for track building—to perform track fitting within the CMS High-Level Trigger, evaluated using realistic Phase-2 simulation data.

Result: Preliminary results demonstrate feasible integration of mkFit into track fitting with competitive physics performance and measurable computational speedup in HLT context.

Conclusion: Extending mkFit to track fitting is viable and beneficial for CMS tracking at HL-LHC, enabling balanced acceleration across the full tracking pipeline.

Abstract: The mkFit algorithm provides an implementation of the Kalman filter-based track reconstruction algorithm that exploits both thread- and data-level parallelism. It has been adopted by the CMS Collaboration as the primary track building algorithm during the Run 3 of the LHC for both the online and offline track reconstruction sequences. Thanks to mkFit, an average speedup of a factor 3.5 is achieved in track building while retaining or improving physics performance. As a consequence of the speedup provided by mkFit in track building, track fitting has become a comparably significant component of the overall tracking time. Given the increased demands of the High-Luminosity Large Hadron Collider, further speedup can be achieved by extending mkFit to the track fitting task. We present preliminary results for track fitting in the CMS High-Level Trigger using mkFit, covering both the physics and the computational performance, based on realistic Phase-2 simulations.

</details>


### [26] [Goodness-of-fit for multi-distribution neutrino cross-section measurements with shared events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24796)
*Francisco Martinez Lopez,London Cooper-Troendle,Steven Gardiner,Patrick Green,Tanaz Mohayai*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Introduces range-projected χ² to correctly handle rank-deficient covariance matrices arising from event-sharing across differential cross-section measurements, ensuring physically meaningful goodness-of-fit tests with proper degrees of freedom.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard χ² tests become unphysical when applied to correlated differential measurements from shared event samples, due to rank-deficient covariance matrices caused by exact linear constraints among bin counts.

Method: Proposes the range-projected χ² statistic, which projects residuals onto the column space (range) of the covariance matrix, effectively restricting the fit to the subspace of independent statistical information; identifies and separates structural (geometry-based) and kinematic (phase-space occupancy–dependent) contributions to the nullity N_null.

Result: The range-projected χ² yields a well-defined test statistic with N_bins − N_null degrees of freedom; validated analytically (toy model) and numerically (simulated neutrino–argon cross-section measurement with unfolding and multi-source systematics).

Conclusion: Event-sharing induces predictable rank deficiency in covariance matrices; the range-projected χ² resolves associated χ² inflation and restores physical interpretability in global fits of multi-differential measurements.

Abstract: When multiple differential cross-section measurements are extracted from a common event sample, the same events contribute simultaneously to multiple distributions. This event-sharing structure imposes exact linear constraints among the bin counts, reducing the effective dimensionality of the measurement below the total number of bins. Since the covariance matrix obeys these inter-distribution constraints, it contains a rank deficiency. While limited numerical precision may inadvertently restore invertibility, the $χ^{2}$ contributions along constrained dimensions will be arbitrary, yielding unphysically inflated $χ^{2}$ values in global goodness-of-fit tests. We present the range-projected $χ^{2}$, a test statistic that restricts the goodness-of-fit test to the subspace carrying independent statistical information, yielding a $χ^{2}$ with $N_\text{bins} - N_\text{null}$ degrees of freedom, where $N_\text{null}$ is the number of independent constraints. We show that this rank deficiency is a predictable consequence of the event-sharing structure, and that $N_\text{null}$ decomposes into a structural contribution determined a priori from the binning geometry and a kinematic contribution that depends on the phase-space occupancy. The method is validated with an analytical toy model and a simulated neutrino--argon cross-section measurement including unfolding and multi-source systematic uncertainties.

</details>


### [27] [Search for a top-philic heavy resonance in association with top quarks in $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s} = $ 13 TeV and 13.6 TeV with the ATLAS detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24950)
*ATLAS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a search for a top-philic Z' boson produced with top quarks and decaying to tt̄, using 140 fb⁻¹ (13 TeV) and 56 fb⁻¹ (13.6 TeV) ATLAS data; it employs a mass-parameterised transformer classifier on multi-lepton final states (≥3ℓ or 2 same-sign ℓ), finds no significant excess, and sets a 95% CL exclusion limit of m_Z' < 1150 GeV (expected 1250 GeV) for ct = 1, θ = 0 in a simplified colour-singlet model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe new physics beyond the Standard Model via heavy resonances preferentially coupling to top quarks — motivated by anomalies, hierarchy problem, and flavour structure — specifically testing top-philic Z′ bosons that could enhance tt̄ production or modify top-quark interactions.

Method: Analysis of pp collision data from ATLAS at √s = 13 TeV (140 fb⁻¹) and 13.6 TeV (56 fb⁻¹); event selection targets multi-lepton signatures (≥3 leptons or exactly 2 same-sign leptons) sensitive to Z′ → tt̄; background estimation uses data-driven and simulation-based methods; signal discrimination enhanced via a transformer-based neural network whose architecture is explicitly parameterised by the hypothesised Z′ mass (m_Z′).

Result: No statistically significant deviation from Standard Model expectations observed; upper limits on the production cross section × branching ratio are derived; in a simplified model with colour-singlet Z′, chiral coupling ct = 1, and left-handed coupling (θ = 0), observed (expected) 95% CL exclusion reaches m_Z′ < 1150 GeV (1250 GeV).

Conclusion: The absence of excess constrains top-philic Z′ models, disfavouring low-mass (≤1150 GeV) colour-singlet vector bosons with unit-strength left-handed top couplings; the transformer-based mass-parameterised classifier demonstrates effective signal-background separation in complex multi-lepton tt̄ final states, validating its use for future resonance searches.

Abstract: A search for a new top-philic vector boson ($Z'$) produced in association with a top-quark or a pair of top quarks, and decaying into a top quark pair is presented. The search uses $pp$ collision data collected by the ATLAS detector at the Large Hadron Collider at centre-of-mass energies of $\sqrt{s} =$ 13 TeV and 13.6 TeV, corresponding to integrated luminosities of 140 fb$^{-1}$ and 56 fb$^{-1}$, respectively. Events containing exactly two same-sign leptons or at least three leptons (electrons or muons) are selected. A transformer-based classifier parameterised as a function of the $Z'$ mass is used to optimise the signal-to-background discrimination. The results are interpreted in a simplified model with a colour-singlet vector boson coupled to top quarks with coupling strength $c_t$ and chirality angle $θ$. No significant excess is observed above the expected Standard Model background. A $Z'$ boson with mass below 1150 GeV is excluded at 95% confidence level for a benchmark scenario with $c_t = 1$ and $θ= 0$, with an expected limit of 1250 GeV.

</details>
