### [28] [Theory of Gravity as Theory of Local Inertial Frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22354)
*Bartłomiej Bąk*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This dissertation establishes the affine theory of the full Riemann tensor as an extended theory of gravity, where a symmetric spacetime connection—not the metric—is fundamental; gravity emerges as a local version of Newton’s First Law. A variational formulation is developed, and a general transition from the affine to the metric picture is achieved, revealing non-metricity as a matter field—reminiscent of Weyl’s geometric unification of electromagnetism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend gravity theory beyond the metric framework by elevating the symmetric affine connection to the fundamental dynamical object, thereby geometrizing inertia and potentially unifying gravity with matter fields via non-metricity.

Method: Affine differential geometry with a symmetric connection as the primary variable; variational principle applied to Lagrangians depending on the full Riemann tensor; systematic derivation of the affine-to-metric transition, including analysis of non-metricity as a physical field.

Result: A fully general, previously unachieved transition from the affine theory (with full Riemann tensor) to the standard metric theory; demonstration that non-metricity maps to a physical matter field in the metric picture—realizing Weyl’s electromagnetic interpretation in broader context; validation through explicit examples.

Conclusion: The affine theory of the full Riemann tensor constitutes a consistent, extended gravitational framework where inertia and gravity are unified geometrically; the metric description emerges dynamically, with non-metricity encoding physical degrees of freedom akin to matter or gauge fields.

Abstract: It is proved that the affine theory of the full Riemann tensor constitutes the extended theory of gravity. The fundamental object here is a spacetime symmetric connection. Its physical interpretation is that of a field of local inertial frames. In this approach, gravity arises, in a natural way, as a local version of Newton's First Law. A variational formulation of the theory is presented, based on the results introduced in the article co-authored by the present author and one of the supervisors. The general framework is supported by several examples. A central result of the dissertation is the transition from the affine picture to the metric picture. A simplified version of this procedure -- valid for a specific class of Lagrangians. It was proved there that the non-metricity in the affine picture can be interpreted as a matter field in the metric picture. This is precisely Hermann Weyl's interpretation of electromagnetism. Here, the transformation from the full Riemann tensor theory to the conventional metric theory is examined in full generality for the first time and illustrated using the previously introduced examples.

</details>


### [29] [Sourced Gravitational Radiation in Parity-Violating Tetrad Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22396)
*G. G. L. Nashed*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes linear gravitational modes, energy balance, and source response in a four-parameter tetrad-based gravity theory with parity-even and parity-odd torsion couplings; it identifies eight physical degrees of freedom (2 tensor, 2 scalar, 4 vector), finds light-speed propagation for tensor/scalar modes but coupling-dependent speeds for vectors, reveals indefinite vector Hamiltonian despite real speeds and positive kinetic eigenvalues, derives explicit source-to-amplitude mappings including spin-multipoles via $V_μ = ∂^νB_{νμ}$, and proves equivalence of quadratic Noether and Møller currents—though vector energy flux remains formal due to Hamiltonian unboundedness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize the linearized dynamics, degrees of freedom, stability, and radiation properties of a generalized tetrad theory of gravity that includes both parity-even torsion invariants and a parity-odd $v^μa_μ$ coupling—addressing gaps in understanding vector-mode behavior, energy positivity, and source coupling in teleparallel-like frameworks.

Method: Linearization around Minkowski spacetime; scalar–vector–tensor (SVT) decomposition of perturbations; analysis of kinetic matrix eigenvalues, characteristic speeds, and flux normalizations; derivation of Green’s function response to compact canonical sources; use of $V_μ = ∂^νB_{νμ}$ to isolate intrinsic-spin multipoles; proof of quadratic Noether–Møller current equivalence for first-derivative tetrad Lagrangian; examination of trace identities for Hamiltonian definiteness.

Result: Eight linear degrees of freedom: two massless tensor modes, two massless scalar modes, and four vector modes with coupling-dependent propagation speeds; tensor and scalar modes propagate at c; vector modes exhibit kinetic degeneracy and splitting under parameter variation; vector Hamiltonian is generically indefinite (not positive definite) despite real speeds and positive kinetic eigenvalues; explicit amplitude relations derived for all eight polarizations; $V_μ$ encodes intrinsic-spin multipole structure; quadratic Noether current equals second-order Møller current; vector energy/momentum fluxes remain formal due to unbounded Hamiltonian.

Conclusion: The generic parity-violating branch of this tetrad theory hosts physically propagating but energetically unstable vector modes, undermining full viability as a fundamental theory unless constrained by additional principles or boundary conditions; while tensor and scalar sectors are healthy, the indefinite vector Hamiltonian implies potential ghosts or gradient instabilities, necessitating careful parameter tuning or UV completion.

Abstract: We study linear gravitational modes, sourced far-zone fields, and quadratic energy balance in a four-parameter tetrad theory of gravity. The theory contains three parity-even torsion invariants and a parity-odd coupling $v^μa_μ$, where $v_μ$ and $a_μ$ are the vector and axial torsion components. Linearizing around Minkowski spacetime and applying a scalar--vector--tensor decomposition, we find two tensor, two scalar, and four vector degrees of freedom in the generic nondegenerate parity-violating region. The tensor and scalar modes propagate at the speed of light, while the two vector branches have coupling-dependent propagation speeds. We derive their kinetic eigenvalues, characteristic speeds, and flux normalizations. An exact trace identity shows that the vector Hamiltonian is not positive definite in the generic branch, even when the kinetic eigenvalues are positive and the propagation speeds are real. We also derive the response to a general compact canonical source and obtain explicit source-to-amplitude relations for all eight linear polarizations. The field $V_μ=\partial^νB_{νμ}$ is used to illustrate intrinsic-spin multipoles. {We further prove that the quadratic Noether current equals the second-order Møller current for the chosen first-derivative tetrad Lagrangian and derive the corresponding mode-resolved energy and momentum fluxes.} The vector contribution remains a formal signed response because of the unbounded Hamiltonian. The stationary limit reproduces the results of Ref.~\cite{ShirafujiNashed1997}, and a representative parameter scan illustrates vector-mode splitting and kinetic degeneracy.

</details>


### [30] [Charged particles and fields in bumblebee gravity: Periodic orbits and superradiance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22482)
*Marco A. A. de Paula,Renan B. Magalhães,V. B. Bezerra,A. A. Araújo Filho*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies charged particle dynamics and scalar wave absorption in a charged black hole solution of bumblebee gravity—a Lorentz-violating extension of Einstein-Maxwell theory—focusing on the impact of the Lorentz-violating parameter $l$. It finds that $l$ increases the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO), modifies the total absorption cross section via asymptotic momentum correction, yields three distinct absorption regimes (unbounded/bounded absorption, bounded superradiance), suppresses superradiance for $l > 0$, enhances it for $l < 0$, and reduces total absorption due to a geometric $1/(1+l)$ factor despite lowering the potential barrier.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how Lorentz violation—encoded in the bumblebee gravity parameter $l$—affects fundamental black hole phenomena: geodesic motion of charged particles and scalar wave scattering/absorption, particularly deviations from Reissner-Nordström predictions.

Method: Analytical derivation and geometric analysis of the charged bumblebee black hole solution; geodesic analysis to obtain circular and bound orbits; numerical computation of the total absorption cross section for scalar waves using modified asymptotic flux and momentum due to non-asymptotically-flat spacetime; analytical approximations in low- and high-frequency limits; identification and classification of superradiant regimes via the condition $\omega < \omega_c$.

Result: ISCO radius increases with $l$; total absorption cross section is modified by $1/(1+l)$ and asymptotic momentum rescaling; low-frequency cross section exhibits a transition velocity generalizing the RN case; three absorption regimes identified; superradiance occurs for $\omega < \omega_c$, is suppressed for $l > 0$, enhanced for $l < 0$; increasing $l$ lowers the effective potential barrier but reduces absorption cross section overall.

Conclusion: The Lorentz-violating parameter $l$ in bumblebee gravity qualitatively and quantitatively alters black hole physics: stabilizing orbits at larger radii, reshaping wave absorption phenomenology, and modulating superradiance strength—demonstrating that Lorentz violation leaves distinctive imprints on both particle and field dynamics around charged black holes.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamics of charged particles and fields in the background of a charged black hole in bumblebee gravity, aiming to better understand the role played by the Lorentz-violating parameter $l$. We first review the derivation of the charged bumblebee black hole and investigate its main physical and geometrical properties. We then obtain the circular and bound trajectories of charged particles and find that the innermost stable circular orbit increases with $l$. By using a numerical approach, we compute the total absorption cross section for arbitrary values of the scalar wave frequency $ω$. Since the spacetime is not asymptotically flat, we construct the total absorption cross section by taking into account the modified asymptotic momentum and flux. Analytical approximations are derived in the low- and high-frequency regimes. The low-frequency expression displays a transition velocity that separates two distinct asymptotic branches and generalizes the corresponding Reissner-Nordström result. Our numerical results for the cross section agree with the analytical approximations in their corresponding limits, and the absorption parameter space can be divided into three regions: Unbounded absorption, bounded absorption, and bounded superradiance. In particular, superradiance occurs whenever $ω<ω_{c}$, where $ω_{c}$ is the threshold for superradiance. Moreover, the superradiance amplification decreases as we increase $l$, and charged bumblebee black holes with $l < 0$ lead to greater superradiant scattering than in the Reissner-Nordström case. We also note that although increasing $l$ lowers the potential barrier, it reduces the total absorption cross section because of the geometrical factor $1/(1+l)$ present in the cross section formula.

</details>


### [31] [Flexible numerical characteristic gluing to extremal Reissner-Nordström black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22518)
*Junwoo Lee*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper numerically investigates characteristic gluing for gravitational collapse to extremal Reissner-Nordström black holes, improving lower bounds on achievable black hole size for massless scalar fields and showing sharpness of the m/q < 1 threshold for massive scalar fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the validity and limits of the third law of black hole mechanics via characteristic gluing constructions, particularly regarding extremal black hole formation from charged scalar field collapse.

Method: Numerical implementation of the characteristic gluing method applied to spherically symmetric gravitational collapse of charged (massless and massive) scalar fields in spherical symmetry.

Result: 1) Significantly improved lower bound on final black hole size for massless scalar field collapse; 2) Strong numerical evidence that the m/q = 1 threshold for impossibility of extremal black hole formation with massive scalar fields is sharp.

Conclusion: The third law of black hole mechanics can be violated via characteristic gluing, and the known obstruction for massive scalar fields (m/q ≥ 1) is tight—gluing succeeds arbitrarily close to m/q = 1.

Abstract: Recent work has used the method of characteristic gluing to construct spacetimes violating the third law of black hole mechanics. This paper uses numerical methods to investigate two aspects of the gluing construction of solutions describing the gravitational collapse of a charged scalar field to form an extremal Reissner-Nordström black hole. First, for a massless scalar field, we improve significantly the lower bounds on the size of the final black hole for which gluing is possible. Second, for a massive scalar field, it is known that the formation of an extremal Reissner--Nordström black hole is impossible if the mass-to-charge ratio of the scalar field exceeds unity. We present strong evidence that this bound is sharp by constructing gluing solutions with a scalar field mass-to-charge ratio just below unity.

</details>


### [32] [Black Holes as Frequency-Dependent Filters of Stochastic Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22644)
*Sefi Katznelson,Aidan Minger,Stefano Profumo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper quantifies how black holes filter gravitational-wave backgrounds, showing that Schwarzschild holes induce only negligible spectral distortion via horizon absorption, while Kerr holes allow weak superradiant amplification—yet observable effects require rare, aligned, rapidly spinning, or transiently illuminated systems—not cosmological accumulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the distinction between black hole ringdown (transient, QNM-dominated) and stationary gravitational-wave background scattering, and to assess whether cosmological black hole populations imprint detectable spectral features on stochastic gravitational-wave backgrounds.

Method: Treats black holes as frequency-, angle-, and polarization-dependent linear filters; computes exact transfer functions for Schwarzschild (absorption + phase delay) and Kerr (including superradiance); derives angular/polarization transport kernels for cosmological populations; evaluates optical depth and signal dilution from isotropy and spin randomness.

Result: For Schwarzschild holes, horizon absorption is the sole monopole-distorting effect—and yields negligible optical depth even for asteroid-mass PBH dark matter; for Kerr holes, superradiant amplification exists but is strongly diluted by isotropic incidence and random spin orientations, rendering cumulative cosmological effects undetectable.

Conclusion: Stationary gravitational-wave backgrounds do not produce quasi-normal-mode lines; measurable black hole filtering signatures require exceptional local conditions—nearby, aligned, rapidly rotating, or transiently illuminated black holes—not ensemble-averaged cosmological propagation.

Abstract: Black holes ring when perturbed, whereas their response to a stationary gravitational-wave background is a real-frequency scattering problem, not a source of additional quasi-normal-mode lines. We make this standard distinction quantitative by treating a black hole as a frequency-, angle-, and polarization-dependent filter. For an isotropic stationary background around Schwarzschild holes, elastic scattering produces no net monopole signal, so horizon absorption is the only population-level spectral distortion. We calculate this transfer function for Schwarzschild holes in detail, identify its absorptive and phase-delay signatures, and connect the stationary response to the causal ringdown excited by a finite wave packet. We then promote the single-hole result to an angular and polarization transport kernel for a cosmological population. The resulting optical depth is negligible for realistic black-hole populations, including asteroid-mass primordial black holes comprising all dark matter. We then extend the analysis to Kerr holes, for which superradiance allows genuine amplification in selected co-rotating modes, but isotropic incidence and random spin orientations strongly dilute the diffuse signal. Observable effects are therefore more likely in rare, nearby, aligned, rapidly spinning, or transiently illuminated systems than through cumulative cosmological propagation.

</details>


### [33] [An exact Kerr-like rotating Morris--Thorne wormhole: throat, ergoregion and equatorial shadow slice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22823)
*Mark Sukaiti,Davide Batic,Denys Dutykh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs an exact, rotating, Kerr-like extension of the zero-redshift Morris–Thorne wormhole within a Teo-type stationary, axisymmetric metric ansatz; it identifies a family of solutions parameterized by throat radius $r_0$, oblateness length $a$, and ADM angular momentum $J_{\rm ADM}$, with independent $a$ and $J_{\rm ADM}$ (no $J = a M$ relation due to vanishing ADM mass); it establishes a regular, timelike throat at $r_{\rm th} = \sqrt{r_0^2 - a^2}$ for $|a| < r_0$, confirms absence of CTCs, defines the throat quasi-locally via zero mean curvature, and computes ergoregion onset and equatorial capture dynamics using optical approximation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct an exact, asymptotically flat, rotating wormhole solution that generalizes the static zero-redshift Morris--Thorne wormhole while preserving traversability and avoiding pathologies (e.g., horizons, CTCs), and to clarify the geometric and physical roles of rotation parameters—particularly the independence of oblateness length $a$ and ADM angular momentum $J_{\\rm ADM}$ in the zero-mass limit.

Method: Uses a Teo-type stationary, axisymmetric line element ansatz; solves Einstein equations exactly for an anisotropic comoving stress-energy source; fixes $\\mathcal{K}(r) = r^2 + a^2$ and reduces frame-dragging to a radial quadrature solvable via incomplete elliptic integrals; normalizes angular momentum via ADM formalism; defines the throat quasi-locally as the unique closed surface $S_0$ with zero mean curvature and positive area second variation; employs signed throat-adapted coordinates to verify global structure and causal regularity; analyzes ergoregion and equatorial null geodesics via optical (high-frequency) approximation due to nonseparability of full Hamilton--Jacobi equation.

Result: Derives an exact 3-parameter family $(r_0, a, J_{\\rm ADM})$ of rotating wormholes; finds throat radius $r_{\\rm th} = \\sqrt{r_0^2 - a^2}$ with reality bound $|a| < r_0$; proves throat is timelike, regular, and free of CTCs; shows asymptotic flatness and zero ADM mass imply $a$ and $J_{\\rm ADM}$ are independent (no $J = aM$ constraint); identifies ergoregion onset and computes equatorial capture interval using optical limit.

Conclusion: The constructed spacetime is a physically viable, exact, rotating traversable wormhole: it is asymptotically flat, horizonless, causally well-behaved, and supports a regular timelike throat defined quasi-locally; the parameters $a$ and $J_{\\rm ADM}$ encode distinct geometric and gravitational effects and remain independent in the zero-mass limit; the supporting stress tensor is effective and phenomenological—no claim is made about underlying matter physics.

Abstract: We construct an exact Kerr-like rotating extension of the zero-redshift Morris--Thorne wormhole in a Teo-type stationary, axisymmetric ansatz. The Einstein equations for an anisotropic source comoving with zero angular momentum observers fix $\mathcal{K}(r)=r^2+a^2$ and reduce frame dragging to a radial quadrature. For $b(r)=r_0^2/r$, this is evaluated using incomplete elliptic integrals and normalised by the ADM angular momentum. The spacetime is asymptotically flat with zero ADM mass, so $a=J_{\rm ADM}/M_{\rm ADM}$ does not apply. The oblateness length $a$ and angular momentum $J_{\rm ADM}$ remain independent, yielding a family $(r_0,a,J_{\rm ADM})$. The choice $J_{\rm ADM}=ar_0$ selects only a one-parameter slice for numerical ergoregion and equatorial-capture illustrations; it is not a field-equation constraint. We find $r_{\rm th}=\sqrt{r_0^2-a^2}$, with reality bound $|a|\leq r_0$ and regular range $0\leq |a|<r_0$. In the canonical stationary foliation, the throat is defined quasi-locally as the unique closed surface $S_0$ in the family $S_\ell$ with zero mean curvature and positive area second variation. Moreover, $r=r_{\rm th}$ and $\ell=0$ are coordinate representations. A signed throat-adapted coordinate reveals two asymptotically flat ends, excludes closed timelike curves, and establishes that the throat is timelike, not a horizon. These conclusions hold independently of $J_{\rm ADM}=ar_0$. The supporting stress tensor, reconstructed from the Einstein tensor, is an effective phenomenological source. We do not claim any microscopic matter Lagrangian or realistic equation of state. We also determine the ergoregion onset. Since the full Hamilton--Jacobi equation is nonseparable, we limit ourselves to derive the optical result for a one-dimensional equatorial capture interval.

</details>


### [34] [Covariant kinetic theory of photons coupled to massive particles in curved spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22837)
*Long Cui,Yi-fan Cai,Xin Hao,Bin Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper formulates a covariant Boltzmann equation for photons and massive particles in general curved spacetimes, derives collision integrals for key radiative processes (emission/absorption, Compton, particle scattering), enforces the H-theorem to recover the Einstein relation and detailed balance, and applies the framework to thermal radiation from Kerr accretion disks—both globally and locally in thermodynamic equilibrium.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a first-principles, fully general-relativistic kinetic description of non-equilibrium radiative transfer involving photons and massive particles in strong gravitational fields, where standard flat-spacetime or fluid approximations break down.

Method: Covariant phase-space formulation of the Boltzmann equation; explicit construction of Lorentz- and diffeomorphism-invariant collision integrals for photon–matter interactions (including emission/absorption, Compton scattering, and massive-particle scattering); derivation of conservation laws and entropy production; imposition of the H-theorem to constrain transition rates; application to Kerr spacetime with accretion disk models (global and local thermodynamic equilibrium).

Result: A fully covariant Boltzmann equation with explicitly gauge-invariant collision terms; proof of energy-momentum and particle number conservation; derivation of the Einstein relation from the H-theorem; recovery of covariant photon thermodynamics (e.g., Planck spectrum, Stefan–Boltzmann law) at equilibrium; concrete computation of thermal radiation spectra from Kerr accretion disks under global and local equilibrium assumptions; extension to arbitrary multi-species, null–massive interaction processes.

Conclusion: The framework provides a rigorous, geometrically consistent foundation for relativistic radiative transfer in strong-field astrophysics—enabling accurate modeling of emission, absorption, and scattering in black hole environments, neutron stars, and early-universe plasmas, beyond the limitations of fluid or approximate kinetic treatments.

Abstract: This paper develops the covariant Boltzmann equation governing the coupled evolution of photons and massive particles in general curved spacetimes. We construct the collision integrals for photon emission-absorption, Compton scattering, and scattering between massive particles, and show the conservation laws. Requiring the H-theorem to hold determines the relation between the absorption and emission transition rates, which reduces to the Einstein relation in the non-relativistic limit. At equilibrium the entropy production vanishes, yielding the detailed balance condition, from which we obtain the covariant thermodynamic relations of the photon gas. We illustrate these results with the thermal radiation from an accretion disk in Kerr spacetime, first in global equilibrium and then in a Keplerian disk in local thermodynamic equilibrium. We further extend the collision integrals to arbitrary multi-species processes involving both null and massive particles. The formulation treats non-equilibrium radiative processes in strong gravitational fields from first principles.

</details>


### [35] [Charged Klein-Gordon modes on an Ellis wormhole: magnetic confinement and conditional Heun solvability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22860)
*Abdullah Guvendi,Omar Mustafa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes charged Klein-Gordon modes on a 2+1D Ellis wormhole in a uniform external magnetic field (defined via Euclidean embedding), revealing radial confinement for nonzero magnetic coupling, reduction to confluent Heun equation, and non-generic polynomial solutions constrained by termination conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand quantum scalar field behavior—specifically charged Klein-Gordon modes—on a topologically nontrivial, catenoidal 2+1D spacetime (Ellis wormhole) under an externally imposed magnetic field, with emphasis on spectral properties, confinement, and exact solvability.

Method: Uses geometric embedding to define a regular azimuthal gauge field; performs separation of variables on the Klein-Gordon equation; maps the radial equation to a one-dimensional Schrödinger-type operator; identifies effective potential structure and asymptotics; reduces radial ODE to confluent Heun form; analyzes polynomial (quasi-polynomial) solutions via simultaneous termination conditions; distinguishes continuous-ℓ formal solvability from physically admissible integer azimuthal quantum numbers.

Result: Radial confinement arises for nonzero magnetic coupling due to quadratic asymptotic growth of effective potential; radial equation is exactly reducible to confluent Heun class; genuine polynomial solutions exist only on codimension-2 parameter submanifolds (requiring two independent termination conditions); relativistic energy eigenvalues are derived explicitly for lowest polynomial sector; angular periodicity enforces integer ℓ, breaking formal continuous-ℓ solvability.

Conclusion: Charged scalar fields on this magnetized 2+1D Ellis wormhole exhibit analytically tractable yet highly constrained spectral structure: confinement is generic, but exact bound-state energies with polynomial solutions are nongeneric and parametrically fine-tuned; the interplay between topology, embedding-induced gauge structure, and separation constants dictates physical admissibility of solutions.

Abstract: We study charged Klein-Gordon modes on a $(2+1)$-dimensional Ellis traversable wormhole in an external magnetic field defined as uniform in an auxiliary Euclidean embedding space. The spatial geometry is intrinsically catenoidal, while the embedding is used only to construct the external gauge connection. Pullback of the ambient potential yields a regular azimuthal gauge field whose associated intrinsic magnetic scalar reverses sign across the throat. Separation of the Klein-Gordon equation leads to a radial problem that is unitarily equivalent to a one-dimensional Schrödinger operator with a regular effective potential. For nonzero magnetic coupling, the quadratic asymptotic term produces radial confinement on the complete wormhole. The radial equation can be reduced to the confluent-Heun class. Polynomial truncation is possible only on constrained parameter submanifolds determined by simultaneous termination conditions and therefore does not represent the generic confined spectrum. We derive the corresponding relativistic energies and analyze the lowest polynomial sector, while distinguishing the formal continuous-$\ell$ solvability correlations from the integer values of the azimuthal separation constant required by the standard angular periodicity condition.

</details>


### [36] [Probing black holes surrounded by Dust field in Quantum Fluctuation Modified Gravity with twin-peak QPOs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22874)
*Kamola Shermatova,Qodir Badalov,Abubakir Shermatov,Javlon Rayimbaev,Bekzod Rahmatov,Shokhzod Jumaniyozov,Dilafruz Xujanova,Farhod Nuraliev*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates a quantum-fluctuation-modified black hole solution with surrounding dust, analyzing how the quantum parameter α and dust strength K affect horizon structure, orbital dynamics, and QPO models; MCMC fitting to astrophysical QPO data yields constrained ranges: K ∈ (0, 0.5) and α ∈ (0.3, 0.6) at 2σ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore observational signatures of quantum-gravity corrections in black hole spacetimes by incorporating dust as a classical matter field and studying their joint impact on strong-field relativistic phenomena—particularly twin-peak quasi-periodic oscillations (QPOs)—as probes of spacetime geometry.

Method: Analytical derivation of static, spherically symmetric black hole solutions in quantum-fluctuation-modified gravity with dust; systematic analysis of horizon structure, effective potential, circular orbit radii (ISCO, MBO, MCO), and epicyclic frequencies; construction and comparative testing of three twin-peak QPO models (relativistic precession, warped-disk, epicyclic-resonance) against observational data from four sources using Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) inference.

Result: K and α exert opposing effects: increasing K compresses horizons and shifts orbital features inward, while increasing α restores Schwarzschild behavior and shifts them outward; all QPO resonance radii follow this same trend; MCMC fitting constrains K < 0.5 and α ∈ (0.3, 0.6) at 2σ across all models and sources.

Conclusion: The quantum-fluctuation parameter α and dust-field parameter K are jointly constrained by current QPO observations, supporting the viability of this modified gravity framework; the consistent directional trends across geometrical and dynamical quantities strengthen the physical interpretability of the model, and the tight α-range suggests measurable quantum-gravity effects may already be probed in stellar- and intermediate-mass black hole systems.

Abstract: We study a static, spherically symmetric black hole solution surrounded by a dust field within quantum fluctuation modified gravity, in which the quantum-fluctuation parameter $α$ and the dust-field strength parameter $K$. The horizon structure reveals the two parameters acting in opposite directions: strengthening $K$ draws the inner and outer horizons together until they merge into a single extremal horizon, while a larger $α$ restores the Schwarzschild limit; the same opposing trend extends to the effective potential and the characteristic circular-orbit radii -- the innermost stable circular orbit, the marginally bound orbit, and the marginal circular orbit -- which shift inward with $K$ and outward with $α$, and to the Keplerian and radial epicyclic frequencies of massive test particles. From these frequencies we build three twin-peak quasi-periodic oscillation models -- relativistic precession, warped-disk, and epicyclic-resonance -- along with their $3{:}2$, $4{:}3$, and $5{:}4$ resonance radii, all following the same $K$-$α$ trend. Testing all three models against twin-peak QPO data from two stellar-mass (GRO~J1655$-$40, XTE~J1550$-$564) and two intermediate-mass (M82~X$-$1, NGC~1313~X$-$1) sources through a Markov Chain Monte Carlo analysis places best-fit constraints, at the $2σ$ level, of $K\in(0,0.5)$ and $α\in(0.3,0.6)$ across all sources and models.

</details>


### [37] [Time and causality in quantum gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22890)
*Charis Anastopoulos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper re-examines the 'problem of time' in quantum gravity by tracing conceptual tensions about time—causal order, duration, and the present—across classical mechanics, relativity, quantum theory, and QFT; it argues these tensions predate quantum gravity and persist even in weak-gravity regimes, surveying how major quantum gravity approaches handle time and emphasizing implications from quantum foundations and relativistic quantum information.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the narrow view that the problem of time arises solely from quantising general relativity, and instead show it stems from deeper, pre-quantum-gravity tensions among causal structure, clocks, observables, and measurement across foundational physical theories.

Method: Conceptual analysis of time across classical mechanics, relativity, quantum theory, and QFT; comparative survey of quantum gravity approaches (canonical/Wheeler-DeWitt, path-integral/histories, perturbative, causal sets, twistor theory) along three criteria: retention of external time, time-problematization via quantisation, or foundational incorporation of causal/temporal structure; focus on linearised and weak-gravity quantum systems to test operational relevance beyond Planck scale.

Result: Demonstrates that conceptual difficulties with time are already present in the parent theories of quantum gravity—not emergent only upon quantisation—and persist in weak-field regimes, implying the problem is operational and foundational, not merely Planck-scale or technical.

Conclusion: The problem of time is not a pathology of quantum gravity per se, but a symptom of unresolved tensions in how time is conceptualised and operationalised across physics; viable quantum gravity frameworks must therefore address temporal structure at their foundations—not as an afterthought—and weak-gravity experiments are crucial for empirical and conceptual progress.

Abstract: The problem of time in quantum gravity is often presented as a consequence of applying quantum theory to general relativity. These notes adopt a broader perspective. We first examine how time enters classical mechanics, relativity, quantum theory, and quantum field theory, distinguishing three principal aspects: causal order, temporal duration, and the present. This analysis shows that the conceptual difficulties associated with time do not arise only when gravity is quantised. Tensions between causal structure, clocks, observables, and measurement are already present in the theories from which quantum gravity is constructed. We then survey responses to the problem of time across the principal approaches to quantum gravity. These include canonical and Wheeler-DeWitt quantisation, path-integral and histories formulations, perturbative and background-dependent approaches, causal sets, and twistor theory. The programmes are compared according to whether they retain an external time, render time problematic through quantisation, or incorporate causal or temporal structure into their foundations. Particular emphasis is placed on questions arising from quantum foundations and relativistic quantum information. We conclude by examining linearised gravity and weak-gravity quantum systems. This regime shows that the problem of time is not confined to Planck-scale physics, but arises already in attempts to provide a predictive and operationally meaningful quantum description of spacetime near flat geometry.

</details>

Abstract: We investigate the equilibrium structure of electrically charged quark stars within the framework of energy-momentum-squared gravity (EMSG), adopting a unified interacting quark-matter equation of state that incorporates perturbative QCD corrections, color superconductivity, and finite-strange-quark-mass effects. Analytical solutions to the null geodesic equations are obtained for each category. Based on these solutions, we numerically simulate light trajectories and analyze in detail the effects of the black hole charge $Q$ and the cosmic string parameter $α$. It is found that the cosmic string parameter $α$ enhances the winding behavior of null geodesics across all three main categories. Finally, we consider light deflection in the equatorial plane and derive an expression for the deflection angle, revealing that the charge $Q$ decreases the deflection angle, whereas the cosmic string parameter $α$ increases it.

Abstract: It is well known that in (2+1)-dimensional general relativity, black hole solutions exist only in the anti-de Sitter case--the so-called Banados-Teitelboim-Zanelli (BTZ) black hole. In this letter, we construct, for the first time, three independent LQG-inspired frameworks for (2+1)-dimensional black holes: the $\barμ$-scheme holonomy corrections, the quantum Oppenheimer-Snyder collapse, and a covariant effective model. Remarkably, all three approaches yield the same qualitative picture: when LQG-inspired corrections are included, we find three distinct types of solutions corresponding to asymptotically flat, anti-de Sitter, and de Sitter geometries. Our results demonstrate that the asymmetry between classical (2+1)-dimensional and (3+1)-dimensional solutions is eliminated upon incorporating effective quantum gravity effects.

</details>

Abstract: We investigate the dynamics of charged particles in the dipolar electromagnetic field generated by a stationary current loop around a static black hole in the Randall--Sundrum braneworld model. The current loop is located at the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO) of the tidal charged black hole, and the azimuthal electromagnetic four-potential is obtained as an exact analytical solution of the Maxwell equations in the curved background. Starting from the Hamilton--Jacobi equation, we derive the effective potential for charged particle motion in both the interior and exterior regions of the current loop, and analyze the dependence of the ISCO radius, angular momentum, energy, and orbital frequency on the tidal charge parameter $b$ and the magnetic coupling parameter $ω$. We further compute three fundamental frequencies and show that the dipolar loop field revives a nonzero nodal precession frequency even though the background is static and non-rotating. Using these frequencies we construct five twin-peak QPO models (RP, WD, and ER2--ER4) and perform a Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) analysis to constrain the braneworld parameters $(b,ω)$ from the observed twin-peak QPO frequencies of six black hole sources spanning three mass regimes: GRO~J1655--40, XTE~J1550--564, GRS~1915$+$105, H~1743$+$322, M82~X-1, and Sgr~A$^{*}$. ### [44] [Renormalized Dynamical Friction in Black-Hole Spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23192)

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate a consistent, causal, and renormalizable framework for the gravitational self-force acting on a compact object immersed in matter (e.g., accretion disks or interstellar medium) around a black hole—extending beyond vacuum self-force theory to include environmental coupling and backreaction.

Method: Schwinger–Keldysh closed-time-path integration to obtain a causal effective action; curved-spacetime Feynman diagrammatics organized by mass ratio and environmental density; local large-momentum expansion to isolate UV divergence; reduction of retarded Green’s function to flat tangent-space form; translation into Barack–Ori mode-sum regularization with analytic puncture; near-zone exact matching for dust to fix finite Wilson coefficient.

Result: A renormalized local equation of motion for the gravitational self-force in matter; explicit analytic puncture and regularization parameters for ideal-fluid environments; complete renormalization prescription—including finite Wilson coefficient—for collisionless dust derived from near-zone matching.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a rigorous, computationally ready renormalization framework for the gravitational self-force in matter-filled black-hole spacetimes, resolving both divergent and finite parts of the fluid–particle coupling, thus enabling future numerical self-force simulations in realistic astrophysical environments.

Abstract: We derive the renormalized local equation of motion for the gravitational self-force on a compact object moving through matter in a black-hole spacetime. We integrate out the environment and metric perturbations on a Schwinger--Keldysh contour, obtaining a causal effective action given by a sum of curved-spacetime Feynman diagrams organized by powers of the mass ratio and environmental density. At leading order, an environmental wake gravitationally backreacts on the worldline. The corresponding diagram contains a logarithmic ultraviolet divergence that renormalizes the leading dissipative fluid--particle coupling. We isolate the divergence with a local large-momentum expansion, in which the curved-spacetime retarded Green's function reduces to its flat tangent-space form. We translate the resulting singularity into a Barack--Ori mode-sum scheme, deriving an analytic puncture and regularization parameters for an ideal-fluid environment. Mode subtraction fixes the divergent part but not the finite Wilson coefficient. For collisionless dust, we determine this coefficient by matching to an exact near-zone calculation for a black hole moving through the medium. This gives a complete renormalization prescription suitable for numerical self-force calculations.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the cosmic censorship conjecture's status for self-similar Einstein–Euler collapse by investigating whether naked singularities formed in such models persist under generic (non-symmetric) gravitational perturbations.

Method: Mathematical analysis of the Einstein–Euler system under spherically symmetric self-similar collapse; rigorous treatment of small $C^{1,α}$ conformal metric perturbations without symmetry assumptions; use of causality and characteristic geometry to track gravitational radiation focusing and trapped surface formation.

Result: Small $C^{1,α}$ gravitational perturbations generically lead to trapped surface formation before any fluid singularity develops — i.e., the naked singularity is unstable and gets hidden behind an event horizon.

Conclusion: The naked singularities in this class of self-similar Einstein–Euler collapse are gravitationally unstable, supporting weak cosmic censorship for this physically motivated matter model.

Abstract: We study the instability of the naked singularities arising in the spherically symmetric self-similar collapsing of the Einstein--Euler system under gravitational perturbations. We show that small $C^{1,α}$ perturbations (without any symmetries) of the initial conformal metric lead to trapped surface formation. One key point is that the speed of sound is strictly slower the the speed of light, so the gravitational radiation can become sufficiently concentrated to form a trapped surface before the fluid develops any singularity.

</details>


### [46] [Optimal all-angle reconstruction of the Hellings-Downs curve](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23400)
*Jing-Hong Han,Zhi-Chao Zhao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a joint reconstruction method for the Hellings-Downs curve using full pulsar-pair covariance, improving precision in nanohertz gravitational-wave detection via pulsar timing arrays.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the precision of detecting the angular correlation pattern (Hellings-Downs curve) in pulsar timing array data, which is critical for confirming the gravitational-wave origin of a stochastic background.

Result: On NANOGrav 15 yr data, bin standard deviations reduced by up to 12.9% (median 9.5%); for a SKAO-like PTA, predicted reductions are up to 36.4% (median 29.6%).

Conclusion: The proposed joint covariance-based estimator significantly enhances measurement precision of the Hellings-Downs curve, strengthening tests of gravitational-wave physics with PTAs.

Abstract: Pulsar timing arrays (PTAs) detect nanohertz gravitational waves through spatial correlations between the timing residuals of different pulsars. For an isotropic, unpolarized stochastic background in general relativity, the ensemble-mean correlation follows the Hellings--Downs (HD) curve; measuring this angular pattern tests the gravitational-wave origin of the signal. Standard bin-by-bin reconstructions optimize the weights within each angular bin separately. We reconstruct the curve jointly using the full pulsar-pair covariance, retaining a free amplitude in every bin. The resulting all-angle best linear unbiased estimator minimizes the variance of every bin value and every linear combination of bins. Applied to the public NANOGrav 15 yr data products, our method reduces the bin standard deviations by up to 12.9\%, with a median reduction of 9.5\%. For a future Square Kilometre Array Observatory (SKAO)-like PTA, the predicted reduction reaches 36.4\%, with a median of 29.6\%, enabling significantly more precise measurements of the gravitational-wave background.

</details>


### [47] [Grey-body factor from correspondence with quasinormal mode for gravitational perturbation of higher-dimensional Reissner-Nordström black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23434)
*Hyewon Han,Bogeun Gwak*

Main category: gr-qc
