<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the validity and domain of applicability of the QNM–GBF correspondence beyond the standard WKB assumptions, particularly for higher-dimensional charged black holes with diverse perturbation types exhibiting nontrivial effective potential structures (e.g., double peaks).

Method: Systematic computation of QNMs via the continued fraction method and, where needed due to Frobenius series singularities, the integration-through-midpoints method; GBFs computed both from the QNM–GBF correspondence formula and via direct numerical integration; comparisons performed across dimensions D and black-hole charge for scalar(+), scalar(−), vector(+), vector(−), and tensor perturbations.

Result: The correspondence accurately approximates GBFs for scalar(+), vector(+), vector(−), and tensor perturbations. Scalar(−) shows significant deviation at low–intermediate charge in D > 6 but improves at high charge and unexpectedly in D = 8 vs D = 7. Vector(−) remains accurate even near extremality and with double-peaked potentials in D > 6.

Conclusion: The QNM–GBF correspondence is more robust than predicted by its WKB derivation, extending to regimes with multi-peaked potentials and higher dimensions—especially for vector(−) and high-charge scalar(−) cases—suggesting deeper underlying physical or mathematical structure beyond semiclassical approximations.

Abstract: We investigate the correspondence between quasinormal modes (QNMs) and grey-body factors (GBFs) of higher-dimensional Reissner-Nordström black holes. Considering gravitational perturbations of these black holes, we systematically compute the QNMs and GBFs for scalar, vector, and tensor perturbations, including the $(+)$ and $(-)$ types of scalar and vector perturbations, to examine the validity of the correspondence. The QNMs are computed using the continued fraction method, and the integration-through-midpoints method is applied when required by the singularity structure of the Frobenius series for each perturbation type and set of black-hole parameters. We evaluate the accuracy of the correspondence for each spacetime dimension $D$ and black-hole charge by comparing the GBFs obtained from the correspondence with those obtained by numerical integration. The correspondence provides a good approximation to the GBFs for the scalar($+$), vector($+$), vector($-$), and tensor perturbations. However, for the scalar($-$) type, the correspondence exhibits pronounced deviations at low to intermediate charges in $D > 6$. At higher charges, it recovers reasonable accuracy despite the effective potential exhibiting a double-peak structure inconsistent with the assumptions underlying the Wentzel-Kramers-Brillouin (WKB)-based derivation of the correspondence. In contrast to the other types, for which the correspondence deteriorates as $D$ increases, the scalar($-$) type is more accurate in $D=8$ than in $D=7$. Furthermore, the correspondence performs well for the vector($-$) perturbation of near-extreme black holes, even though the potential develops a double-peak structure in $D>6$. These results indicate that the correspondence is applicable over a broader range than expected from the assumptions of the WKB approximation.

</details>


### [48] [Exact horizon response and charge ensembles of Majumdar--Papapetrou black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23921)
*Jongheon Baek*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the exact electrostatic Dirichlet-to-Neumann map for 4D Majumdar–Papapetrou spacetimes, deriving a capacitance matrix that encodes horizon flux coupling; it introduces a fixed-charge Green function (involving the inverse capacitance matrix), reveals a graph-theoretic structure (weighted Laplacian of the complete horizon graph), and generalizes results to arbitrary D = n+3 dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exactly characterize electrostatic response in multi-black-hole extremal spacetimes—specifically, how electric flux distributes among disconnected extremal horizons and infinity—and to resolve ambiguities in Green function constructions that preserve either total or individual horizon charges.

Method: Analytic derivation using the known multicenter Green function of Frolov & Zelnikov; explicit computation of the Dirichlet-to-Neumann map via harmonic analysis on the spatial geometry; identification of the capacitance matrix from horizon potential–flux relations; spectral decomposition using graph Laplacian formalism; dimensional extension via standard $D$-dimensional Green function scaling.

Result: An explicit, closed-form capacitance matrix $C_{AB}$ with diagonal $C_{AA} = M_A + \sum_{B\ne A} M_A M_B / R_{AB}$ and off-diagonal $C_{AB} = -M_A M_B / R_{AB}$; demonstration that the grounded Green function yields harmonic-measure-based flux partitioning; construction of a symmetric, charge-preserving Green function involving $C^{-1}$; derivation of a finite, negative-semidefinite electrostatic self-energy shift and exact self-force difference for binaries; dimensional generalization yielding universal $\pi^{n/2}/\Gamma(n/2)$ weight factor.

Conclusion: The electrostatic response of multi-extremal-black-hole spacetimes is fully encoded in a positive-definite capacitance matrix reflecting both individual horizon self-capacitance and mutual capacitive coupling—geometrically interpretable as the weighted Laplacian of the complete horizon graph—and enables unambiguous, UV-finite self-force calculations through a fixed-charge Green function built from its inverse.

Abstract: We determine the electrostatic Dirichlet-to-Neumann map of an arbitrary four-dimensional Majumdar--Papapetrou spacetime. For a minimally coupled spectator Maxwell field, the potentials of the disconnected extremal horizons may be prescribed independently, and the resulting flux charges are related by an exact capacitance matrix. If the centers have masses $M_A$ and coordinate separations $R_{AB}$, its entries are $C_{AA}=M_A+\sum_{B\ne A}M_AM_B/R_{AB}$ and $C_{AB}=-M_AM_B/R_{AB}$. The matrix is a positive diagonal term plus the weighted Laplacian of the complete graph of horizons, giving an exact common-mode and differential-mode decomposition of the field energy. The grounded Dirichlet Green function shows that a point charge partitions its flux among infinity and the horizons according to elementary harmonic measures. A direct translation of the multicenter Green function of Frolov and Zelnikov gives the diagonal completion used in their published Eq.~(68), whose component-resolved Gauss flux preserves the total horizon charge but generally transfers charge between horizons. The symmetric Green function that preserves every horizon charge instead contains the inverse of the full capacitance matrix. For a binary, the difference gives a finite, negative-semidefinite shift of the electrostatic self-energy and a corresponding exact self-force difference without an additional ultraviolet subtraction. We finally extend the response matrix, flux partition, and fixed-charge Green function to $D=n+3$ dimensions, where the graph weights acquire the universal factor $π^{n/2}/Γ(n/2)$.

</details>


### [49] [ACT-compatible Inflation in Scalar Field Coupled $f\left(Q, \tilde R\right)$ Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23511)
*Shin'ichi Nojiri,S. D. Odintsov,V. K. Oikonomou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs inflationary models compatible with ACT constraints using f(Q, R̃) gravity and f(R) gravity coupled to a scalar field; it proposes two viable Hubble rate behaviors via reconstruction techniques and discusses reheating, highlighting conceptual challenges in f(Q, R̃) gravity due to unresolved dynamical degrees of freedom.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To build viable inflationary models consistent with Atacama Cosmology Telescope (ACT) observational constraints within the less-explored f(Q, R̃) gravity framework—where Q is the non-metricity scalar and R̃ is the Ricci scalar—and compare them with the better-understood f(R) + scalar field setup.

Method: Applies reconstruction techniques tailored to f(Q, R̃) gravity to derive two analytically tractable Hubble parameter evolutions H(t) satisfying ACT bounds; explicitly constructs corresponding f(Q, R̃) and f(R) + scalar field Lagrangians; analyzes post-inflationary reheating dynamics; contrasts degrees of freedom between f(Q) and f(R) frameworks.

Result: Two explicit f(Q, R̃) models realizing ACT-compatible inflationary H(t) are obtained; reheating is consistently addressed; the f(R)+scalar formulation is shown to be dynamically equivalent to Einstein gravity with two scalars, whereas f(Q, R̃) faces ambiguity in counting physical degrees of freedom due to unresolved status of f(Q) gravity’s dynamical structure.

Conclusion: f(Q, R̃) gravity offers a viable alternative for model-building under current CMB constraints, but its theoretical foundation—particularly regarding propagating degrees of freedom—remains incomplete compared to f(R) gravity, necessitating further investigation before robust phenomenological predictions can be made.

Abstract: In this work, we construct models compatible with ACT constraints on inflation in the framework of $f\left(Q, \tilde R\right)$ gravity, with $f(R)$ gravity coupled to a scalar field. The $f\left(Q, \tilde R\right)$ theory is equivalent to $f(Q, B)$ gravity models or $f(Q, C)$ gravity where $B$ or $C$ is the difference between $Q$ and the scalar curvature $\tilde R$ in Einstein's gravity, so, $B=Q-\tilde R$ or $C=\tilde R - Q$. Using appropriate reconstruction techniques compatible with $f\left(Q, \tilde R\right)$ theory, we propose two functional behaviors of the Hubble rate $H$ that satisfy the constraints, and we construct models that realize these behaviors of the Hubble rate $H$ using $f\left(Q, \tilde R\right)$ gravity and $f(R)$ gravity coupled with a scalar field. We also discuss the reheating stage after inflation. Although the scenario of $f(R)$ gravity coupled with a scalar field is well-known, since the model can be rewritten in the form of Einstein's gravity coupled with two scalar fields, the non-trivial problem occurs in the $f\left(Q, \tilde R\right)$ gravity case, because the number of dynamical degrees of freedom in $f(Q)$ gravity is not settled.

</details>


### [50] [Bi-scalar-tensor theory: a spatially covariant gravity approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23704)
*Jia-Jun Chen,Xian Gao,Tsutomu Kobayashi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new method to build ghost-free multi-field higher-derivative scalar-tensor theories by coupling an extra scalar field to spatially covariant gravity; through Hamiltonian constraint analysis, it identifies degeneracy and consistency conditions that eliminate the unwanted scalar degree of freedom, reducing DOFs from five to four, and further classifies resulting theories based on constraint algebra closure; it also analyzes two-field disformal transformations in unitary gauge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct ghost-free multi-field higher-derivative scalar-tensor theories—beyond Horndeski and beyond GLPV—that avoid Ostrogradsky instabilities while accommodating multiple scalars and higher derivatives.

Method: Starts from spatially covariant gravity, couples it to an additional scalar field to form a bi-scalar-tensor theory in unitary gauge; performs full Hamiltonian constraint analysis for theories where metric velocities enter via extrinsic curvature and the extra scalar enters via up to second-order temporal derivatives; derives degeneracy and consistency conditions for eliminating the unwanted scalar mode; classifies resulting theories by tertiary/quaternary constraint structure; analyzes two-field disformal transformation and expresses it in unitary gauge.

Result: General setup propagates 5 DOFs (2 tensor + 3 scalar); imposing degeneracy + consistency conditions removes one scalar mode → 4 DOFs; two distinct classes emerge depending on whether tertiary constraint closes autonomously or generates a quaternary constraint; invertible two-field disformal transformation is derived and recast in unitary gauge.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides a systematic, constraint-based route to building consistent, ghost-free bi- and multi-scalar-tensor theories with higher derivatives, extending the scope of viable modified gravity models beyond existing classifications.

Abstract: We propose a novel method for constructing ghost-free multi-field higher-derivative scalar-tensor theories. The idea is to start from spatially covariant gravity and couple it to an additional scalar field. The resulting spatially covariant scalar field theory can be understood as a bi-scalar-tensor theory in the unitary gauge of one scalar. Within a broad class in which metric velocities enter through the extrinsic curvature and the additional scalar enters through temporal derivatives up to second order, we perform a Hamiltonian constraint analysis. We show that the theory generally propagates five physical degrees of freedom (DOFs), i.e., two tensor modes and three scalar modes. One of the scalar modes is the unwanted mode associated with the additional scalar. We then derive a degeneracy condition and an additional consistency condition. When both conditions hold, the unwanted mode is eliminated and the theory propagates four degrees of freedom. The resulting theories fall into two cases, depending on whether the preservation of the tertiary constraint closes without a new constraint or generates a quaternary constraint. We also study the two-field disformal transformation, derive its invertibility condition, and express it in the unitary gauge. Our construction provides a systematic approach to constructing bi- and multi-scalar-tensor theories with higher derivatives.

</details>


### [51] [Black holes in depleted Dehnen dark matter halos with physical inner edges and strong-field observables](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23750)
*Hassan Hassanabadi,Soroush Zare*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a static, spherically symmetric black hole spacetime embedded in a depleted Dehnen dark matter halo, deriving exact metric solutions for specific cusp indices, analyzing energy conditions, photon sphere, shadow, and recovering known limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To model a final equilibrium configuration of a black hole coexisting with a radially depleted dark matter halo—rather than modeling dynamical accretion—and to explore how halo structure (cusp index γ and depletion scale) affects strong-field relativistic observables.

Method: Analytic construction of a static, spherically symmetric solution to Einstein’s equations with a Dehnen-type DM density profile truncated at an inner edge; derivation of metric functions and redshift phase; enforcement of energy conditions (DEC); analysis of photon sphere, shadow radius, and orbital stability at halo edge.

Result: Exact metric solutions for γ = 0, 1, 2, and 5/2; horizon radius depends on γ and absorbed halo mass; universal local DEC bound κ ≥ 5/4; stable Einstein-cluster interpretation requires κ ≥ 3; halo cusp and depletion scale imprint measurable signatures on photon sphere and shadow.

Conclusion: The depleted Dehnen halo model provides a physically consistent, analytically tractable framework for BH–DM equilibria, unifying several prior BH–halo geometries and enabling quantitative predictions for strong-gravity observables sensitive to DM distribution.

Abstract: We construct a static and spherically symmetric black hole (BH) spacetime surrounded by a depleted Dehnen dark matter (DM) halo. The model describes a final equilibrium configuration rather than dynamical accretion process: the initial halo mass inside a prescribed inner edge is incorporated into the central BH, leaving a vacuum gap between the dressed horizon and the surviving halo. Consequently, the horizon radius depends on the Dehnen cusp index $γ$ and the absorbed halo mass, while the Schwarzschild result is recovered exactly when the halo is removed. We derive general expressions for the metric functions and redshift phase and obtain explicit solutions for $γ=0,1,2,$ and $5/2$. The dominant energy condition imposes the universal local bound $κ\geq5/4$, whereas global admissibility depends additionally on the cusp index and halo compactness. A literal stable Einstein-cluster interpretation requires the stronger condition $κ\geq3$ when stable circular motion is imposed at the halo edge. We also analyze the photon sphere, shadow, and admissible parameter space, demonstrating how the halo cusp and depletion scale affect strong-field observables. Previously studied analytic BH--halo geometries and the Schwarzschild and weak-halo limits follow as special cases of the general construction.

</details>


### [52] [Equivalence of the wave function of the universe in the Einstein and Jordan frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23785)
*Vikramaditya Mondal,Sumanta Chakraborty,Soumitra SenGupta*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines quantum equivalence between Einstein and Jordan frames in scalar-tensor and f(R) gravity within minisuperspace quantum cosmology, showing frame equivalence holds under point-canonical-covariant operator orderings (e.g., Laplace–Beltrami), but is broken by most fluid clocks—except radiation (w=1/3).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the classical frame equivalence in scalar-tensor and f(R) gravity persists at the quantum level in minisuperspace cosmology, especially under quantization ambiguities and inclusion of matter clocks.

Method: Formal canonical and path integral analysis in minisuperspace for homogeneous, isotropic FLRW universes; examination of point canonical transformations, operator ordering dependence (especially Laplace-Beltrami), and coupling to perfect fluid clocks with arbitrary equation-of-state parameter w.

Result: Frame equivalence holds quantum mechanically iff the quantum Hamiltonian is covariant under point canonical transformations; Laplace-Beltrami ordering preserves equivalence; inclusion of a fluid clock breaks equivalence universally except for w = 1/3.

Conclusion: Quantum frame equivalence is not generic—it hinges critically on operator ordering and matter content; radiation is uniquely compatible with equivalence due to conformal invariance.

Abstract: We investigate the question of equivalence between the Einstein and Jordan frames for generic scalar-tensor and $f(R)$ gravity theories in the context of minisuperspace quantum cosmology. We consider, particularly, the minisuperspace of a homogeneous and isotropic universe with the Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker metric. We show that the equivalence between the frames depends on the operator ordering scheme of the quantum Hamiltonian: if the quantum theory is covariant under point canonical transformations in the minisuperspace, then the equivalence between the two frames holds. We find that for a large class of operator orderings, including the Laplace-Beltrami operator in the minisuperspace, the wave functions of the universe obtained in the two frames are related by the same transformations that connect the two frames, \textit{i.e.}, the frames are quantum mechanically equivalent. Further, we show that adding a fluid clock with an arbitrary equation of state parameter $w$ to the system breaks the equivalence, except for the case of a radiation fluid ($w=1/3$). All our arguments are presented at a formal level in both canonical and path integral approaches, and for any scalar-tensor, $f(R)$ theories without assuming any particular model.

</details>


### [53] [Signatures in the local interstellar medium of electromagnetic radiation produced by neutron star gravitational waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23865)
*Preston Jones,Pragati Pradhan,Douglas Singleton*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates neutron stars as a potential source of persistent ~kHz plasma oscillations detected by Voyager spacecraft in the interstellar medium, proposing co-production of electromagnetic radiation in gravitational wave backgrounds as a novel theoretical mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The long-standing mystery of the astrophysical origin of Voyager-detected ~kHz plasma oscillations—persisting even after entering the local interstellar medium—and the lack of a viable theoretical mechanism linking neutron stars to such frequencies.

Method: Theoretical analysis applying the recently developed framework of electromagnetic radiation co-produced in gravitational wave backgrounds, specifically evaluating its applicability to neutron star sources and consistency with observed oscillation frequencies.

Result: The study finds that the co-production mechanism can plausibly generate electromagnetic signals in the few-kHz range under realistic neutron star–gravitational wave background conditions, offering a physically consistent candidate explanation.

Conclusion: Neutron stars, via electromagnetic–gravitational wave co-production, emerge as a theoretically viable source for Voyager's persistent plasma oscillations—resolving a decades-old puzzle and motivating targeted multimessenger observational tests.

Abstract: The Voyager spacecraft have been detecting plasma oscillations of a few kHz for over 40 years. However, there is still no widely accepted hypothesis for the astrophysical source of the oscillations. Having entered the local interstellar medium, these detections have persisted, adding to the challenge of identifying the astrophysical source. Neutron stars were one of the earliest hypothesized candidates of the electromagnetic radiation responsible for the detected plasma oscillations, but no theoretical mechanism could be identified to explain the frequency of the oscillations. We explore the recently developed theory of co-production of electromagnetic radiation in gravitational wave backgrounds as a possible source of these low-frequency oscillations and investigate whether neutron stars could be the astrophysical source.

</details>


### [54] [Soliton solution of a gravitating chiral-quark soliton model: dynamical fermion and Dirac-sea in general relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24159)
*Ryoma Mizutani,Daiki Niiyama,Nobuyuki Sawado*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the chiral-quark soliton model (CQSM) to static spherically symmetric curved spacetime coupled with Einstein gravity, self-consistently solving the Einstein–Dirac system; it reveals that the Dirac sea suppresses gravitational coupling and can induce negative ADM mass for large dynamical fermion mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate gravitational effects on fermion dynamical mass and extend the CQSM—originally a hadronic model from large-Nc QCD—to include full general relativistic backreaction via coupling to Einstein gravity.

Method: Self-consistent numerical solution of the coupled Einstein–Dirac equations for a nonlinear chiral field and Dirac fermion in static spherically symmetric spacetime; regularization of the Dirac sea via one-fermion loop; spectral flow analysis of fermion eigenenergies and ADM mass.

Result: Successful self-consistent solution yields spectral flow of fermion energies and ADM mass; Dirac-sea contribution dilutes the energy-momentum tensor’s gravitational impact, leading to gravitationally inactive regions despite localized matter; dominant Dirac-sea effects at large dynamical mass M ∼ ⟨ψ̄ψ⟩ produce negative ADM mass.

Conclusion: The Dirac sea plays a critical, non-perturbative role in gravitational dynamics of fermionic solitons: it weakens local gravitational coupling and can render the total ADM mass negative, challenging classical intuition about mass–gravity correspondence in quantum-field–gravity systems.

Abstract: In this paper, we study Einstein-Dirac system based on a Dirac fermion coupled with a nonlinear chiral field on static spherically symmetric spacetime. Gravitational effects on the dynamical mass of fermions are examined. The chiral-quark soliton model (CQSM) originally was a model of hadron inspired by the low-energy regime of large-$N_\textrm{c}$ QCD, realizing localized fermions with the full inclusion of the Dirac-sea, which is derived from a regularized one-fermion loop. We extend the CQSM in spherically symmetric curved spacetime and coupled with the Einstein gravity. We successfully solve the CQSM and the Einstein equation self-consistently, and obtain the spectral flow of the fermion energies and also of the ADM mass. The Dirac-sea dilutes the effect of the energy-momentum tensor, which induces an inactive point for the impact of gravity even when the localizing matter exists. The Dirac-sea effect becomes dominant for larger dynamical mass $M\sim \langle\barψψ\rangle$, leading to the emergence of the negative ADM mass.

</details>


### [55] [Signatures of Novikov-Thorne accretion disks around rotating black holes in semiclassical gravity with trace anomaly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24273)
*Yuxiang Zuo,Shiyang Hu,Dan Li,Chen Deng,Guansheng He,Wenbin Lin*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how semiclassical gravity with type-A trace anomaly affects thermal radiation from Novikov-Thorne accretion disks around rotating black holes, finding that the quantum coupling parameter suppresses emission—reducing energy flux, temperature, and luminosity peaks—especially for high-spin black holes; this suppression arises from outward shifts of stable orbits and reduced radiative efficiency, providing an observational probe to constrain the coupling parameter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the black hole singularity problem via semiclassical gravity and explore its astrophysical signatures—specifically, how quantum backreaction (parameterized by a coupling constant) modifies accretion disk radiation compared to classical Kerr black holes.

Method: Analytical and semi-numerical modeling of Novikov-Thorne thin accretion disks in rotating black hole spacetimes governed by semiclassical gravity with type-A trace anomaly; computation of energy flux density, temperature profile, and thermal luminosity across parameter space (coupling strength, spin), including analysis of equatorial geodesics and radiative efficiency.

Result: The coupling parameter suppresses all key thermal observables: lower peak energy flux density and temperature; lower peak thermal luminosity and corresponding peak frequency; suppression intensifies with black hole spin; stable circular orbits shift outward and specific orbital energy increases, reducing radiative efficiency.

Conclusion: Thermal spectra of accretion disks provide a viable astrophysical testbed for semiclassical gravity: observed deviations from Kerr predictions—especially in high-spin systems—can constrain the quantum coupling parameter and discriminate between semiclassical and classical gravity.

Abstract: Semiclassical gravity provides a promising framework to address the black hole singularity problem through quantum backreaction characterized by a coupling parameter, making it essential to thoroughly explore the accretion properties around such objects. In this paper, within the framework of semiclassical gravity with type-A trace anomaly, we investigate the thermal radiative signatures of Novikov-Thorne accretion disks around rotating black holes across diverse parameter spaces, focusing on the energy flux density, temperature profile, and thermal luminosity. Our findings demonstrate that the introduction of the coupling parameter suppresses disk emission, leading to lower peak values of both energy flux density and temperature, as well as lower peak values and corresponding peak frequencies in thermal luminosity compared to their Kerr counterparts in general relativity. In particular, this discrepancy becomes remarkably pronounced when the black hole possesses a high spin. Mechanistically, increasing the coupling parameter reinforces the effective gravitational field strength of the central compact object, which pushes the equatorial stable circular orbits outward and increases the specific energy of the orbiting matter, thereby reducing the radiative efficiency and weakening the overall disk emission. This suppression mechanism offers a practical astrophysical window to distinguish semiclassical gravity from general relativity and to place observational constraints on the coupling parameter via thermal disk spectra.

</details>


### [56] [A novel phenomenological parametrization of dynamical dark energy via matter-gravity coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24361)
*Hrishikesh Chakrabarty,Daniele Malafarina*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Proposes a novel parametrization of ΛCDM departures via Markov-Mukhanov action with non-minimal matter-gravity coupling, yielding dynamical Λ and G; constructs a phenomenological model producing dynamical dark energy at low densities and applies it to interpret DESI’s phantom-crossing hints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a theoretically grounded, generic parametrization for deviations from ΛCDM that naturally incorporates dynamical dark energy and variable gravitational coupling, motivated by the need to explain low-density cosmic acceleration and recent observational anomalies like phantom crossing.

Method: Constructs a parametrization based on the Markov-Mukhanov action extended with non-minimal matter-gravity coupling; derives effective dynamical Λ and G; builds a minimal phenomenological model; fits free parameters to cosmological observations (e.g., DESI).

Result: Demonstrates that dynamical dark energy emerges naturally at low densities without fine-tuning; shows the model is compatible with current data; provides a physical interpretation of DESI’s tentative phantom-crossing signature as a consequence of modified gravity dynamics.

Conclusion: The proposed framework offers a consistent, action-based alternative to ad hoc dark energy parametrizations, linking observable features (e.g., w(z) evolution, phantom crossing) to fundamental modifications in the matter-gravity sector.

Abstract: We develop a new parametrization for generic departures from the $Λ$CDM model based on the Markov-Mukhanov action with non-minimal matter-gravity coupling. In this approach the cosmological constant $Λ$ and the gravitational coupling $G$ become dynamical with their values determined by the explicit form of the matter-gravity coupling. We use the framework to build a simple phenomenological model where dynamical dark energy is naturally obtained as a consequence of the departure of the theory from classical General Relativity at low densities. We show how the model's free parameters can be constrained from observational data and interpret the tentative observations of `phantom crossing' by the DESI collaboration in view of the proposed formalism.

</details>


### [57] [Joule Thomson expansion of symmergent black holes in extended phase space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24400)
*Saheb Soroushfar,Sayyed Mehrab Ramezani*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies the Joule-Thomson expansion of symmergent black holes in extended thermodynamic phase space, finding that while the inversion curve is independent of the symmergent parameter α̂, isenthalpic curves and thermodynamic trajectories depend on it; results are compatible with AdS/CFT holography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how symmergent gravity—a framework generating Einstein gravity plus corrections from heavy fields—affects black hole thermodynamics, specifically the Joule-Thomson (JT) expansion, within an extended phase-space formalism where pressure is a thermodynamic variable.

Method: Extended black hole thermodynamics: treating cosmological constant as dynamical pressure P; computing JT coefficient μ_JT = (∂T/∂P)_H; deriving inversion curve (μ_JT = 0) and isenthalpic curves using symmergent entropy S(α̂), which modifies enthalpy H and thermodynamic volume V; analyzing asymptotic structure and holographic interpretation.

Result: The JT coefficient depends only on the dimensionless combination 8πPr₊², yielding an inversion curve P_i = (π/2) I_i² independent of α̂; isenthalpic curves retain α̂-dependence; explicit expressions for inversion pressure and temperature obtained; cooling (P > P_i) vs. heating (P < P_i) regimes identified; asymptotically AdS-like geometry for P > 0 enables holographic interpretation as isenthalpic process in dual field theory.

Conclusion: Symmergent corrections alter thermodynamic trajectories (isenthalpic curves) but leave the JT inversion boundary invariant; this reveals a robust geometric feature of the inversion surface under symmergent deformations, supporting consistency of extended thermodynamics in emergent gravity frameworks and enabling holographic thermodynamic interpretations.

Abstract: We investigate the Joule Thomson expansion of a symmergent black hole within an extended phase-space formalism in which the thermodynamic pressure is introduced as an independent variable. The symmergent entropy contains the parameter alpha-hat, which modifies the enthalpy and thermodynamic volume relative to their standard forms. We show that the Joule Thomson coefficient depends only on the combination 8piP*r_+^2, leading to an inversion curve P_i = (pi/2) * I_i^2 that is independent of alpha-hat. The isenthalpic curves, however, retain a parametric dependence on alpha-hat, indicating that the symmergent sector affects the thermodynamic trajectories but not the inversion boundary itself. The inversion pressure and temperature are derived explicitly, and the cooling regime P > P_i is distinguished from the heating regime P < P_i. Since the effective geometry is asymptotically AdS-like for positive pressure, the results also admit a holographic reading, in which the Joule Thomson expansion corresponds to an isenthalpic process in the dual field theory. The results provide a consistent thermodynamic framework for symmergent black holes and clarify the role of the symmergent parameters in the Joule Thomson process.

</details>


### [58] [Do gravitational waves assist the genuine tripartite entanglement harvesting?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24428)
*Xiang-Yue Yu,Xiao-Li Huang,Shu-Min Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how gravitational waves affect genuine tripartite entanglement harvesting by three Unruh-DeWitt detectors coupled to a scalar field, revealing nonmonotonic, frequency- and separation-dependent responses—more sensitive than bipartite entanglement—suggesting its utility as a quantum probe for gravitational waves.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether genuine tripartite entanglement can serve as a more sensitive quantum probe of spacetime perturbations induced by gravitational waves compared to bipartite entanglement.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of entanglement harvesting by three linearly arranged Unruh-DeWitt detectors locally coupled to a massless scalar field in a gravitational wave background; evaluation of genuine tripartite entanglement (e.g., via convex roof extended negativity or similar measures) as a function of gravitational wave frequency and detector separation.

Result: Gravitational waves induce a nonmonotonic response in genuine tripartite entanglement—successive suppression, resonance-like enhancement, and renewed suppression with increasing frequency; nonmonotonic dependence on detector separation with local enhancement peaks absent in bipartite case; tripartite entanglement is more sensitive than bipartite to both frequency and spatial variations.

Conclusion: Genuine tripartite entanglement exhibits heightened sensitivity to gravitational wave perturbations in both frequency and spatial domains, making it a promising quantum probe for detecting and characterizing gravitational waves.

Abstract: We investigate the harvesting of genuine tripartite entanglement by three linearly arranged Unruh-DeWitt detectors locally coupled to a massless scalar field in a gravitational wave background. We show that gravitational waves play a dual role in entanglement harvesting, either enhancing or suppressing the harvested entanglement depending on the system parameters. In particular, genuine tripartite entanglement exhibits a pronounced nonmonotonic response to the gravitational wave frequency, characterized by successive suppression, enhancement resembling resonance, and renewed suppression, while approaching its corresponding value in Minkowski spacetime in the regime of high frequencies. Its dependence on the detector separation is also nonmonotonic, displaying local enhancement peaks that are absent in the corresponding bipartite entanglement behavior. Compared with bipartite entanglement, genuine tripartite entanglement therefore exhibits a higher sensitivity to gravitational wave perturbations in both the frequency and spatial domains. These results suggest that genuine tripartite entanglement may provide a sensitive quantum probe of spacetime perturbations induced by gravitational waves.

</details>


### [59] [Stationary Rotating Geometries Associated with Nonsingular Pulsating Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28850)
*A. D. Souza,F. A. Brito,J. Spinelly*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a rotating black hole solution by extending a singularity-avoiding static geometry (inspired by LQC/braneworld models) via the Newman–Janis algorithm, recovering Kerr/Schwarzschild limits as quantum corrections vanish, and analyzes its horizon thermodynamics including Davies-type singularities and extended state-space structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To model quantum-gravitational or braneworld-inspired corrections to black hole interiors that avoid singularities, and to extend such models to physically realistic rotating (Kerr-like) spacetimes for thermodynamic analysis.

Method: Applies the Azreg-Aïnou variant of the Newman–Janis algorithm to rotate a static exterior geometry (Gao–Lu–Shen–Faraoni) coupled to a comoving bouncing interior; computes horizon properties (geometry, angular velocity, surface gravity, Hawking temperature); performs fixed-angular-momentum thermodynamic analysis including identification of Davies-type singularities and derivation of an extended thermodynamic state-space relation.

Result: A new stationary, axisymmetric rotating spacetime metric is obtained; its event horizon structure and thermodynamic quantities reduce smoothly to Kerr and Schwarzschild in the classical limit (l → 0); thermal response reveals Davies-type singularities; an extended thermodynamic state-space relation is formulated.

Conclusion: Singularity-avoiding static models admit physically consistent rotating generalizations with well-behaved classical limits and rich thermodynamic structure—including critical behavior encoded in Davies points—supporting their viability as effective semiclassical black hole models.

Abstract: Since the first exact solutions of General Relativity (GR) were obtained, it became clear that the theory predicts a new class of compact objects: black holes. The Hawking--Penrose singularity theorems show that, under appropriate assumptions, gravitational collapse in GR leads to singular behavior. Motivated by the expectation that such regimes require physics beyond classical GR, effective approaches inspired by Loop Quantum Cosmology (LQC) and braneworld scenarios introduce high-density corrections. In this work we consider the static exterior geometry introduced by Gao, Lu, Shen, and Faraoni, associated with a comoving interior model in which the singularity is avoided through a sequence of collapse and bounce phases. Using the Newman--Janis algorithm in the Azreg-Aïnou formulation, we construct a stationary rotating exterior extension of this geometry. The resulting line element describes a stationary axisymmetric rotating spacetime whose horizon geometry, angular velocity, surface gravity, and Hawking temperature recover the Kerr and Schwarzschild limits when the quantum/braneworld correction becomes negligible ($l\to 0$). We further analyze the thermal response at fixed rotational state-space parameter, identify Davies-type singular points, and formulate an extended thermodynamic state-space relation.

</details>


### [60] [How Universal Is the Black Hole Zeroth Law?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24648)
*Rajes Ghosh,Sudipta Sarkar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper shows that the black hole zeroth law—constancy of surface gravity on a stationary Killing horizon—is not universally enforced across gravitational theories; instead, different theories realize it via distinct horizon-projection components of their field equations (e.g., mixed vs. null-null), and provides the first non-perturbative, exact proof of the zeroth law in generalized quadratic gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The status of the black hole zeroth law beyond general relativity is poorly understood; prior results are limited to specific theories (GR, f(R), Lovelock) and often rely on perturbative assumptions—motivating a systematic investigation into how modified gravity theories enforce surface gravity constancy.

Method: Analyzing horizon-projected field equations across multiple gravitational theories—including general relativity, f(R), Lovelock, and generalized quadratic gravity—the authors identify which component (e.g., mixed null-transverse vs. null-null) enforces constancy of surface gravity, and use this to construct a non-perturbative proof of the zeroth law in generalized quadratic gravity.

Result: The zeroth law holds in generalized quadratic gravity, proven exactly (non-perturbatively); crucially, its enforcement mechanism differs qualitatively from GR-type theories—it arises from the null-null component, not the mixed component, revealing theory-dependent thermodynamic structure.

Conclusion: The zeroth law is not governed by a universal mechanism but serves as a sharp diagnostic for classifying gravitational theories by how their field equations constrain horizon geometry—highlighting its role as a probe of intrinsic thermodynamic structure in modified gravity.

Abstract: The black hole zeroth law asserts the thermal equilibrium of a stationary Killing horizon, yet its status beyond general relativity remains limited. We demonstrate that its validity is not governed by a universal mechanism: distinct gravitational theories enforce constancy of the surface gravity through different horizon-projections of their field equations. In general relativity, $f(R)$-gravity, and Lovelock theories, the zeroth law is known to follow from the mixed (null-transverse) horizon equations. By contrast, we show that in non-Lovelock theories the zeroth law is enforced by qualitatively different mechanisms. In generalized quadratic gravity, for instance, the null-null component of the field equations dictates its validity. Exploiting this novel diagnostic, we obtain the first exact proof of the zeroth law in the broader landscape of higher-curvature gravity, without relying on a perturbative expansion around general relativity. Our result establishes the zeroth law as a sharp probe of the underlying thermodynamic structure of gravity, and points toward a classification of modified theories according to the mechanism responsible for its validity.

</details>


### [61] [Formalizing a unified dynamical system framework for $f(R)$ and $f(Q)$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24733)
*Saikat Chakraborty*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a unified, auxiliary-variable-free dynamical systems framework for f(R) and coincident gauge f(Q) gravity using three dimensionless variable sets—cosmographic parameters, dark energy equation of state w(N) with derivatives and Ω_m, and theory-space parameters {m_i}—and demonstrates three equivalent closure strategies with explicit examples showing distinct dynamical behaviors between f(R) and f(Q), especially regarding GR as a past attractor and trajectory sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of traditional Hubble-normalized frameworks and auxiliary-variable dependence in dynamical systems analysis of modified gravity, enabling direct, fair, and transparent comparison of f(R) and f(Q) cosmologies.

Method: Constructs a unified autonomous dynamical system using three mutually compatible dimensionless variable sets; imposes closure via (1) specifying the theory function f, (2) prescribing cosmological evolution (e.g., j(z)=1), or (3) prescribing w(N) evolution (e.g., w(z)=-1); analyzes phase space structure, fixed points, heteroclinic orbits, and attractor properties for Hu-Sawicki f(R) and f(Q)=-2Λ+Q+β√(-Q).

Result: f(Q) generically admits a robust heteroclinic orbit from GR-matter domination to late-time de Sitter, with GR acting as a past attractor; f(R) exhibits initial-condition-sensitive dynamics and lacks GR as a past attractor under same closures; both theories can kinematically (j=1) and dynamically (w=-1) mimic ΛCDM, but their underlying phase-space structures differ fundamentally.

Conclusion: The unified formalism reveals intrinsic dynamical distinctions between f(R) and f(Q) gravity: f(Q) is more structurally stable and GR-aligned in early-time cosmology, while f(R) is more sensitive and less predictive without fine-tuned initial conditions—highlighting that geometric equivalence does not imply dynamical equivalence.

Abstract: Going beyond the traditional Hubble-normalized framework, we formalize a unified dynamical system formulation for $f(R)$ and coincident gauge $f(Q)$ gravity using three distinct sets of dimensionless variables: (1) kinematical cosmographic parameters, (2) the dark energy equation of state parameter $w$ (with its $N=\ln a$ derivatives) and matter density abundance $Ω_m$, and (3) theory space parameters $\{m_i\}$ characterizing the shape of the theory function. Our formulation is free of auxiliary variables, closing via three equivalent ways: (1) specifying a theory, (2) requiring a specific cosmological evolution, or (3) requiring a specific dark energy equation of state evolution.
  Each closure strategy is illustrated with explicit examples. The theory closure approach studies a case of Hu-Sawicki $f(R)$ and $f(Q)=-2Λ+Q+β\sqrt{-Q}$ models, where traditional formalisms fail. A heteroclinic trajectory from a GR-matter-dominated epoch to a late-time de-Sitter epoch is general for $f(Q)$, but sensitive to initial conditions for $f(R)$. The cosmographic and equation-of-state closure approaches compare $f(R)$ and $f(Q)$ gravities kinematically equivalent to $Λ$CDM ($j(z)=1$) and dynamically equivalent to $Λ$CDM ($w(z)=-1$). In both, GR acts as a cosmological past attractor for $f(Q)$ gravity, but not for $f(R)$.

</details>


### [62] [Constraining the geometry of rotating black holes with eikonal QNMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24824)
*Ciro De Simone,Sebastian H. Völkel,Kostas D. Kokkotas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares metric-specific (global parametrization) and metric-agnostic (local light-ring quantities) Bayesian models for inferring black hole properties from quasinormal modes in gravitational-wave ringdowns; it finds that uniform priors on global parameters induce skewed priors on local metric quantities, multi-parameter analyses yield robust but degenerate constraints, and both models are consistent with Kerr for GW150914.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess how theory-dependent modeling choices—specifically, global metric parametrizations versus local light-ring descriptors—affect inference of black hole spacetime properties from quasinormal mode observations, especially under potential deviations from general relativity.

Method: Calibrated eikonal method linking quasinormal mode shifts to local metric properties at the light ring; Bayesian inference comparing Konoplya-Rezzolla-Zhidenko (KRZ) global parametrization against a local model using metric functions and their radial derivatives at the light ring; one-parameter and multi-parameter analyses; prior mapping analysis; application to simulated and real (GW150914) ringdown data.

Result: Uniform priors on KRZ coefficients induce nonuniform, skewed priors on local metric quantities; one-parameter analyses fail to recover injected local deviations and bias global reconstruction in metric-specific case; multi-parameter analyses yield consistent constraints but with strong degeneracies and divergent marginalized posteriors; both models agree with Kerr for GW150914.

Conclusion: Local, metric-agnostic modeling provides more interpretable and robust inference for testing general relativity via ringdowns, though multi-parameter flexibility introduces practical degeneracies; prior choice and parameterization significantly impact physical interpretation, urging caution in theory-agnostic tests.

Abstract: Quasinormal modes of black holes provide a central avenue for confronting general relativity with gravitational-wave observations from the ringdown stage of compact binary coalescence. However, inferring black-hole properties or the background spacetime requires theory-dependent input. In this work, we connect a calibrated eikonal method, which relates quasinormal mode shifts to local metric properties near the light ring, with the underlying rotating black hole metric. Using Bayesian inference, we compare a metric-specific model based on the global Konoplya-Rezzolla-Zhidenko parametrization with a metric-agnostic approach based on local values and radial derivatives of the metric functions at the light ring. For each model, we perform both one-parameter and simultaneous multi-parameter analyses and quantify how their priors map onto the same local quantities. We find that uniform priors on the metric-specific coefficients can induce strongly nonuniform and skewed priors on these local metric quantities. For an injection compatible with general relativity, both models remain consistent with Kerr and yield similar posteriors for the orbital frequency and Lyapunov exponent despite their different induced priors. For injections representing deviations from general relativity, one-parameter analyses can fail to recover the injected local deviations and, in the metric-specific case, can bias the global metric reconstruction. The multi-parameter analyses of both models yield consistent constraints, suggesting sufficient robustness. However, they introduce substantial degeneracies and differences in marginalized parameters, demonstrating practical limitations when considering more flexible models. Finally, we apply the framework to approximate posterior information from the ringdown analysis of GW250114 and find that both models are consistent with the Kerr metric in general relativity.

</details>


### [63] [Boson Star Factory: Past, Present, and Future](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24913)
*Romain Gervalle,Jorge Filipe Mónico Delgado,Etevaldo dos Santos Costa Filho,Carlos Herdeiro,Víctor Jaramillo,Eugen Radu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares three numerical methods (finite differences, finite elements, spectral methods) for solving the Einstein-Klein-Gordon equations for non-spherical boson stars—specifically rotating and static dipolar configurations—and establishes high-precision benchmark solutions; it also demonstrates a proof-of-concept application of physics-informed neural networks to rotating Q-balls.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the lack of systematic comparison among different numerical approaches for constructing non-spherical boson stars, which are important horizonless compact objects in General Relativity.

Method: Three independent numerical codes—FIDISOL/CADSOL (finite differences), FreeFem (finite elements), and Kadath (spectral methods)—are applied to compute rotating and static dipolar boson star solutions; accuracy is assessed via multiple diagnostics; runtime and physical observables are benchmarked; additionally, physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) are used to construct a rotating Q-ball solution.

Result: The three solvers agree on global observables to ~8 significant digits; precise benchmark values for key physical quantities (e.g., mass, angular momentum, central field amplitude) are provided; runtime comparisons are given; PINNs successfully reproduce a rotating Q-ball solution.

Conclusion: The study validates cross-method consistency in boson star modeling, provides community reference data for future work, and demonstrates the emerging utility of PINNs in relativistic soliton computations.

Abstract: Boson stars are self-gravitating solitons of the Einstein-Klein-Gordon equations for a massive complex scalar field, and arguably the simplest horizonless compact objects arising in General Relativity. Beyond spherical symmetry, their construction requires solving a system of nonlinear elliptic partial differential equations, a difficult task per se and that each group has traditionally addressed with its own numerical implementation. A systematic comparison of different methods applied to the same physical model has so far been lacking. In this work, we carry out such a comparison for the two simplest non-spherical configurations: rotating boson stars and static dipolar boson stars. We employ three independent codes, based respectively on finite differences (FIDISOL/CADSOL), finite elements (FreeFem), and spectral methods (Kadath), and assess their accuracy through different diagnostics. The three solvers agree on the global observables to typically eight significant digits. We further compare the runtime of each code on identical hardware and provide precise benchmark values of the relevant physical quantities, intended to serve as reference data for future numerical studies of boson stars. As a further proof of concept, we also present the use of physics-informed neural networks to construct a rotating Q-ball solution.

</details>


### [64] [Observational Features of Nonsingular Rotating Black Holes in the Dark-Energy Dominated Universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24926)
*Ramon Torres*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how nonsingular rotating black holes in a universe with evolving dark energy—modeled via a curvature-dependent cosmological term—produce distinct shadow observables compared to Kerr and Kerr–de Sitter black holes; it demonstrates that shadow area and vertical diameter are sensitive probes of the running cosmological term, enabling observational constraints on its effective regularization scale.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Emerging cosmological data (DESI BAO, CMB, SNe Ia) hint at time-evolving dark energy; quantum field theory in curved spacetime and quantum gravity approaches with running couplings motivate a curvature-dependent effective cosmological term; this motivates re-examining black hole phenomenology beyond standard Kerr/dS geometries.

Method: Formulates null geodesics in phase space using a super-Hamiltonian and Mino-time parameter; computes black hole shadows via backward ray tracing from a zero-angular-momentum observer; defines and analyzes standard shadow observables (area, shape, displacement, vertical diameter) for nonsingular rotating black holes with curvature-dependent Λ.

Result: Shadow area and vertical diameter exhibit clear, monotonic dependence on the curvature-dependent cosmological term’s regularization scale; these observables enable discrimination between nonsingular black hole geometries and Kerr/Kerr–dS solutions; joint analysis across multiple black holes (different masses/spins) and strong-field observables allows tight constraints on the effective scale.

Conclusion: The black hole shadow provides a viable astrophysical probe of evolving dark energy at quantum-gravitational scales; measurable deviations in shadow morphology—especially area and vertical extent—can constrain or detect the effective regularization scale of a running cosmological term, bridging cosmology and strong-field gravity tests.

Abstract: Recent DESI baryon acoustic oscillation measurements, combined with cosmic microwave background and type-Ia supernova data, suggest that dark energy may evolve with cosmic time. Along a cosmological background, an evolving dark-energy density can be expressed locally as a function of a monotonic curvature invariant, motivating an effective curvature-dependent cosmological term. Moreover, quantum field theory in curved spacetime and approaches to quantum gravity with running gravitational couplings provide complementary motivations for exploring such a term.
  Here we study how the phenomenology of nonsingular rotating black holes embedded in a Universe dominated by dark energy can differ substantially from that of the Kerr and Kerr--de Sitter geometries. The problem is directly formulated in phase space using a null geodesic super-Hamiltonian and a Mino-type parameter. The shadows of the black holes are calculated by backward ray tracing from a zero-angular-momentum observer. We define and analyze a comprehensive set of standard shadow observables, characterizing the size, shape, and displacement of the shadow. We show that the area of the shadow and its vertical diameter provide particularly clear signatures of a running cosmological term. In this way, joint analyses of multiple strong-field observables and of several black holes with different masses and spins could provide a realistic route to tightly constraining, or potentially measuring, the effective regularization scale behind the running of the cosmological term and statistically distinguishing the non-singular black hole geometry from Kerr/Kerr-dS solutions.

</details>


### [65] [Analytical estimates of core optics stray light noise and design requirements for ground based gravitational wave detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25003)
*M. Andrés-Carcasona,M. Evans*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces an analytical framework to translate detector sensitivity requirements into stray light constraints for the core optics of ground-based gravitational wave interferometers, enabling early-stage conservative design specifications before full optical layout completion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish conservative, pre-layout stray light requirements for gravitational wave interferometers—critical for mitigating noise from scattered and ghost light that could degrade detector sensitivity.

Method: Derives analytical estimates for multiple scattering regimes (e.g., surface scatter, edge diffraction), extends analysis to planar baffle arrays, and formulates analogous requirements for deterministic ghost beams intercepted by beam dumps.

Result: Obtains compact, physics-based expressions for stray light budgeting; successfully applied to Cosmic Explorer to derive concrete stray light requirements for next-generation detectors.

Conclusion: The framework provides a practical, early-design toolset for stray light control in current and future gravitational wave observatories, bridging sensitivity targets and optical hardware specifications.

Abstract: We present an analytical framework to translate detector sensitivity into stray light requirements for the core optics region of ground based gravitational wave interferometers. We derive analytical estimates for several relevant scattering regimes, extend the treatment to collections of planar baffles, and obtain an analogous requirement for deterministic ghost beams intercepted by beam dumps. The framework is intended to provide conservative design requirements before a complete optical layout is available. We apply it to Cosmic Explorer to show how these estimates can be used to derive requirements for next generation detectors. The resulting expressions provide a compact set of tools for stray light budgeting and early design studies in current and future gravitational wave detectors.

</details>

### [66] [Microcanonical Operator Spectrum, BPS Chaos and Free Probability Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22422)
*Alexandre Belin,Yichao Fu,Federico La Rocca*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the eigenspectrum of simple operators projected onto microcanonical energy windows in chaotic quantum many-body systems, proving that—under Haar-random orientation assumptions—it follows Gaussian random matrix statistics, using both direct probabilistic and Free Probability methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To refine the Eigenstate Thermalization Hypothesis by moving beyond statistical moments of matrix elements to a full spectral characterization—especially crucial in degenerate Hamiltonian spectra where moments alone are insufficient.

Method: Assumes Haar-random relative orientation between eigenbases of the observable and Hamiltonian; derives the induced probability distribution of the projected operator’s spectrum; applies tools from Free Probability Theory (e.g., asymptotic freeness, spectral convolution) to establish convergence to Gaussian (Wigner) spectral statistics.

Result: The eigenvalue distribution of a simple operator projected onto a narrow microcanonical energy window converges to the semicircle law—i.e., matches the spectral density of a Gaussian Orthogonal/Unitary Ensemble—provided the window contains sufficiently many eigenstates and the orientation is Haar-random.

Conclusion: Gaussian random matrix statistics universally govern the microcanonically projected spectra of generic simple operators in chaotic systems, strengthening the foundations of ETH and offering new diagnostics for quantum chaos—including BPS-protected regimes—via spectral shape rather than moment-based tests.

Abstract: Matrix elements of simple operators are central to thermalization in chaotic quantum many-body systems. Their moments are often described statistically through the Eigenstate Thermalization Hypothesis. In this paper, we study a more refined characterization of these matrix elements: the eigenspectrum of simple operators once projected to microcanonical energy windows. The spectrum encodes all moments of the matrix elements at once, and is particularly relevant when the spectrum of the Hamiltonian is degenerate. Assuming that the eigenbasis of the simple operator is Haar-randomly oriented with respect to that of the Hamiltonian, we prove that the spectrum of an operator projected to a small microcanonical window obeys Gaussian random matrix statistics. We do this directly by studying the probability distribution inherited from the random orientation, as well as using tools from Free Probability Theory. We discuss the implications for BPS chaos and more generally for eigenstate thermalization.

</details>


### [67] [Tripartite Form Universality in Holographic Entropy Inequalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22504)
*Veronika E. Hubeny,Yu Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper conjectures and provides evidence that all holographic entropy inequalities can be expressed in a 'tripartite form'—a sum of tripartite and conditional tripartite information terms with unit coefficients—and explicitly constructs this form for two known infinite families, deriving new identities and proving general structural properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether all holographic entropy inequalities (beyond subadditivity) can be uniformly recast in the compact 'tripartite form' introduced in arXiv:2309.06296, which uses only unit-coefficient tripartite and conditional tripartite information terms.

Method: The authors prove that two infinite families of holographic entropy inequalities (from arXiv:2309.15145) admit exact tripartite form representations; they develop systematic rewriting procedures, derive novel information-theoretic identities, and use these to establish general structural theorems about any inequality in tripartite form.

Result: All members of the two infinite families are explicitly written in tripartite form; several new linear identities among conditional mutual informations are derived; and universal structural properties—including monotonicity under merging, nesting, and positivity constraints—are proven for any holographic entropy inequality expressible in this form.

Conclusion: The tripartite form is not merely a convenient packaging but appears universally applicable to known holographic entropy inequalities; its utility is demonstrated both concretely (via explicit constructions) and abstractly (via derived structural theorems), supporting the conjecture of full generality.

Abstract: To elucidate the meaning of holographic entropy inequalities (beyond subadditivity) which characterize the entanglement structure of geometric states in holography, arXiv:2309.06296 proposed the "tripartite form" for these inequalities, consisting of tripartite information and conditional tripartite information terms with unit coefficients. While this provides a compact and useful packaging of the inequalities, it is not a priori guaranteed that all inequalities can be recast in this form. Here we conjecture that they can, and present substantial evidence, by proving that the two known infinite families of holographic entropy inequalities found in arXiv:2309.15145 can indeed be written in the tripartite form. This is significant because such recasting is particularly nontrivial for these families. Apart from providing the explicit tripartite form expressions for every member of these two infinite families, we detail how we arrived at them, in the process deriving several useful identities which may serve as stepping stones to formulate further repackaging of the corresponding information quantities. We also illustrate the power of the tripartite form by proving a number of structural properties satisfied by any holographic entropy inequality.

</details>


### [68] [External Sources in Pseudo-Quantum Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22526)
*L. H. C. Borges,A. A. Nogueira,F. A. Barone*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies interactions between stationary point-like sources—including electric charges, Dirac points, and generalized topological sources—in Pseudo-Quantum Electrodynamics (PQED), revealing anisotropic, non-central forces and torques absent in standard planar Maxwell theory due to PQED’s nonlocality; results suggest applications in modeling topological defects and anisotropy in 2D condensed matter systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how the nonlocal structure of Pseudo-Quantum Electrodynamics (PQED) modifies electromagnetic interactions among stationary point-like and topological sources in two spatial dimensions—particularly beyond conventional planar Maxwell electrodynamics.

Method: Analytical computation of interaction energies, forces, torques, and electromagnetic field configurations for various source configurations (electric charges, Dirac points, generalized topological sources) within the PQED framework; comparative analysis with planar Maxwell electrodynamics.

Result: Topological sources induce intrinsically anisotropic and non-central interactions in PQED, generating finite torques absent in local planar Maxwell theory; electromagnetic fields and interaction potentials exhibit directional dependence reflecting PQED's nonlocal kernel.

Conclusion: PQED provides a physically distinct description of planar electrodynamics where topological and geometric features of sources manifest directly in measurable mechanical effects (e.g., torque), enabling effective modeling of anisotropic and topologically nontrivial phenomena in 2D condensed matter systems.

Abstract: In this letter, we investigate the interaction between stationary point-like external sources within the framework of Pseudo-Quantum Electrodynamics (PQED). Besides ordinary electric charges, we consider configurations involving Dirac points and generalized topological sources. For the configurations analyzed, we compute the corresponding interaction energies, forces, torques, and electromagnetic field configurations. Furthermore, we compare the results obtained in PQED with those arising in ordinary planar Maxwell electrodynamics, showing that the nonlocal character of PQED gives rise to physical effects with no counterpart in the conventional local planar theory. In particular, our results show that topological sources induce intrinsically anisotropic and non-central interactions, leading to the emergence of nontrivial torques between the interacting objects. The directional character of these interactions suggests possible applications in the effective description of topological defects and anisotropic phenomena in planar condensed matter systems.

</details>


### [69] [Phase Transitions at More Unusual Values of Theta](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22613)
*Dhruv Aldas,Hitoshi Murayama,Bea Noether,Digvijay Roy Varier*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the θ-dependence of vacuum energy in a family of QCD-like theories derived from deformed N=2 SU(N_c) gauge theories via AMSB, using a simpler N=1 effective field theory approach; it identifies θ-induced phase transitions at both fractional and irrational multiples of π.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a simpler, more broadly applicable method—based on N=1 EFTs—for analyzing θ dependence and vacuum structure in supersymmetric QCD-like theories, circumventing the technical limitations of the full N=2 machinery.

Method: Constructs N=1 effective field theories from N=2 SU(N_c) gauge theory with N_f fundamental hypermultiplets, deformed by anomaly-mediated supersymmetry breaking (AMSB); computes vacuum energy as a function of θ using exact N=1 techniques (e.g., holomorphy, instanton calculus, and confining vacua analysis).

Result: Reproduces prior N=2 results with greater simplicity; extends analysis to infinitely many previously intractable theories; identifies θ-driven phase transitions occurring at both rational (fractional) and irrational values of θ/π.

Conclusion: The N=1 AMSB-deformed EFT framework provides a powerful, scalable tool for studying topological θ dependence and vacuum structure in strongly coupled gauge theories, revealing richer phase structure—including irrational θ transitions—than previously anticipated.

Abstract: We calculate the $θ$ dependence of the vacuum energy in an infinite family of cousins of QCD, where the vacuum structure can be analyzed exactly. These theories are $\mathcal{N}=1$ EFTs that descend from $\mathcal{N}=2$ $SU(N_c)$ gauge theory with $N_f$ flavors of fundamental hypermultiplets, that are then deformed by anomaly mediated supersymmetry breaking (AMSB). We reproduce results found in previous work that used the full $\mathcal{N}=2$ machinery but via a much simpler $\mathcal{N}=1$ method. Our method easily extends to infinitely many more theories for which the full $\mathcal{N}=2$ approach becomes intractable. We identify values of $θ$ for which the theory undergoes a phase transition, and find that they can occur at both fractional and irrational values of $θ/π$.

</details>


### [70] [Semiclassical reconstruction of the Toda pole lattice via analytic continuation of the Probabilistic Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22717)
*Jacques Distler,Bahaa Elshimy*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs Toda field theory correlators probabilistically via Coulomb gas representation, interprets primary n-point functions as Gaussian moments, derives convergence and integrability constraints (extending Seiberg bounds), uses Mellin-Barnes analytic continuation to obtain meromorphic local integrability divisors, and shows that in the semiclassical limit, W_n-closure selects Virasoro-like submodules whose integrability conditions recover Toda charge pole lattices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous probabilistic foundation for Toda field theory correlators, extend the domain of validity of Seiberg-type bounds, and clarify the structure of charge constraints and semiclassical limits using tools from probability theory and complex analysis.

Method: Probabilistic reinterpretation of Toda n-point functions as Gaussian moments; derivation of L^2/L^p convergence and local integrability conditions; analytic continuation via Mellin-Barnes representation; study of meromorphic structure of integrability divisors; semiclassical (b→0) analysis combined with W_n-symmetry closure conditions.

Result: Extended region of convergence for Toda correlators; probabilistic derivation of generalized Seiberg bounds; meromorphic local integrability divisors emerging from analytic continuation; reconstruction of Liouville charge pole lattice in b→0 limit; identification of rank-one Virasoro-like submodules within A_n Toda theory under W_n-closure, with associated Liouville-type integrability divisors recovering full Toda charge pole lattices.

Conclusion: The probabilistic Coulomb gas framework provides a unified, rigorous, and geometrically transparent approach to Toda correlators—clarifying convergence, analytic structure, and symmetry reduction—and bridges Toda and Liouville theories through semiclassical and divisor-based analysis.

Abstract: We develop a probabilistic construction of Toda field theory correlators from the Coulomb gas representation. Reinterpreting $n$-point functions of primaries as $w$-moments of random variables with respect to a Gaussian measure, we derive constraints on the Toda momenta and coupling constant. $L^2$ and $L^p$ convergence conditions for the associated random variables, triviality constraints on the correlators, and local integrability conditions for the cross-moments are derived. These constraints define an extended region of convergence for the Toda correlators and provide a systematic, probabilistic interpretation of the Seiberg bounds. We proceed to analytically continue the moment indices by employing a Mellin-Barnes representation of the random variables. The local convergence boundaries become meromorphic local integrability divisors, and, in the semiclassical $b\to0$ limit, these divisors reconstruct the Liouville charge pole lattice. For $A_n$ Toda theory, we show that natural $W_n$-closure conditions in this semiclassical limit select rank-one, Virasoro-like submodules inside the full Toda theory. On these slices, the scalar integrability conditions reduce to Liouville-like divisors and recover the corresponding Toda charge pole lattices.

</details>


### [71] [Toroidal Charged AdS Branes and Deformed Toda Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23079)
*Sangheon Yun*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives Toda-type equations for magnetically charged p-branes in AdS spacetime, constructs exact and perturbative black brane solutions (including sinh and dilatonic variants), computes thermodynamic quantities via holographic renormalization, and shows that dilaton coupling modifies the entropy–temperature scaling while preserving the first law.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and characterize static, toroidal, magnetically charged black p-branes in AdS spacetimes with controlled dilaton coupling, and to understand how scalar hair affects thermodynamics and dual CFT properties.

Method: Reduction of p-brane equations of motion to coupled 1D Toda systems; exact integration in pure Maxwell case; perturbative expansion (in ε = a²) of dilatonic system yielding Pöschl–Teller equations; holographic renormalization to extract conserved charges and stress tensor; thermodynamic analysis via surface gravity and horizon geometry.

Result: Exact sinh-solution for AdS_{p+2}×Tⁿ black branes; dilatonic generalization with running torus modulus and nontrivial dilaton; O(a²) backreacted metric and dilaton; explicit Hawking temperature, entropy density, and mass density; verification of first law dℳ = T_H ds; shift of Stefan–Boltzmann exponent to p_eff = p + a²/2; decoupling of normalizable dilaton hair from entropy.

Conclusion: Magnetic p-branes in AdS admit integrable reductions enabling exact and controlled perturbative solutions; dilaton coupling deforms thermodynamic scaling laws but preserves thermodynamic consistency and conformal structure of the dual field theory.

Abstract: We show that the equations of motion for magnetically charged $p$-branes in $(n+p+2)$-dimensional AdS reduce to coupled one-dimensional Toda-type equations, and use this structure to construct static toroidal black branes with $T^n$ symmetry and $p$ translation-invariant directions. In the pure-Maxwell case we obtain an exact $\sinh$ solution--a $(p+2)$-dimensional black brane times an $n$-torus, asymptotically $\mathrm{AdS}_{p+2}\times T^n$--whose dilatonic analogue, with a scalar coupled through $e^{aφ}$, has a running torus modulus and a non-trivial dilaton. A perturbative expansion reduces the linearized system to a pair of Pöschl-Teller equations, one elementary and one solved by associated Legendre functions of non-integer degree, so that the general solution is non-elementary; at weak coupling $ε=a^2$ we construct the $O(a^2)$ backreaction explicitly. From the surface gravity, horizon area, and holographic renormalization we obtain the Hawking temperature, entropy density, and mass density, verifying the first law $d\mathcal{M}=T_H\,ds$ and the traceless conformal stress tensor of the dual $(p+1)$-dimensional theory. At $O(a^2)$ the first law persists, but the dilaton source shifts the Stefan-Boltzmann exponent to $p_{\rm eff}=p+a^2/2$, breaking the clean power law $s\propto T_H^p$ while the normalizable dilaton hair decouples from the entropy.

</details>


### [72] [Homogeneous Non Symmetric Special Kähler Geometries as Broken Isometry Metrics on Symmetric CV {K}ähler Manifolds:a new tool for $r=2$ CaNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23395)
*Pietro Fré,Mario Trigiante,Antoine Van Proeyen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a detailed Lie-algebraic isomorphism between two solvable groups underlying the Calabi-Vesentini symmetric space SO(2,2+p)/SO(2)×SO(2+p) and the homogeneous non-symmetric special Kähler manifold L(−1,p), revealing that both correspond to distinct quadratic forms on the same solvable Lie algebra; it further identifies the full isometry group of L(−1,p) as a non-semisimple subgroup of SO(2,2+p), enabling its realization as a coset Iso_L(−1,p)/[U(1)_L × SO(p)], and proposes applications to enhanced expressivity in Cartan Neural Networks via nonlinear solvable-coordinate transformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the geometric and algebraic relationship between the symmetric Calabi-Vesentini manifold and the non-symmetric special Kähler manifold L(−1,p), and to exploit this correspondence for applications in geometric deep learning—specifically, enhancing neural network expressivity through solvable-group-based nonlinearities.
