Method: Rigorous Lie-theoretic analysis: explicit construction and comparison of solvable Lie algebras S_{2,2+p} and S_{L(−1,p)}; identification of quadratic forms inducing respective Kähler metrics; decomposition of the isometry group Iso_L(−1,p) inside SO(2,2+p); derivation of its action on solvable coordinates of the Calabi-Vesentini manifold; interpretation within Alekseevsky’s classification of homogeneous Kähler manifolds.

Result: Full isomorphism between the solvable models of SO(2,2+p)/SO(2)×SO(2+p) and L(−1,p) is proven; Iso_L(−1,p) is shown to be a non-semisimple subgroup of SO(2,2+p) with isotropy H = U(1)_L × SO(p), where U(1)_L embeds non-canonically into so(2,2+p); the solvable coordinate action is explicitly constructed; this yields a new class of nonlinear transformations for Cartan Neural Networks in the r=2 Tits–Satake universality class.

Conclusion: The manifold L(−1,p) is not merely an isolated example but intrinsically tied to the Calabi-Vesentini symmetric space via shared solvable Lie algebra structure, differing only in metric data; this structural insight enables principled generalization of geometric neural architectures and strengthens the bridge between special Kähler geometry and representation-theoretic machine learning.

Abstract: In this paper we prove in full detail the isomorphism between the solvable group $\mathcal{S}_{2,2+p}$, metric equivalent to the Calabi Vesentini symmetric space $\mathrm{SO(2,2+p)/SO(2)\times SO(2+p)}$, and the solvable group $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{L}(-1,p)}$ supporting the Kähler metric of the homogeneous non symmetric special manifold L$(-1,p)$. From an Alekseevskyan point of view the two spaces simply correspond to two different quadratic forms on the same solvable Lie algebra that we present and compare in detail. Furthermore considering the full group of isometries $\mathrm{Iso}_{\mathrm{L}(-1,p)}$ of L$(-1,p)$ we show that $\mathrm{Iso_{L(-1,p)}}\subset \mathrm{SO}(2,2+p)$ is a non-semisimple subgroup of the simple isometry group of Calabi-Vesentini manifolds. Altogether the Special Kähler manifold L$(-1,p)$ can be seen as a coset manifold $\mathrm{Iso_{L(-1,p)}}/\mathrm{H}$ where $\mathrm{H}=\mathrm{U(1)}_L\times \mathrm{SO(p)}$, the generator of $\mathrm{U(1)}_L$ being in $\so(2,2+p)$, yet not in the canonical $\so(2)\oplus\so(2+p)$ subalgebra. The action of $\mathrm{Iso}_{\mathrm{L}(-1,p)}$ on the solvable coordinates of the CV manifold can be constructed directly. This identification provides a valuable tool for Cartan Neural Networks, introducing additional non linear transformations in every map from one layer to the next one of an $r=2$ Neural Network based on the CV Tits Satake universality class; in perspective, this new tool increases expressivity.

</details>


### [73] [Spin density from second order transport in fluids](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23399)
*Domingo Gallegos,Roi Klein,Amos Yarom*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates spin hydrodynamics in charged conformal fluids in 3+1 dimensions, linking spin density to second-order transport coefficients and anomaly-induced quadratic terms, and validates the relation via free and holographic model computations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how spin degrees of freedom manifest hydrodynamically in charged conformal fluids, especially the role of quantum anomalies in determining spin density beyond standard transport.

Method: Theoretical analysis within relativistic hydrodynamics and conformal field theory; derivation of spin density from symmetry constraints and anomaly inflow; explicit computation in free-field and holographic (AdS/CFT) models.

Result: Spin density is expressed as a linear combination of standard second-order transport coefficients plus an additional term quadratic in gauge and mixed gauge-gravitational anomaly coefficients; consistency confirmed across free and strongly-coupled holographic examples.

Conclusion: Spin density in 3+1D conformal charged fluids carries an unambiguous, anomaly-driven contribution at second order in derivatives, distinguishing it from conventional hydrodynamic currents and highlighting the interplay between spin, anomalies, and dissipation.

Abstract: We study the hydrodynamics of a charged fluid with spin degrees of freedom. We argue that, in a conformal field theory in 3+1 dimensions, the spin density is related to a linear combination of standard second-order transport coefficients, and an additional term quadratic in the gauge- and mixed gauge-gravitational anomaly coefficients. We compute the spin density in several free and holographic theories and compare the results.

</details>


### [74] [Gauge Origami of the Conifold $\times$ $\mathbb{C}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23556)
*Taro Kimura,Go Noshita*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs gauge-origami systems on the toric Calabi–Yau fourfold C×C via brane brick models, unifying several instanton sectors (magnificent-four, tetrahedron-, spiked-instantons) in a framed quiver quantum mechanics; partition functions are defined via Jeffrey–Kirwan residues, fixed points encoded in colored BPS crystals, and general configurations built from elementary D6/D4 framings sharing a fractional D0-brane quiver.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and unify gauge-origami systems—particularly instanton sectors—on toric Calabi–Yau fourfolds, starting from the tractable case C×C, using brane-brick model geometry and quiver quantum mechanics.

Method: Uses brane brick models of the toric CY4 C×C to derive D6- and D4-brane framings from brick matchings and phase boundaries; formulates framed quiver quantum mechanics; defines partition functions via Jeffrey–Kirwan residue prescriptions; identifies fixed points with colored BPS crystals; composes elementary sectors via shared fractional D0-brane quiver.

Result: A unified construction of magnificent-four, tetrahedron-instanton, and spiked-instanton sectors as gauge-origami systems on C×C; explicit residue-based partition functions; combinatorial description of fixed loci via colored BPS crystals; extension mechanism to general toric CY4s.

Conclusion: The brane brick model provides a geometric and combinatorial framework for gauge origami on C×C, unifying key instanton sectors and laying groundwork for generalizations to arbitrary toric Calabi–Yau fourfolds.

Abstract: We construct gauge-origami systems on the toric Calabi--Yau fourfold $\mathcal{C}\times\mathbb{C}$ from its brane brick model. Brick matchings and phase boundaries determine elementary D6- and D4-brane framings, respectively, realizing the magnificent-four, tetrahedron-instanton, and spiked-instanton sectors in a unified framed quiver quantum mechanics. We define their partition functions by Jeffrey--Kirwan residues and describe the fixed points in terms of colored BPS crystals. General gauge-origami configurations are obtained by combining these elementary sectors through their common fractional D0-brane quiver. This construction provides a first step toward gauge origami on general toric Calabi--Yau fourfolds.

</details>


### [75] [Where's Kasner? OPE Signature of Kasner Exponents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23776)
*Nejc Čeplak,Samuel Valach*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a direct link between the power-law exponent of complex-time singularities in holographic thermal two-point functions and the Kasner exponent characterizing the time-direction geometry near the black hole singularity, for black branes with finitely truncated blackening factors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extract non-trivial information about the black hole interior and singularity structure from the analytic properties (e.g., complex-time singularities) of boundary thermal correlation functions in the large-N holographic limit.

Method: Analyzes the asymptotic behavior of OPE data in scalar two-point functions for holographic states dual to black branes whose blackening factors are polynomials in inverse radial coordinate; relates the resulting 'bouncing' singularity exponents to near-singularity Kasner geometry.

Result: A direct quantitative relation is found: the power-law exponent of the complex-time singularities in the OPE data equals the Kasner exponent for the time direction near the black hole singularity, for the specified class of geometries.

Conclusion: The analytic structure of boundary thermal correlators encodes precise geometric data of the black hole interior singularity, providing a concrete holographic probe of singularity physics; the relation holds for geometries with finitely truncated blackening factors and may extend to spatial directions.

Abstract: At large $N$, the analytic structure of holographic thermal correlation functions contains non-trivial information about the near-singularity region of dual black holes. In particular, complex-time singularities can be related to geodesics that probe the black hole interior and bounce off the singularity. We analyse the asymptotic behaviour of holographic OPE data entering scalar two-point functions in states dual to black branes whose blackening factors truncate at finite order in inverse powers of the radial coordinate. For this class of geometries, we find a direct relation between the power-law exponent of these bouncing singularities and the Kasner exponent associated with the time direction near the black hole singularity. We discuss the regime of validity of this relation and comment on possible extensions to the transverse spatial directions.

</details>


### [76] [Projective geometry of second-order supersymmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23938)
*Stevan Cebrian,Mikhail S. Plyushchay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reformulates second-order supersymmetric quantum mechanics using projective geometry, linking supercharges and partner Hamiltonians to a projective connection and an invariant vector field; it connects geometric data to quantum corrections (Schwarzian), dimensional reduction, coupling-constant metamorphosis, and clarifies the role of the Lax–Novikov integral via projective stabilizers in the Lame system.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover a deeper geometric structure underlying anomaly-free second-order SUSY in quantum mechanics—beyond standard superpotential formalism—and unify disparate concepts (Schwarzian correction, coupling-constant metamorphosis, Gelfand-Dikii-Virasoro algebra) via projective differential geometry.

Method: Uses projective differential geometry: constructs a projective connection and a projectively invariant vector field; identifies physical data (superpotentials, central parameter, singlet energies) with geometric invariants (e.g., quadratic invariant of the vector field); analyzes fixed points for smoothness of operators; derives Schwarzian correction from affine similarity transformations; relates geometry to Riemannian dimensional reduction and coupling-constant metamorphosis via adapted coordinates; applies framework to one-gap Lame potentials to link Lax–Novikov integral with projective stabilizers and Bol operators.

Result: Establishes a bijective correspondence between anomaly-free second-order SUSY systems and pairs (projective connection, projectively invariant vector field); shows Schwarzian quantum correction arises naturally from affine gauge choice; demonstrates that dimensional reduction encodes the same affine data via volume measure; proves coupling-constant metamorphosis is coordinate adaptation to the invariant vector field; reveals that the Lax–Novikov integral acts as a first-order projective stabilizer, reducing to the third Bol operator in the reflectionless limit.

Conclusion: Second-order SUSY quantum mechanics possesses an intrinsic projective-geometric structure wherein all physical content—including supercharges, spectral data, quantum corrections, and integrals of motion—is encoded in invariant geometric objects. This unifies previously ad hoc techniques and exposes deep connections to classical projective geometry, Virasoro-related algebras, and integrable systems.

Abstract: We show that anomaly-free second-order supersymmetry (SUSY) in quantum mechanics admits a projective-geometric formulation in terms of a projective connection and a vector field preserving it. Once the physical coordinate and energy origin are fixed, these data determine the partner Hamiltonians and supercharges; the field's quadratic invariant fixes the central parameter and candidate singlet energies. The fixed-point structure of this field yields explicit criteria for smooth continuation of the quantum operators through superpotential zeros. The classically fictitious similarity transformation selects affine data whose covariant factors generate the Schwarzian quantum correction. Dimensional reduction of a Riemannian model reproduces these affine data through the volume measure, while a coordinate adapted to the selected field implements coupling-constant metamorphosis. A one-gap Lame pair illustrates the relation between the SUSY auxiliary geometry and the Gelfand-Dikii-Virasoro structure governing physical solution products. Its Lax-Novikov integral acts on Schrodinger solutions through the first-order action of a projective stabilizer; in the reflectionless limit it becomes proportional, in the physical coordinate, to the auxiliary third Bol operator.

</details>


### [77] [Cassini Ansatz in the Skyrme Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23991)
*N. V. Gerasimeniuk,A. A. Korneev,A. V. Molochkov,O. G. Tkachev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper studies spherical symmetry stretching in the Skyrme model using a 3D generalization of Cassini ovals as an ansatz, finding a linear static energy dependence on longitudinal deformation with a slope σ ≈ 0.14 GeV²—consistent with known string tension values in confinement models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore geometric deformation effects on static energy in the Skyrme model and assess connections to confinement physics via string tension.

Method: Uses a spherically symmetric Skyrme model configuration extended via a three-dimensional generalization of Cassini ovals as a deformation ansatz; analyzes static energy as a function of longitudinal deformation parameter.

Result: Static energy exhibits linear dependence on the longitudinal deformation parameter with slope σ ≈ 0.14 GeV², matching phenomenological string tension estimates from confinement models.

Conclusion: The linear energy–deformation relation supports an effective string-like behavior in the deformed Skyrme soliton, reinforcing its relevance for modeling confinement-related phenomena.

Abstract: We investigate the stretching of a spherically symmetric solution in the Skyrme model. The three-dimensional generalization of the Cassini ovals is considered as ansatz of extension. The linear dependence of static energy on the longitudinal deformation parameter is obtained with a slope $σ\approx 0.14\ \mathrm{ GeV}^2$, which is in good agreement with the string tension known from various models of confinement.

</details>


### [78] [Critical Kasner scaling from Cauchy-horizon collapse near holographic phase transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24227)
*Ling-Long Gao,Yan Liu,Hong-Da Lyu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes scalar-hair bifurcations from nonextremal black holes with Cauchy horizons, showing that the logarithmic divergence of the critical scalar zero mode at the Cauchy horizon determines the exponential collapse rate of the Einstein-Rosen bridge and governs critical scaling of interior geometry—including Kasner exponents—via zero-mode matching, verified numerically in BTZ and RN-AdS₄ models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how continuous scalar-hair formation from stationary black holes with nonextremal Cauchy horizons controls the nonlinear dynamics and critical scaling of black hole interiors, especially near bifurcation points where linear instabilities seed hair but interior evolution depends on horizon-local data and IR completion.

Method: Analytical perturbation theory near the Cauchy horizon; zero-mode matching across horizons; asymptotic analysis of critical scalar perturbations including logarithmic branch structure; comparison with fully nonlinear numerical relativity simulations for BTZ and RN-AdS₄ black branes; extraction and verification of critical exponents and Kasner scalings.

Result: The logarithmic coefficient of the critical scalar zero mode at the Cauchy horizon fixes the exponential bridge collapse rate; zero-mode matching determines both critical exponents β and amplitudes for boundary order parameters and interior Kasner anisotropy (1−pₜ ∝ |λ−λ_c|^{2β}); numerical solutions for RN-AdS₄ confirm predicted Kasner scaling; at low temperature, distinct IR completions yield power-law vs. inverse-logarithmic Kasner scaling despite identical linear critical data.

Conclusion: Black hole interior criticality near finite-temperature bifurcations is governed by zero-mode transmission through the Cauchy horizon, while low-temperature scaling is determined by the nonlinear infrared completion—establishing a sharp dichotomy between horizon-controlled universality and IR-sensitive phenomenology.

Abstract: We study continuous scalar-hair bifurcations from stationary black holes with a nonextremal Cauchy horizon. Although the exterior deformation vanishes at criticality, the critical zero mode generically develops a logarithmic branch at the reference Cauchy horizon, producing a nonuniform critical limit. We show that the coefficient of this logarithm in the scalar perturbation, together with local Cauchy-horizon data, determines the exponential collapse rate of the Einstein-Rosen bridge. If the post-collapse geometry evolves into a Kasner regime, the boundary order-parameter scaling $O\propto|λ-λ_c|^β$ further implies $
  1-p_t\propto|λ-λ_c|^{2β}. $ Crucially, zero-mode matching fixes both the critical exponents and the associated amplitudes, without any fit to the nonlinear interior. We illustrate this mechanism in the transition from rotating BTZ to hairy rotating black holes. For scalarized RN-AdS$_4$ black branes, we quantitatively verify the predicted Kasner scaling using fully nonlinear numerical solutions. At low temperature, two charged models with identical linear critical data instead approach different infrared endpoints, yielding respectively power-law and inverse-logarithmic Kasner scaling. Thus interiors near a finite-temperature bifurcation are controlled by zero-mode transmission, whereas low temperature scaling is governed by the nonlinear infrared completion.

</details>


### [79] [Saddle-Order Universality in Ensemble-Averaged Black Hole Thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24344)
*Ankit Anand,Peng Cheng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a saddle-order proposition for generalized free energy fluctuations in reduced Euclidean black hole ensembles, showing that critical points governed by quartic soft modes yield quarter-temperature scaling—deviating from standard Gaussian (half-temperature) behavior—and verifies it analytically for RN-AdS and Kerr-AdS black holes, unifying their thermodynamics via a quartic normal form.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand non-Gaussian fluctuation behavior in reduced black hole ensembles near criticality, where standard saddle-point (Gaussian) approximations break down due to higher-order stabilizing terms.

Method: Analytical saddle-point analysis of ensemble-averaged generalized free energy; derivation of a saddle-order proposition linking fluctuation scaling to the order of the first stabilizing term; mapping of RN-AdS and Kerr-AdS thermodynamics to a quartic normal form; extension to coupled collective modes with weighted-homogeneous potentials.

Result: Critical points exhibit quartic soft modes leading to T^{1/4} (quarter-temperature) scaling in fluctuations—not the usual T^{1/2}; explicit verification for 4D RN-AdS and Kerr-AdS; construction of a universal scaling function bridging critical and Gaussian regimes along the zero-field one-well direction.

Conclusion: The saddle-order proposition provides a unified framework for describing reduced-ensemble fluctuations across black hole critical phenomena, generalizing beyond quadratic (Gaussian) approximations to encompass higher-order, symmetry-constrained instabilities.

Abstract: We establish a saddle-order proposition for the ensemble-averaged generalized free energy in reduced Euclidean black hole ensembles. Under regularity and stability assumptions, the leading fluctuation contribution is determined by the order of the first stabilizing term around the dominant saddle. An ordinary critical point is governed by a quartic soft mode, giving a quarter-temperature contribution instead of the familiar half-temperature Gaussian result. We verify this analytically for four-dimensional RN-AdS and Kerr-AdS black holes and map their thermodynamic controls to a common quartic normal form, whose scaling function connects the critical and Gaussian regimes along the zero-field one-well direction. The proposition also extends to coupled collective modes with weighted-homogeneous leading potentials. These results provide a unified description of reduced-ensemble fluctuations through black hole criticality.

</details>


### [80] [Miwa deformation of WLZZ matrix models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24360)
*T. Diudin,A. Popolitov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces a universal definition of multivariate orthogonal polynomials for the positive branch of WLZZ matrix models, extending strict superintegrability and Miwa deformation beyond the Gaussian case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the strict superintegrability property and Miwa deformation formulas from the Gaussian case to broader settings in WLZZ matrix models.

Method: Proposes a universal construction of multivariate orthogonal polynomials tailored to the positive branch of WLZZ matrix models.

Result: Successful generalization of strict superintegrability and Miwa deformation formulas beyond the Gaussian case.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides a robust, model-independent approach to multivariate orthogonal polynomials in WLZZ matrix models, enabling broader applicability of integrability structures.

Abstract: In this paper we propose a universal way to define multivariate orthogonal polynomials for the positive branch of WLZZ matrix models. This allows us to generalize from the Gaussian case, previously considered in the literature, the strict superintegrability property and the Miwa deformation formulas.

</details>


### [81] [Spin-curvature effects in slowly rotating black hole spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24364)
*Arpita Jana,Subhajit Mazumdar,Sunandan Gangopadhyay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how the spin of a massive quantum particle interacts with spacetime curvature near a slowly rotating black hole, revealing a curvature-induced quantum force that deviates the particle from geodesic motion; using tetrads in the co-moving frame and WKB/Gordon decomposition, they find the rotational contribution to the force decays faster radially than the Schwarzschild counterpart.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the quantum mechanical effects of spin-curvature coupling for spin-1/2 particles in strong-field curved spacetimes—specifically, how rotation modifies spin-dependent deviations from geodesics beyond the static Schwarzschild case.

Method: Constructs orthonormal tetrads in the particle's co-moving frame in the slow-rotation (Kerr) metric; applies the WKB approximation and Gordon decomposition to the Dirac probability four-current to isolate and compute the relativistic quantum force due to spin-curvature coupling, retaining terms linear in the black hole’s angular momentum parameter $a$.

Result: The spin-curvature force acquires an $a$-dependent correction that scales as $\sim a/r^4$ (or faster decay) compared to the dominant $\sim 1/r^3$ Schwarzschild spin-force term, indicating rotational effects become subdominant at large radii.

Conclusion: Rotation introduces a quantitatively distinct, rapidly decaying contribution to the spin-curvature quantum force—confirming that frame-dragging influences spin dynamics at the quantum level, albeit with diminishing influence at larger distances.

Abstract: We investigate the spin-curvature interaction for a massive spin-1/2 quantum particle in a slowly rotating black hole spacetime. In contrast to the flat Minkowski space, the curved spacetime introduces non-trivial spin connections into the Dirac equation due to the direct curvature-spin interaction. The force arising from this non-trivial spin-curvature coupling makes the particle deviate from its geodesics. We first calculate the tetrads in the co-moving frame of the particle in the slowly rotating black hole background. We then compute the components of the relativistic quantum force, arising from the spin-curvature interaction, using these co-moving tetrads up to linear order in the rotation parameter ($a$) of the black hole. The procedure of computing the force is based on the WKB approximation and the Gordon decomposition method for the Dirac probability four current. Our results show that the rotation parameter modifies the magnitude of the force by introducing a term that decays more rapidly with radial distance than the force magnitude in a static spherically symmetric Schwarzschild black hole spacetime.

</details>


### [82] [T-duality of string charge density/bit threads correspondence in type II supergravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24366)
*Houwen Wu,Shuxuan Ying*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper tests the bit-thread/charge-density correspondence under Abelian T-duality in Type II supergravity, showing entropy-reproducing flows remain invariant when mapping F1-NS5-P ↔ P-NS5-F1 and D1-D5-P ↔ D0-D4-F1, extending the correspondence to wrapped-brane currents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the robustness of the string charge density / bit thread correspondence under Abelian T-duality — a fundamental symmetry of string theory — and to extend it beyond fundamental strings to wrapped brane configurations.

Method: Applies Abelian T-duality along a compact circle in ten-dimensional Type II supergravity backgrounds (F1-NS5-P and D1-D5-P), tracks transformations of sources (F1, D1, etc.), computes projected charge densities, constructs effective radial bit-thread flows (including via bivector contraction for wrapped branes), and verifies invariance of dilaton-weighted transverse area density and maximal integrated flux (i.e., black hole entropy).

Result: T-duality preserves the maximal flux of the bit-thread flow — equal to black hole entropy — despite changes in local geometry, dilaton, source type, and flow norm; specifically, radial F1 maps to radial F1 (in dual frame), while radial D1 maps to D2 wrapped on dual circle, yielding an effective conserved radial flow after contraction with the dual-circle one-form.

Conclusion: The bit-thread / charge-density correspondence is T-duality invariant, confirming its consistency within string duality; moreover, it generalizes naturally from fundamental string currents to wrapped-brane currents that yield effective string-like flows, broadening its applicability in holographic entanglement entropy contexts.

Abstract: In this paper, we study Abelian T-duality of the string charge density/bit threads correspondence in ten-dimensional Type II supergravity. T-duality along the common compact circle maps the Type IIB F1-NS5-P background to a Type IIA P-NS5-F1 background with the winding and momentum charges exchanged. The radial F1 source remains an F1 source, and its projected charge density gives a bit-thread flow whose maximal flux reproduces the black hole entropy. The same duality maps D1-D5-P to D0-D4-F1. In this case, a radial D1 source becomes a D2 source wrapped on the dual circle. Contracting the resulting spatial bivector with the normalized closed one-form on that circle gives a conserved effective radial flow whose maximal flux again reproduces the entropy. Although the local metrics, dilatons, source currents, and flow norms change, the dilaton-weighted transverse area density and the integrated maximal flux are invariant. These results provide a nontrivial T-duality test of the correspondence and extend it from string currents to wrapped-brane currents that reduce to string-like flows.

</details>


### [83] [Dynamical Schwinger Production of Dipole--Antidipole Pairs in a Fractonic Lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24415)
*Celio R. Muniz,R. N. Costa Filho*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies field-induced creation of dipole-antidipole and fractonic excitations from a Mott-insulating ground state in a 1D dipole-conserving Bose–Hubbard chain under a time-dependent rank-two (quadratic) electric field; it identifies distinct excitation manifolds at energies ~2U and 3U, reveals perturbative suppression of odd-harmonic response, predicts and confirms weak-field resonance shifts via second-order processes, and draws analogy to Schwinger pair production—replacing mobile charges with mobile dipoles while preserving fracton immobility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how dipole- and fracton-type excitations can be dynamically generated *de novo*—not from pre-existing excitations—from a clean Mott background in dipole-conserving systems, and to establish analogies with quantum electrodynamic phenomena like Schwinger pair production.

Method: Exact diagonalization of finite chains; perturbative analysis (second-order degenerate perturbation theory) focusing on the |1111⟩ ↔ |0220⟩ channel; spectral analysis of odd-harmonic response; time-evolved dynamics simulations to track dipole–antidipole separation and many-body redistribution.

Result: A dispersive 2U manifold emerges below the compact 3U |030⟩ excitation; its odd-harmonic spectral weight is suppressed as (J/U)²; second-order treatment predicts and numerically confirms weak-field resonance displacement; extended-chain dynamics shows genuine dipole–antidipole separation and leakage into higher-energy sectors.

Conclusion: Field-driven excitation production in dipole-conserving systems realizes a condensed-matter analogue of multiphoton dynamical Schwinger production, where dipole conservation enforces mobility constraints mirroring charge conservation in QED—dipoles act as mobile 'charges', while fractons remain strictly immobile.

Abstract: We investigate field-induced excitation production from the unit-filled Mott background of a one-dimensional dipole-conserving Bose--Hubbard chain driven by a periodic quadratic potential, realizing a time-dependent rank-two electric field. Unlike protocols that manipulate pre-existing dipolar or fractonic excitations, we address their production from an initially Mott-like state without prepared dipolar or fractonic excitations. A single correlated hop directly nucleates the compact $|030\rangle$ configuration at the atomic energy $3U$, while a lower manifold derived from $2U$ contains spatially resolved dipole--antidipole configurations. Exact diagonalization shows that finite hopping turns this lower manifold into a dispersive band whose finite-chain edge lies substantially below the compact $3U$ scale. Despite this lower energetic edge, its normalized odd-harmonic spectral weight is perturbatively suppressed as $(J/U)^2$, reflecting virtual hybridization of the initial and final eigenstates. A consistent second-order treatment of the $|1111\rangle\leftrightarrow|0220\rangle$ channel includes diagonal self-energy shifts and predicts a weak-field resonance displacement confirmed by exact time evolution. Extended-chain dynamics shows genuine dipole--antidipole separation together with redistribution into higher-energy many-body sectors. The resulting mechanism provides a condensed-matter analogue of multiphoton dynamical Schwinger production in which exact dipole conservation replaces mobile opposite charges by mobile opposite dipoles, while isolated fractonic charges remain immobile.

</details>


### [84] [Unistochastic reduction of multi-matrix coherent-state kernels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24483)
*Shannon Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies finite-N coherent-state overlap integrals in multi-matrix BPS correlators, interpreting them as Laplace transforms of the Haar-pushforward measure on the unistochastic set; it derives a compact Bessel-integral representation for N=3 and provides exact moment expansions and sampling methods for general N.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and compute finite-N coherent-state overlap integrals arising in multi-matrix BPS correlators, especially under commuting parameter assumptions, and to exploit geometric and probabilistic structure of unistochastic matrices.

Method: Interprets the integral I_N(C) as a Laplace transform of the Haar-pushforward measure on the unistochastic set; uses stochasticity constraints to reduce dimensionality; applies known Haar-induced measure for N=3; employs Weingarten calculus for moment expansion and constructs a direct Haar-pushforward sampling scheme.

Result: A compact 3D Bessel-integral representation for I_3(C); exact low-order moment expansions via Weingarten calculus for general N; a constructive sampling algorithm for the Haar-pushforward measure on unistochastic matrices.

Conclusion: The coherent-state overlap integral admits a natural probabilistic-geometric interpretation via unistochasticity and Haar measure, enabling analytic results for small N and systematic computational approaches for general N.

Abstract: We revisit the finite-$N$ coherent-state overlap integrals appearing in multi-matrix BPS correlators. For commuting coherent-state parameters, these integrals take the form $I_N(C)=\int_{U(N)}dμ_H(U)\, \exp\!\left(\sum_{ij}C_{ij}|U_{ij}|^2\right)$. We show that $I_N(C)$ is naturally the Laplace transform of the Haar-pushforward measure on the unistochastic set. Row and column stochasticity reduce the source variables from $N^2$ to $(N-1)^2$. For $N=3$, using the known Haar-induced measure on the unistochastic body, we obtain a compact three-dimensional Bessel-integral representation. For general finite $N$, the same formulation gives an exact low-order moment expansion via Weingarten calculus and a direct Haar-pushforward sampling program.

</details>


### [85] [Braneworld tidal charge through the classical double copy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24521)
*Juan C. La Cruz,Jesús A. Rodríguez*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores the classical double copy formalism applied to a rotating black hole geometry on a brane in the Randall-Sundrum II model, where five-dimensional bulk effects manifest as a tidal charge in the effective 4D metric (which admits a Kerr–Schild form). It constructs single and zeroth copies, showing that regular sources arise solely from the tidal charge—mass contributes only to source-free fields. A formal analogy is found between the tidal charge β and the squared electric charge Q² in Kerr–Newman, despite differing physical origins and sign flexibility of β. With an added brane electromagnetic field, both Q² and β enter the geometry and its double copies only via the sum Q² + β, demonstrating degeneracy between distinct physical contributions in the double copy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how higher-dimensional gravitational effects (encoded via tidal charge in braneworld scenarios) map onto the classical double copy framework, and whether such geometrically induced 'charges' can mimic or merge with conventional gauge-theoretic charges in the double copy correspondence.

Method: Analyzes the induced 4D rotating black-hole metric on the RS-II brane, which takes Kerr–Schild form over flat spacetime and includes a tidal charge β from bulk Weyl curvature. Constructs the single copy (gauge field) and zeroth copy (scalar field) by applying standard double copy prescriptions to this Kerr–Schild geometry; examines source structure (via field equations) and compares to Kerr–Newman and charged generalizations.

Result: The single and zeroth copies possess regular sources strictly proportional to the tidal charge β; the mass term contributes only to source-free components in the regular region. A formal duality emerges: β ↔ Q² in the induced geometry and its double copies—even though β is gravitational and sign-agnostic, while Q² is electromagnetic and non-negative. When a physical U(1) field is present on the brane, the full low-energy Kerr–Schild geometry and its double copies depend on Q² and β only through the combination Q² + β.

Conclusion: The classical double copy of effective braneworld geometries cannot distinguish between physically distinct origins—here, bulk-induced tidal charge and brane-localized electromagnetic charge—as both contribute identically to the double-copied fields. This reveals an intrinsic degeneracy in the double copy when applied to effective theories with mixed gravitational and gauge degrees of freedom.

Abstract: We investigate the classical double copy of the rotating black-hole geometry induced on the brane in the Randall-Sundrum II scenario. The influence of the five-dimensional bulk is encoded in the effective four-dimensional geometry through a tidal charge, while the metric admits a Kerr-Schild representation over a flat background. We construct the corresponding single and zeroth copies and find that their regular sources are entirely proportional to the tidal charge, whereas the mass-dependent contribution is source-free in the regular region. Comparison with Kerr-Newman reveals a formal correspondence between the tidal charge and the squared electromagnetic charge at the level of the induced geometry and its double copies, despite their different physical origins and the fact that the tidal charge may take either sign. When an electromagnetic field is included on the brane, both contributions enter the low-energy Kerr-Schild geometry and its double copies only through the combination $Q^2+β$. These results illustrate how physically distinct brane and bulk contributions can become indistinguishable in the classical double copy of an effective geometry.

</details>


### [86] [On the (In)equivalence of de Sitter and Holographic Entanglement Entropies in Lovelock Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24557)
*Takamasa Kanai*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes that de Sitter entropy and holographic entanglement entropy coincide in static asymptotically de Sitter braneworld spacetimes (RS II model) for Gauss-Bonnet and general Lovelock gravity, but generally differ in stationary cases due to extrinsic-curvature contributions on constant-time slices; the same static/stationary dichotomy holds for braneworld black hole entropy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the relation between gravitational (de Sitter/black hole) entropy and holographic entanglement entropy in higher-curvature gravity—specifically Lovelock theories—and their AdS/braneworld realizations, where entropy functionals are modified by curvature corrections.

Method: Canonical formulation of gravitational action and Euclidean gravitational path integral to derive de Sitter entropy with higher-curvature surface terms; comparison with holographic entanglement entropy computed from the corresponding Wald–Iyer-type entropy functional for braneworld geometries in the RS II model; analysis performed first in Gauss-Bonnet gravity, then generalized to full Lovelock gravity; distinction drawn between static and stationary asymptotically dS and black hole configurations.

Result: Exact agreement between de Sitter entropy and holographic entanglement entropy for static asymptotically de Sitter RS II braneworlds in Gauss-Bonnet and general Lovelock gravity; disagreement in stationary cases due to nonvanishing extrinsic-curvature contribution on constant-time hypersurfaces; analogous agreement/disagreement holds for braneworld black hole entropy versus holographic entanglement entropy.

Conclusion: The equivalence of gravitational and holographic entropies in higher-curvature braneworlds is conditional on spacetime symmetry—specifically, it holds for static but not generic stationary configurations—thereby refining the holographic dictionary in Lovelock gravity and highlighting the role of foliation-dependent terms in entropy matching.

Abstract: We investigate the relation between de Sitter entropy and holographic entanglement entropy in higher-curvature gravity and its braneworld realization. Using the canonical formulation of the gravitational action and the Euclidean gravitational path integral, we derive the de Sitter entropy, including contributions from higher-curvature surface terms, and compare it with the holographic entanglement entropy obtained from the corresponding entropy functional.
  We first consider Gauss-Bonnet gravity and show that the two entropies coincide for static asymptotically de Sitter braneworld spacetimes on the RS II model, while they generally differ for stationary spacetimes. The mismatch in the stationary case arises from an extrinsic-curvature contribution associated with the constant-time surface, which vanishes for static configurations but is generally nonzero for stationary geometries.
  We then extend the analysis to general Lovelock gravity and show that the agreement between the de Sitter entropy and holographic entanglement entropy persists for static asymptotically de Sitter braneworld spacetimes in the RS II model beyond the Gauss-Bonnet case. We also comment that the same distinction between static and stationary configurations applies to braneworld black holes: the black hole entropy agrees with the holographic entanglement entropy in the static case, while the two generally differ for stationary braneworld black holes.
  Our results clarify the relation between gravitational and holographic entropies in higher-curvature gravity and provide a broader perspective on holographic correspondence in Lovelock theories and their braneworld realizations.

</details>


### [87] [The Gaiotto-Kim Trace Formula and Line Defects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24582)
*Sungjoon Kim,Spencer Stubbs*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how 4d half-BPS line defects embed into the Gaiotto–Kim trace formula for 3d TQFT ellipsoid partition functions, yielding a homomorphism to 3d Wilson loops that preserves fusion. It provides an algorithm to identify simple objects of the 3d TQFT as Wilson loops in UV abelian Chern–Simons–matter theory and computes modular $S$- and $T$-matrices via supersymmetric localization, applied to $(A_1,G)$ Argyres–Douglas theories with higher-power monodromy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the correspondence between 4d $\mathcal{N}=2$ SCFT line defects and 3d TQFT observables—specifically how half-BPS line defects map to Wilson loops—and to systematically reconstruct the categorical data (e.g., simple objects, modular matrices) of the IR 3d TQFT from its UV abelian Chern-Simons-matter description.

Method: Supersymmetric localization on the 3-sphere; analysis of the Gaiotto-Kim trace formula with inserted 4d half-BPS line defects; algebraic reconstruction of fusion rules and modular data from Wilson loop operators in abelian Chern-Simons-matter theory; application to $(A_1,G)$ Argyres-Douglas theories via their associated monodromy data.

Result: A homomorphism from the fusion algebra of 4d half-BPS line defects to that of 3d Wilson loops is established; a constructive algorithm identifies simple objects of the 3d TQFT as specific Wilson loops; explicit $S$- and $T$-matrices are derived for several 3d TQFTs descending from $(A_1,G)$ AD theories with higher-power monodromy.

Conclusion: The trace formula framework consistently encodes 4d defect data into 3d TQFT structure, and the UV abelian Chern-Simons-matter description fully determines the IR modular tensor category—including its simple objects and braiding/fusion data—via Wilson loop identification and localization.

Abstract: We study the insertion of 4d half-BPS line defects into the trace formula proposed by Gaiotto and Kim, which computes the ellipsoid partition function of the 3d topological quantum field theory (TQFT) arising from a 4d $\mathcal{N}=2$ superconformal field theory. This yields a homomorphism to 3d Wilson loops, preserving the fusion algebra of the 4d defects. We also present a systematic algorithm for determining the simple objects of a 3d TQFT in terms of Wilson loops in the UV 3d $\mathcal{N}=2$ abelian Chern-Simons-matter theory. Utilizing supersymmetric localization, we characterize the modular $S$- and $T$-matrices of the TQFT. We apply our algorithm to various 3d TQFTs arising from the trace formula with higher-power monodromy of the $(A_1,G)$ Argyres-Douglas theories.

</details>


### [88] [ABJM to BMN in a Double Scale Limit: Indices and Bubbling Geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24649)
*Zhengyuan Du*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a precise double-scaling limit of ABJM theory that maps its monopole-charge-q sector to the rank-q BMN matrix model, with coupling controlled by ν = N/k²; the correspondence is verified via both gravity (Penrose/Hopf limits of half-BPS geometries) and field theory (exact factorization of the superconformal index).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify a controllable, nontrivial large-N limit of ABJM theory where a well-defined quantum mechanical dual—specifically the BMN matrix model—emerges naturally from both bulk geometry and exact field-theoretic observables.

Method: 1) Gravity side: Take a double-scaling limit (N,k→∞, N/k²→ν) of Hopf-quotiented Donos–Simón half-BPS geometries; analyze electrostatic data (Lin–Maldacena problem) and flux quantization. 2) Field-theory side: Apply supersymmetric localization to the ABJM index at finite N,k, then rigorously prove coefficientwise factorization of the index at fixed monopole charge q into neutral (q=0) and BMN-refined-Witten-index parts.

Result: The monopole-charge-q sector of ABJM in the N/k²→ν limit is exactly dual to the rank-q BMN matrix model with dimensionless coupling ∝ ν; the partition data of Lin–Maldacena disks bijectively label BMN vacua; the ABJM index factorizes as Index_{ABJM}^{(q)} = Index_{ABJM}^{(0)} × [z^q × Index_{BMN,q}^{refined}], isolating the BMN index upon division.

Conclusion: The double-scaled ABJM theory provides a first-principles, index-level derivation of the BMN matrix model as a consistent quantum subsector, bridging half-BPS supergravity geometries and exact supersymmetric counting in a controlled AdS₄/CFT₃ context.

Abstract: We study a double scale limit of $U(N)_k\times U(N)_{-k}$ ABJM theory, with $N,k\to\infty$ and $N/k^2\toν\in(0,\infty)$, at fixed positive total monopole charge $q$. In this limit, the Penrose geometry retains a compact null circle of finite radius, and the sector of monopole charge $q$ carries $q$ units of longitudinal momentum. It is therefore naturally associated with the rank-$q$ BMN matrix model, while $ν$ fixes its dimensionless coupling. We establish this relation from both gravity and the supersymmetric index. On the gravity side, we take the double scale limit of Hopf quotients of the Donos--Simón half-BPS geometries. The disk carrying the growing background flux becomes an infinite conducting plane, while the remaining finite disks reproduce the Lin--Maldacena electrostatic problem. Their quantized fluxes map directly to the partition data labeling BMN vacua. On the field-theory side, starting from the finite-$N$, finite-$k$ ABJM localization formula, we prove that, at fixed positive monopole charge $q$, the ABJM superconformal index factorizes coefficientwise in the transverse fugacities into a universal neutral contribution and the refined Witten index of the rank-$q$ BMN matrix model, together with the longitudinal momentum weight. The neutral contribution is precisely the limiting zero-monopole-charge index. Dividing by this universal factor therefore isolates the BMN index summed over all its supersymmetric vacua.

</details>


### [89] [Finite-Time Doppler Covariance Spectroscopy in the AdS/CFT correspondence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24806)
*Feiyi Liu,Shiyang Chen,Yang Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a finite-duration covariance framework within AdS/CFT to quantify intrinsic chirality of a rotating BTZ black hole using boundary control protocols, quadrature phase cycling, and resonance-velocity sampling—enabling subpercent deterministic estimation of the black hole’s rotation parameter from boundary covariance data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and quantify intrinsic bath chirality (i.e., spacetime rotation) in the AdS/CFT correspondence, disentangling it from control-induced kinematic effects in finite-time measurements.

Method: Develops an exact finite-duration covariance formalism for scalar fields in rotating BTZ spacetime; employs boundary controls on a compact cylinder to induce velocity-resonant sum- and difference-frequency sectors; uses two-setting quadrature phase cycling to cancel difference-frequency contributions and reconstruct resonant pair responses; applies normalized contrast between opposite-resonance velocities to eliminate control- and state-dependent artifacts; derives leakage bounds and validates with exact BTZ spectra and truncated transfer amplitudes.

Result: Enables quantitative, finite-linewidth estimation of BTZ rotation parameter with subpercent deterministic bias using only boundary covariance data; demonstrates quadratic-order linewidth bias suppression for smooth spectra; establishes explicit real-projection leakage control; verifies phase-cycle reconstruction and convergence via long-pulse and bath-weighted calculations.

Conclusion: The framework successfully separates control-induced resonance kinematics from intrinsic spacetime chirality, converting finite-time, real-boundary covariance measurements into a robust, operator-normalization–independent estimator of black hole angular momentum in AdS₃/CFT₂.

Abstract: From a holographic perspective, we formulate an exact finite-duration covariance framework that resolves chirality in the AdS/CFT correspondence. In a rotating BTZ black hole spacetime, relative motion between two boundary controls converts sum-frequency matching on the compact boundary cylinder into discrete velocity resonances indexed by the angular momentum of the scalar field. Because the complete real-control covariance also contains difference-frequency sectors, a two-setting quadrature cycle cancels them and reconstructs the resonant pair contribution from complete positive-semidefinite covariance matrices. For an isolated angular mode, reconstructed pair responses at opposite resonance velocities sample the boundary spectrum at opposite angular momentum and a common control-selected frequency. Their normalized contrast cancels reflection-symmetric control factors and the state-independent operator normalization, yielding a finite-linewidth estimator of intrinsic bath chirality. For a smooth bath spectrum, the bias from common centered even narrowing windows begins at quadratic order in linewidth, extending the protection beyond Gaussian sampling. An explicit real-projection leakage bound controls contamination from neighboring modes. Long-pulse calculations with the exact BTZ spectrum verify phase-cycle reconstruction and quadratic linewidth scaling. Bath-weighted calculations with exact truncated transfer amplitudes establish convergence to this baseline. Under the default controls, the first two nonzero angular modes support extraction from the complete angular sum and reconstruct the BTZ rotation parameter with subpercent deterministic bias. The framework thus separates control-induced resonance kinematics from intrinsic bath chirality and converts finite-time covariance data into a quantitative estimate of the rotating BTZ black hole.

</details>


### [90] [The Generalized Uncertainty Principle. Massless QED Renormalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24817)
*Ezequiel Valero,Héctor Gisbert,Victor Ilisie*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether generalized uncertainty principle (GUP)-deformed quantum electrodynamics remains renormalizable at one loop; it finds that only one new physical counterterm—an axial-axial four-fermion interaction—is needed, establishing a minimal radiative completion and linking its running to Einstein-Cartan gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the tree-level operator content of GUP-deformed quantum electrodynamics is closed under renormalization, i.e., whether additional operators are generated radiatively beyond those present at tree level.

Method: One-loop perturbative computation of UV divergences in massless GUP-deformed QED to first order in the deformation parameter; systematic determination of required counterterms and elimination of redundant operators using field redefinitions and symmetry constraints.

Result: Renormalization introduces exactly one independent physical counterterm—the axial-axial four-fermion interaction—beyond the original tree-level operators; the Wilson coefficient of this operator exhibits leading-logarithmic running.

Conclusion: The GUP deformation admits a minimal radiative completion at one loop and first order in the deformation parameter; the induced contact interaction has an operator-level correspondence with the four-fermion term in Einstein-Cartan gravity.

Abstract: The effective-field-theory interpretation of generalized uncertainty principle (GUP) deformations does not by itself establish whether their tree-level operator content is closed under renormalization. We investigate this question in massless GUP-deformed quantum electrodynamics at one loop and to first order in the deformation parameter. We compute the ultraviolet divergences of the relevant Green functions and determine the counterterm structure. Our central result is that, after eliminating redundant operators, renormalization requires only one independent physical counterterm beyond those associated with the tree-level theory: an axial-axial four-fermion interaction. The original deformation therefore admits a minimal radiative completion at this order. We derive the leading logarithmic running of the induced Wilson coefficient and discuss its operator-level correspondence with the contact interaction arising in Einstein-Cartan gravity.

</details>


### [91] [Quantum inversion of the vacuum hierarchy and multi-kink unbinding in the non-degenerate double sine-Gordon model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24861)
*Jonathan Lozano-Mayo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum corrections to vacuum energies and multi-kink binding in the non-degenerate double sine-Gordon model, finding a quantum-induced vacuum hierarchy inversion confirmed by both one-loop analytic and matrix-product-state (MPS) numerical methods; the inversion crossing is quantitatively refined beyond one-loop via Gaussian effective potential and lattice extrapolation, and multi-kink size scaling reflects progressive unbinding while remaining weakly bound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve how quantum fluctuations modify the classical vacuum energy hierarchy and stability of topological multi-kinks in a non-degenerate double sine-Gordon model—particularly whether quantum effects can invert vacuum ordering and destabilize composite kinks.

Method: Combined analytical and numerical approaches: (i) one-loop quantum field theory with normal ordering at the primary vacuum meson mass; (ii) matrix-product-state simulations for n=4 kinks on finite lattices; (iii) continuum extrapolation of bulk energy densities; (iv) Gaussian effective potential for improved crossing prediction; (v) finite-chain analysis of Q=4 multi-kink spatial extent versus vacuum energy difference.

Result: Quantum corrections invert the classical vacuum hierarchy, favoring the central secondary vacuum above a critical coupling determined by classical vacuum splitting and meson mass ratio; MPS confirms inversion, with crossing ~22% lower than one-loop prediction at α=0.3; Gaussian effective potential closely reproduces the crossing; Q=4 multi-kink expands as vacuum energy difference decreases, and its size diverges beyond the crossing—indicating incipient unbinding—while total mass correction remains <10% of classical value.

Conclusion: Quantum effects fundamentally restructure the vacuum landscape and multi-kink binding in the non-degenerate double sine-Gordon model: vacuum hierarchy inversion is robust across methods, and multi-kinks undergo a continuous, weakly perturbative unbinding transition rather than abrupt dissociation.

Abstract: We study the quantum corrections to the vacuum energies and to the binding of the multi-kinks of the non-degenerate double sine-Gordon model. With normal ordering defined at the meson mass of the primary vacuum, the one-loop calculation predicts an inversion of the classical vacuum hierarchy, in which quantum fluctuations favour the central secondary vacuum above a coupling set by the classical splitting of the vacua and their meson mass ratio. Matrix-product-state calculations for $n=4$ confirm the inversion, and extrapolation of the bulk energy densities in the lattice spacing supports its persistence in the continuum. The crossing lies below the one-loop prediction, by $22$ percent at $α=0.3$, and is reproduced closely by the Gaussian effective potential. Finite-chain calculations at $α=0.3$ show that the central region of the multi-kink of topological charge $Q=4$ expands as the difference of the vacuum energies falls, as a collective-coordinate description predicts, and that beyond the crossing it grows with the size of the system. The composite therefore approaches the loss of its binding with a correction to its total mass still under a tenth of the classical value.

</details>


### [92] [Measuring correlations using local and nonlocal quenches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24873)
*Alexey G. Mikhaylenko,Andrew G. Semenov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes a quantum quench-based method to measure correlation functions—including non-Gaussian ones—in scalar field theories via local/nonlocal perturbations and their imprint on post-quench field evolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a theoretically grounded, experimentally feasible protocol for measuring higher-order (especially non-Gaussian) correlation functions in quantum field systems, which are typically inaccessible via conventional linear-response techniques.

Method: Analyzes the dynamical response of massive and massless scalar QFTs to sudden quantum quenches—both local (spatially localized Hamiltonian change) and nonlocal (global or extended coupling change)—and derives how the time evolution of the expectation value of the field operator encodes initial-state correlation functions.

Result: Demonstrates that local quenches probe spatially resolved two-point and higher-point correlations, while nonlocal quenches access momentum-space integrated or global correlators; explicitly shows the mapping between quench-induced field dynamics and initial n-point functions up to quartic order.

Conclusion: Quantum quenches serve as a versatile, controllable probe for reconstructing both Gaussian and non-Gaussian correlation structure in QFTs, enabling new avenues for characterizing quantum many-body states and out-of-equilibrium dynamics.

Abstract: We present a theoretical proposal for measuring correlation functions in a quantum field system based on the use of quantum quenches. Using the examples of massive and massless scalar field theories, we show that a short-term perturbation of the system leads to a dependence of the further evolution of the average field on the initial state's correlation functions. Considering two types of perturbations, namely local and nonlocal quenches, we show which correlation functions can be measured in each of these cases. The proposed procedure can be applied to study non-Gaussian correlations.

</details>


### [93] [Gauss--Bonnet running and the de Sitter saddle of quadratic gravity inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24912)
*Ruolin Liu,Niayesh Afshordi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper shows that including the Gauss-Bonnet term in the one-loop renormalization group running of quadratic gravity restores a stationary de Sitter solution, enabling hilltop inflation with a flat scalaron potential; it predicts testable CMB observables (ns ≈ 0.973–0.978, r ≳ 0.008) consistent with upcoming polarization experiments, and constrains matter content to ~10⁵–10⁶ fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the absence of a stationary de Sitter state in quadratic gravity inflation when the Euler trace anomaly is included, and to reconcile the high scalar spectral tilt (ns) favored by CMB data with a robust, scheme-independent inflationary mechanism.

Method: Analyzes the full one-loop RG running of curvature-squared couplings—including the usually neglected Gauss–Bonnet coefficient—in quadratic gravity; identifies a unique coupling ratio where Euler-induced running balances R² running, yielding a stationary de Sitter solution for both the gravitational constraint and compact Euclidean action; computes scalaron potential, inflationary dynamics (e-folds, ns, r), and consistency bounds from tensor limits and loop control.

Result: A stationary de Sitter state is recovered; scalaron potential is an extremely flat hilltop (m²/H² ≈ −5×10⁻¹⁰) joined to an inverse-linear plateau; last N* ≈ 50–60 e-folds are scheme independent with ns ≈ 1 − 4/(3N*); r is determined by number of matter fields enhancing running; current r-bound implies minimum ~4×10⁶ scalars or ~3×10⁵ vectors; loop control sets upper limit ~10× higher; predicted r ≳ 0.008 and 0.973 ≲ ns ≲ 0.978.

Conclusion: Quadratic gravity with full one-loop running—including Gauss–Bonnet—provides a viable, predictive, and falsifiable inflationary framework compatible with Planck/BICEP/Keck constraints and within reach of next-generation CMB B-mode surveys.

Abstract: Inflation driven purely by the quantum running of the curvature-squared couplings of quadratic gravity was shown to accommodate the high scalar tilt favored by recent cosmic microwave background observations, starting from a Euclidean four-sphere at a maximum of the running $R^2$ coupling. That maximum exists only in one renormalization scheme, and is not stationary once the Euler trace anomaly is included. We show that the standard one-loop running, with the usually neglected Gauss--Bonnet coefficient retained, recovers the missing de Sitter state: the Euler running balances the scale dependence of the $R^2$ term at a unique coupling ratio, giving a de Sitter solution that is stationary for the gravitational constraint and for the compact Euclidean action alike, and whose scalaron potential is an extremely flat hilltop ($m^2/H^2\simeq-5\times10^{-10}$) joined to an inverse-linear inflationary plateau. The last $N_*\simeq50$--$60$ $e$-folds are scheme independent, with scalar tilt $n_s\simeq1-4/(3N_*)$, while the tensor-to-scalar ratio, $r$, is set by the number of matter fields that enhance the running. The current tensor bound excludes pure gravity and sets a minimum matter content, some $4\times10^6$ conformally coupled scalars or $3\times10^5$ vectors---$2.6$ times fewer than the momentum-induced scheme requires---while one-loop control to the end of inflation sets a maximum about ten times higher. Across that window the model predicts $r\gtrsim0.008$ with $0.973\lesssim n_s\lesssim0.978$, within reach of upcoming CMB polarization surveys.

</details>


### [94] [Chiral Carroll string near Schwarzschild black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24939)
*Kedar S. Kolekar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives the chiral Carroll string action via a 1/c expansion of the relativistic bosonic string action, and applies it to study string dynamics near a Schwarzschild black hole horizon, revealing chiral (left- or right-moving) horizon fluctuations that drive non-trivial motion in the near-horizon Rindler geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the ultra-relativistic (c → 0) limit of string theory in curved spacetimes—specifically, how closed bosonic strings behave as chiral Carroll objects near black hole horizons where relativistic causality breaks down.

Method: Carroll expansion (systematic 1/c expansion) of the Polyakov action for the closed bosonic string; application to Schwarzschild spacetime with near-horizon (string-Carroll) geometry identified as a Rindler-like Carroll background; analysis of worldsheet equations of motion and mode structure.

Result: The chiral Carroll string admits purely left- or right-moving excitations on the black hole horizon; these horizon modes source non-trivial bulk motion in the Rindler region, indicating a holographic-like coupling between horizon chirality and transverse dynamics.

Conclusion: The chiral Carroll string provides a consistent ultra-relativistic effective description for strings near black hole horizons, with chirality at the horizon governing dynamical behavior in the near-horizon region—suggesting a geometric origin for chiral edge modes in Carrollian gravity/string correspondence.

Abstract: We obtain the action for the chiral Carroll string moving in a curved string-Carroll target spacetime from a Carroll expansion, in powers of the speed of light $c$, of the action for the relativistic closed bosonic string. Applying this setup to a classical closed bosonic string approaching the $4$-dimensional Schwarzschild black hole, we study the dynamics of the string when it behaves as a chiral Carroll string with the near-horizon string-Carroll expanded geometry as the target spacetime. We find that the chiral Carroll string can have either left-moving or right-moving fluctuations on the horizon, while exhibiting a non-trivial motion in the Rindler spacetime which depends on these horizon fluctuations.

</details>


### [95] [Towards All-Order Locality in the Higher-Spin Holomorphic Zero-Form Sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24956)
*D. A. Valerev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes necessary and sufficient conditions for constructing space-time spin-local holomorphic zero-form vertices in Vasiliev's higher-spin theory, extending prior work by enforcing a stricter locality requirement; it explicitly computes the spin-local η² vertex and enables systematic all-order local vertex construction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To ensure space-time spin-locality of interaction vertices in Vasiliev's higher-spin theory—beyond the weaker spinor spin-locality previously established—by imposing a refined requirement on holomorphic zero-form vertices.

Method: Derives necessary and sufficient conditions on holomorphic one-form sector vertices that guarantee existence of holomorphic zero-form vertices satisfying the strengthened locality requirement; applies the scheme to compute the η² zero-form vertex explicitly.

Result: A complete characterization of holomorphic one-form vertices ensuring space-time spin-local zero-form vertices; explicit computation of the spin-local holomorphic η² zero-form vertex, surpassing prior spinor-local results.

Conclusion: The derived conditions provide a foundational framework for systematically constructing fully space-time spin-local interaction vertices to all orders in higher-spin theory.

Abstract: This work addresses the construction of local interaction vertices in the holomorphic zero-form sector obtained from Vasiliev equations. We focus on a requirement on zero-form vertices, previously proposed in a less restrictive form in arXiv:2208.02004, which, together with spinor spin-locality, guarantees space-time spin-locality. Necessary and sufficient conditions on the holomorphic one-form sector vertices are derived that ensure the existence of holomorphic zero-form vertices satisfying this requirement. As a concrete demonstration, the proposed scheme is applied to compute the spin-local holomorphic $η^2$ zero-form sector vertex, a result that goes beyond previous work where only spinor spin-locality was established. These findings lay the groundwork for a systematic all-order construction of local vertices in higher-spin theory.

</details>


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [96] [Coherent deeply virtual Compton scattering on helium-4 beyond the leading twist approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22405)
*V. Martínez-Fernández,B. Pire,P. Sznajder,J. Wagner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes deeply virtual Compton scattering (DVCS) on helium-4 to probe its 3D quark-gluon structure, incorporating leading-twist and kinematical twist-3/4 contributions; provides JLab-relevant cross section and asymmetry predictions; highlights the necessity of NLO and higher-twist effects for data interpretation; and presents the first nuclear tomographic image of quarks and gluons in ^4He.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To access the quark and gluon structure of light nuclei and enable three-dimensional nuclear tomography via coherent hard exclusive reactions, specifically DVCS on helium-4.

Method: Theoretical analysis of DVCS on ^4He including leading-twist contributions as well as kinematical twist-3 and twist-4 effects; numerical computation of cross sections and asymmetries for JLab-relevant kinematics; incorporation of next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD corrections and higher-twist contributions.

Result: Quantitative predictions for DVCS cross sections and asymmetries in JLab kinematics; demonstration that NLO and kinematical twist-3/4 contributions are essential for accurate interpretation; derivation of the first tomographic image of quark and gluon distributions in the helium-4 nucleus.

Conclusion: Coherent DVCS on ^4He, when treated with full twist and NLO accuracy, enables high-fidelity 3D imaging of nuclear partonic structure; the results establish a foundation for future experimental validation and extension to other light nuclei.

Abstract: Coherent hard exclusive reactions on light nuclei offer access to their quark and gluon structure and provide a framework for three-dimensional nuclear tomography. We analyze deeply virtual Compton scattering on a helium-4 target, including leading-twist contributions as well as kinematical twist-3 and twist-4 effects. We present numerical estimates of cross sections and asymmetries for kinematic regimes relevant to JLab experiments. We emphasize the importance of next to leading order (NLO) and kinematical twist 3 and 4 contributions to interpret data in the JLab kinematics. We deduce a first tomographic image of quarks and gluons in the helium-4 nucleus.

</details>


### [97] [Resonant Gravitational Production of Chiral Dark Photons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22423)
*Leah Jenks,Marc Kamionkowski,Edward W. Kolb*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates chiral, parity-odd couplings between a massive vector field (dark photon) and the inflaton during inflation, identifying two resonant production mechanisms—tachyonic instability and parametric resonance—that enhance dark-photon abundance, broaden viable dark matter parameter space, yield chiral (helicity-biased) populations with parity-violating signatures, and encode reheating history in the number density spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain how chiral couplings of dark photons to the inflaton during inflation can significantly enhance gravitational particle production, potentially accounting for dark matter while generating testable parity-violating signals and encoding reheating dynamics.

Method: Analytical and semi-numerical analysis of quantum production of a massive vector field coupled via a parity-odd operator (e.g., φ F_μν \tilde{F}^μν) during slow-roll inflation; classification of regimes by coupling strength vs. mass; computation of mode functions, occupation numbers, helicity asymmetry, and post-inflation evolution including reheating effects.

Result: Two distinct production regimes emerge: tachyonic (strong coupling, low mass), yielding highly chiral, helicity-polarized dark photons; and parametric resonance (weaker coupling, higher mass), enhancing transverse modes. Both increase total abundance beyond standard minimal/nonminimal coupling predictions. Reheating duration and temperature imprint characteristic features on the comoving number density spectrum.

Conclusion: Chiral inflaton–dark photon coupling substantially enriches the phenomenology of inflationary vector production: it expands the viable dark-photon dark matter parameter space, predicts observable chiral/parity-violating imprints (e.g., in CMB or birefringence), and makes the dark photon spectrum a potential probe of post-inflationary reheating physics.

Abstract: Vector fields with a chiral, parity-odd coupling to a pseudoscalar field are known to undergo resonant amplification. In this work, we study this phenomenon in the context of cosmological gravitational particle production during early-universe inflation. We consider a massive vector (dark photon) field with a parity-odd coupling to the inflaton. There are two regimes of production, delineated by the relative size of the coupling and the dark photon mass, arising from a tachyonic instability, and a parametric resonance, respectively. As a result of the coupling, the transverse modes, subdominant in both the minimally and nonminimally coupled theories, can dominate the total late-time abundance. The viable parameter space for dark-photon dark matter is thus widened. Furthermore, in the tachyonic case, the production is dominated by a single helicity mode, leading to a population of chiral dark photons that can lead to potential parity-violating observational signatures. We further explore the dependence of the production on the reheating history, and find that information about reheating is encoded in the number density spectrum.

</details>


### [98] [Gravitational Waves from Long Strings and Loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22430)
*Christos Litos,Sarunas Verner,Wei Xue,Fengwei Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the gravitational wave (GW) spectrum from a global cosmic string network in the scaling regime, splitting it into infrared (analytically derived via unequal-time correlator of stress-energy tensor in the unconnected segment model generalized to loops) and ultraviolet (data-driven, calibrated to lattice simulations) components; the spectrum exhibits distinct power-law behaviors (k and k³ in IR, plateau with log tilt in UV), with turnover set by axion mass; constraints and projections on axion parameter space (fa, ma) are provided.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To predict the gravitational wave spectrum from global cosmic strings—particularly relevant for axion-like models—and bridge analytical modeling with numerical lattice simulation results to enable observational tests.

Method: Analytical derivation of the infrared GW spectrum using the unequal-time correlator of the string stress-energy tensor within a generalized unconnected segment model that includes loop contributions; development of a data-driven method linking this correlator formalism to instantaneous radiation power spectra from existing lattice simulations for the ultraviolet regime.

Result: The predicted GW energy density spectrum Ω_GW(k) rises as ∝ k and k³ in the infrared, flattens into a plateau with mild logarithmic tilt in the ultraviolet, with turnover frequency determined by the axion (Goldstone boson) mass m_a; quantitative constraints and sensitivity projections in the (f_a, m_a) plane are obtained using current and future GW observatories.

Conclusion: Global cosmic strings produce a distinctive, calculable GW spectrum whose shape and amplitude depend critically on axion parameters; this work enables robust exclusion or discovery prospects for axion-like particles and symmetry-breaking scales via gravitational wave astronomy.

Abstract: We compute the gravitational wave spectrum produced by a global cosmic string network in the scaling regime. The spectrum naturally divides at the string correlation length into infrared and ultraviolet parts. In the infrared, we derive the spectrum analytically from the unequal-time correlator of the string stress-energy tensor within the unconnected segment model, which we generalize for the first time to string loops. The loop contribution can be comparable to that of long strings, depending on the loop evolution parameters. In the ultraviolet, where structure below the correlation length dominates the source, we develop a data-driven method that connects this correlator formalism to the instantaneous radiation power spectrum measured in existing lattice simulations. The predicted spectrum rises with frequency as $Ω_{\rm GW}\propto k$ and $k^{3}$ in the infrared, and flattens into a plateau with a mild logarithmic tilt in the ultraviolet. The turnover frequency between the two is set by the mass of the Goldstone boson, the axion. Confronting the predicted spectrum with current data and projected sensitivities, we map out current constraints and future probes in the plane of the symmetry-breaking scale~$f_a$ and the axion mass~$m_a$.

</details>


### [99] [Non-Perturbative Modulus Decay and Multi-Component Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22438)
*Gordon Kane,Leia Price,Luis Rufino,Scott Watson,Fred Adams*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the dynamical robustness of non-thermal cosmological histories—particularly modulus-dominated eras—against non-perturbative decay, using an effective field theory from G₂ compactifications; it finds Wino-like fermion production suppressed, but axion production via a modulus-dependent kinetic term can yield rapid instabilities requiring nonlinear analysis, potentially altering dark matter composition and boosting dark radiation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether the standard non-thermal cosmological scenario—featuring coherent scalar oscillations, early matter domination, and perturbative decay—is dynamically stable when non-perturbative (e.g., parametric) decay channels are included.

Method: Applies effective field theory from G₂ compactifications to model oscillating moduli; performs linear Floquet analysis to identify instability bands for Wino-like fermions and axions; assesses relevance of cosmic expansion, backreaction, rescattering, and higher-order operators.
