Result: Non-perturbative Wino/gauge-field production is strongly suppressed; axion production exhibits narrow, Hubble-exceeding instability bands due to modulus-dependent kinetic term—but linear analysis is insufficient to quantify condensate depletion.

Conclusion: Conventional modulus-dominated cosmology remains robust against Wino and gauge-field preheating, but axion-channel preheating may significantly modify dark matter relic abundances and dark radiation, necessitating full nonlinear simulation.

Abstract: Non-thermal cosmological histories rest on the assumption that scalars displaced during inflation undergo coherent oscillations, dominate the energy density of the universe before Big Bang Nucleosynthesis, and decay perturbatively through gravitationally suppressed interactions. In addition, usually a single dark matter component is assumed. This early matter-dominated era is the basis for the usual predictions of non-thermal WIMP production, axion dark matter, entropy generation, and dark radiation. This paper examines whether this picture is dynamically robust against non-perturbative decay of the modulus condensate. In this work, we study non-perturbative particle production from oscillating moduli using the effective field theory appropriate to $G_2$ compactifications. We find that non-perturbative production of Wino-like fermions is strongly suppressed in the relevant parameter regime. In contrast, a modulus-dependent axion kinetic term admits narrow instability bands with growth rates that can exceed the Hubble rate. A linear Floquet analysis alone, however, cannot determine whether these bands significantly deplete the modulus condensate, since cosmic expansion, backreaction, rescattering, and higher-order operators in the effective theory can become important. As a result, the conventional modulus-dominated cosmology remains robust against Wino and gauge-field preheating, while the axion channel provides a potentially important modification that requires nonlinear study. If sufficiently efficient, axion production can alter the division of dark matter between Winos and axions and enhance the dark-radiation abundance.

</details>


### [100] [Machine Learning for Invisible Dark Boson Searches at the Electron-Ion Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22484)
*Rojae Mighty,Ankush Reddy Kanuganti*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates whether incorporating the squared nuclear four-momentum transfer $t$ improves boosted decision tree (BDT) performance over optimized rectangular cuts for invisible dark boson selection at the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC), using generator-level coherent exclusive production in e–Au collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether machine learning—specifically BDTs—can outperform traditional cut-based methods in selecting invisible dark bosons in exclusive processes at the EIC, particularly when kinematic information such as $t$ is available and reconstructable.

Method: The authors simulate coherent exclusive scalar and vector dark boson production in 18 GeV electron + 100 GeV/nucleon gold collisions at generator level. They compare BDTs and optimized rectangular cuts using identical weighted samples, input features, preselection criteria, and optimization objectives—first with only electron-level inputs, then augmented with $t$.

Result: With only electron information, BDTs show no consistent advantage over optimized cuts across 11 boson masses for either scalar or vector types. When $t$ is included, BDTs achieve marginally better signal-background separation than cuts specifically at 10 GeV boson mass for both types.

Conclusion: Inclusion of $t$ enables a small but measurable BDT advantage in dark boson selection at a specific mass; this justifies deeper exploration of ML techniques in exclusive EIC dark-sector searches where $t$ reconstruction is feasible.

Abstract: We investigate whether adding $t$, the positive magnitude of the squared nuclear four-momentum transfer, enables boosted decision trees (BDTs) to improve invisible-dark-boson selection relative to optimized rectangular cuts at the Electron-Ion Collider. We model coherent exclusive scalar and vector production at generator level in electron-gold collisions at 18 GeV by 100 GeV per nucleon. Both methods use identical weighted samples, inputs, preselection, and optimization objectives. Using only electron information, the BDT provided no consistent advantage over optimized cuts for signal selection across 11 masses for each boson type. When both methods also use $t$, the BDT distinguishes signal from background slightly better than optimized cuts at 10 GeV for both boson types. These results motivate further investigation of machine learning in EIC dark-boson searches through exclusive processes where $t$ can be reconstructed.

</details>


### [101] [Heavy-quark spin precession as a magnetic field chronometer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22492)
*Dushmanta Sahu,Captain R. Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes heavy-quark spin precession as a novel probe to quantify the strength and lifetime of magnetic fields in relativistic heavy-ion collisions, using polarized charm and bottom quarks evolving via Larmor precession in the quark–gluon plasma; it predicts measurable charge-dependent polarization splittings in Λc/Λb baryons, with distinct pT and mass dependence, enabling differential constraints on magnetic-field lifetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The lifetime of the strong magnetic field generated in relativistic heavy-ion collisions is poorly constrained, limiting understanding of electromagnetic effects in quark–gluon plasma (QGP) dynamics and related phenomena (e.g., chiral magnetic effect). Existing probes lack direct sensitivity to the field's space–time profile.

Method: The authors model Larmor precession of initially polarized charm and bottom quarks propagating through an expanding QGP medium under realistic time-varying magnetic fields (e.g., decaying exponentially with lifetime τB); they compute spin evolution including transverse/longitudinal mixing, hadronization via coalescence, and final-state Λc+/Λ̄c− and Λb0/Λ̄b0 polarization observables, incorporating hydrodynamic background and realistic field magnitudes (~mπ² at early times).

Result: Realistic magnetic fields (|eB| ~ 1–10 mπ², τB ~ 0.1–1 fm/c) induce measurable precession phases before hadronization, yielding O(1–10%) charge-asymmetric polarization splittings in Λc and Λb baryons; the splittings are stronger and more pT-sensitive for charm than bottom due to longer precession time (1/mQ scaling), providing complementary τB constraints.

Conclusion: Heavy-flavor baryon polarization—particularly in the charm sector—is a quantitative, experimentally accessible probe of the magnetic field’s magnitude and lifetime in heavy-ion collisions; it offers unique sensitivity to the early-time electromagnetic environment of the QGP.

Abstract: The lifetime of the strong magnetic field generated in relativistic heavy-ion collisions remains one of the major open questions in the study of the quark--gluon plasma. We propose heavy-quark spin precession as a probe to quantify the magnetic field and its profile in such collisions. We investigate the Larmor evolution of polarized charm and bottom quarks propagating through an expanding quark--gluon plasma in the presence of the magnetic field. We show that realistic magnetic-field strengths and lifetimes generate measurable accumulated precession phases prior to hadronization, leading to characteristic charge-dependent polarization splittings between $Λ_c^+$ and $\barΛ_c^-$, as well as between $Λ_b^0$ and $\barΛ_b^0$, through the mixing of transverse and longitudinal polarization components. The predicted signals exhibit distinctive transverse-momentum and quark-mass dependence, providing complementary sensitivity to the magnetic-field lifetime in the charm and bottom sectors. Our results suggest heavy-flavor polarization, especially in the charm sector, as a quantitative probe for the space--time evolution of magnetic fields in relativistic heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [102] [Tackling the noisy truncated moment problem with Gaussian Processes: An application to parton distribution functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23196)
*Rohith Karur*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses the noisy truncated Hausdorff moment problem for reconstructing parton distribution functions (PDFs) using Bayesian Gaussian Processes (GPs), introducing novel bias-correction and adaptive kernel methods, and demonstrates robust performance across diverse phenomenological PDF datasets—enabling future application to lattice QCD moment data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations in reconstructing parton distribution functions (PDFs) from a finite, noisy set of moments—especially given the ill-posed nature of the truncated Hausdorff moment problem and challenges in conventional approaches.

Method: Bayesian inference with Gaussian Processes: includes covariance kernel design (including a new class with learnable x-correlation length), analytical posterior characterization, Monte Carlo hyperparameter sampling, validation metrics, and a novel mean-bias correction technique for posterior distributions.

Result: The GP framework robustly reconstructs PDFs from a moderate number of moments across five diverse phenomenological datasets (valence, gluon, sea quark, pion, nucleon); the novel tools resolve persistent reconstruction issues not easily addressed by standard methods.

Conclusion: The proposed GP-based Bayesian framework provides a flexible, robust, and principled solution to the noisy truncated Hausdorff moment problem for PDF reconstruction, and is directly applicable to lattice QCD moment data for ab initio PDF extraction.

Abstract: In this work, we study the noisy truncated Hausdorff moment problem in the context of recovering parton distribution functions (PDFs) from their moments. We start by reviewing basics of the Hausdorff moment problem and analyze how the relation between the amount of moment data provided and the ability for $f(x)$ to be reconstructed varies across different classes of parton distribution functions; we then turn to using Bayesian methods centered around Gaussian Processes (GPs) to solve the moment problems. Standard aspects of Bayesian regression such as covariance kernel choice, analytical characterization of the posterior, hyperparameter sampling with Monte Carlo methods, and validation metrics are detailed. In addition to this, powerful novel tools, such as a method to correct for mean bias in posterior distributions, and a simple class of covariance kernels whose $x$ correlation length is learned during sampling are introduced.
  Finally, we test our framework on 5 datasets from valence, gluon, and sea quark phenomenological pion and nucleon PDF datasets, selected to reflect diversity in PDF behavior. We find that our GP framework robustly reconstructs PDFs given a moderate number of moments. Additionally, the novel tools developed in this work resolve persistent issues that would be difficult to address via conventional means. The presented framework can be readily applied to moments obtained via lattice Quantum Chromodynamics (LQCD) to reconstruct PDFs from theoretical first principles.

</details>


### [103] [Probing light axion-like particles in vector boson fusion at CMS with data parking and scouting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22570)
*Sena Durgut,Mariel Peczak,Gonzalo Alonso-Álvarez,Chiara Amendola,Matteo Cremonesi,Joerg Jaeckel,Matteo Marchegiani*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes novel CMS analysis strategies—data parking for Run 3 and trigger-level data scouting for HL-LHC—to detect axion-like particles (ALPs) in the 10 MeV–10 GeV mass range, overcoming soft-trigger rejection and photon-merging challenges via tracker-based reconstruction of converted photons and diphoton kinematics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: ALPs with masses 10 MeV–10 GeV and moderate couplings evade detection: too short-lived for intensity-frontier experiments and too weakly coupled/light for standard collider searches; LHC VBF production is abundant but obscured by softness and merged photon signatures in the calorimeter.

Method: Leverages CMS data-acquisition innovations: (i) data parking on VBF jet topology (Run 3, 312 fb⁻¹) to retain full events without photon pT thresholds; (ii) Level-1 trigger data scouting (HL-LHC, 3 ab⁻¹) to preserve trigger-level info; uses silicon tracker reconstruction of photon conversions (e⁺e⁻ pairs) to resolve merged photons, reconstructs ALP decay vertex to suppress prompt backgrounds, and employs diphoton invariant mass (Run 3) or pT + conversion tracks + hadronic activity (scouting) to reject K_L → γγ background.

Result: Projected sensitivity reaches unexplored regions in the (mₐ, gₐγγ) plane with Run 3 parked data; HL-LHC scouting extends reach by ~an order of magnitude in both mₐ and gₐγγ.

Conclusion: Tracker-based reconstruction of converted photons combined with specialized data acquisition enables the first viable search for MeV–GeV ALPs at the LHC, closing a major experimental blind spot.

Abstract: Axion-like particles (ALPs) with masses between 10 MeV and 10 GeV and moderately small couplings sit in an experimental blind spot. They decay too fast for intensity-frontier experiments and are too feebly coupled and too light for conventional collider searches. The LHC produces them at substantial rates through vector boson fusion (VBF), the dominant production mode for electromagnetically coupled ALPs, but two obstacles keep this signal out of reach. The events are soft, so trigger thresholds discard most of them, and the Lorentz boost merges the two decay photons into a single calorimeter deposit. We show that the Compact Muon Solenoid (CMS) experiment can overcome both by combining its data-acquisition strategies with the tracker-based reconstruction of merged photon pairs. Data parking on the VBF jet topology in Run 3 (312 fb$^{-1}$) and trigger-level data scouting at the High-Luminosity LHC (HL-LHC, 3 ab$^{-1}$) remove the need for a threshold on the photons. Photons that convert to electron-positron pairs in the silicon tracker resolve the merged pair, since the tracker measures their direction far more finely than the calorimeter granularity. Reconstructing the ALP decay vertex from the conversion tracks removes the prompt backgrounds but not the long-lived $K_L\toγγ$. In Run 3, where the full event is recorded, the merged diphoton invariant mass confines the $K_L$ to a narrow window around its own mass. In the scouting stream, where only trigger-level information survives, the diphoton $p_T$, the conversion tracks, and the hadronic activity in the event take over this role. We project that the parked Run 3 data, already recorded, reach a region of the $(m_a, g_{aγγ})$ plane that no measurement has probed. Level-1 trigger data scouting at the HL-LHC extends that reach by roughly an order of magnitude in both coupling and mass.

</details>


### [104] [Spectroscopic parameters of $B_c$ meson](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24536)
*Sinem Küçükyılmaz,Halil Mutuk*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the $B_c$ meson using a nonrelativistic quark model, computing its mass spectrum (including radial and orbital excitations in S-, P-, D-waves), decay constants (with QCD corrections), radiative widths (E1/M1), and Regge trajectories; key predictions include inverted fine-structure ordering in P/D-waves due to OGE–confinement competition, suppressed M1 widths from small hyperfine splittings, and a $D\to P\to S$ radiative cascade as the optimal discovery channel for D-wave states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe spin-dependent dynamics of the $B_c$ meson—particularly the interplay between one-gluon-exchange and confinement in spin-orbit coupling—and to guide experimental searches for excited states (especially unobserved D-wave) via precise spectroscopic and decay predictions.

Method: Nonrelativistic quark model with phenomenological potential; calculation of mass spectrum (radial/orbital excitations in S/P/D waves), pseudoscalar/vector decay constants including one-loop QCD corrections, E1 and M1 radiative transition widths, and radial Regge trajectories; analysis of wave-function overlaps, fine-structure splittings, and hyperfine effects.

Result: Ground and first radial $B_c$ states agree with experiment; decay constants align with relativistic models and predict $B_c \to \tau\nu_\tau$ rate; E1 transitions favor $D \to P \to S$ cascade; inverted fine-structure ordering predicted in P- and D-wave multiplets due to OGE–confinement competition; M1 widths strongly suppressed by small hyperfine splittings; radial Regge trajectories are nearly linear with decreasing slope as $L$ increases.

Conclusion: The $B_c^*$ mass and fine-structure ordering of the lowest P-wave multiplet are critical experimental benchmarks for testing spin-dependent forces in the $B_c$ system; the predicted $D \to P \to S$ radiative cascade provides a viable pathway to discover D-wave states.

Abstract: We investigate the spectroscopic and decay properties of the $B_c$ meson within a nonrelativistic quark model. We calculate the mass spectrum of radially and orbitally excited states in the $S$-, $P$-, and $D$-wave sectors, the pseudoscalar and vector decay constants with one-loop QCD corrections, the E1 and M1 radiative widths, and radial Regge trajectories. The ground state and its first radial excitation agree well with experiment. The predicted decay constants are in line with relativistic model estimates and yield a corresponding prediction for the leptonic decay $B_c\toτν_τ$. The E1 transition pattern is governed primarily by radial-wave-function overlaps, and the dominant transitions of the lowest $D$-wave multiplet suggest the $D\to P\to S$ radiative cascade as a promising pathway to the unobserved $D$-wave states. The model predicts an inverted fine-structure ordering in the $P$- and $D$-wave multiplets, reflecting the competition between the one-gluon-exchange (OGE) and confinement contributions to the spin--orbit interaction. The allowed M1 widths are strongly suppressed by the small predicted hyperfine splittings, highlighting the importance of a direct $B_c^*$ mass measurement. The radial Regge trajectories are nearly linear across all considered families, with slopes decreasing as the orbital angular momentum increases. These results identify the $B_c^*$ mass and the fine-structure ordering of the lowest $P$-wave multiplet as the key measurements for testing the spin-dependent dynamics of the $B_c$ system.

</details>


### [105] [New bounds on light scalars from the tip of the red giant branch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22638)
*Natnael Debru,Audrey Fung,Saniya Heeba,Hugo Schérer,Katelin Schutz,Aaron C. Vincent*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper improves constraints on light scalars coupled to electrons using red giant branch (TRGB) observations, achieving a 100× stronger bound on the scalar-electron coupling α_φee > 1.6×10⁻³⁴ (95% CL) for sub-keV masses, via self-consistent MESA simulations incorporating resonant plasmon→scalar conversion in degenerate stellar cores.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To tighten astrophysical bounds on light, weakly coupled scalars—especially Higgs portal scalars—by properly modeling scalar energy loss in red giant cores, where prior bounds relied on simplified rescaling of neutrino rates rather than self-consistent stellar evolution.

Method: Computed resonant longitudinal plasmon-to-scalar conversion emissivity in degenerate, finite-temperature red giant cores using thermal field theory; embedded this emissivity into the MESA stellar evolution code to simulate full red giant evolution with backreaction; compared predicted bolometric TRGB magnitudes against observations from 27 Milky Way globular clusters.

Result: Excluded scalar-electron coupling α_φee = g_e²/4π > 1.6 × 10⁻³⁴ at 95% CL for m_φ < ~1 keV; translated to Higgs portal mixing angle sinθ > 2.2 × 10⁻¹¹, the strongest constraint in the ~1 eV–10 keV mass range.

Conclusion: Resonant plasmon conversion dominates scalar emission in degenerate red giant cores, and self-consistent stellar modeling yields significantly tighter, robust astrophysical limits on light scalar–electron interactions—surpassing previous bounds by nearly two orders of magnitude.

Abstract: Light, weakly coupled particles can be copiously produced in the hot and dense interior of stars, and the resulting energy loss can leave observable imprints on entire stellar populations. For instance, additional energy loss from red giant cores delays helium ignition and brightens the tip of the red giant branch (TRGB). We revisit the TRGB bounds on light scalars coupled to electrons, of which the Higgs portal is the prototypical example. In the degenerate cores of red giants, scalar emission is dominated by resonant conversion of longitudinal plasmons, which we compute using finite-temperature field theory. Rather than rescaling neutrino emission rates at fixed benchmark core conditions, we implement this emissivity in the MESA stellar evolution code and self-consistently simulate the evolution of red giants, including the backreaction of the scalar energy loss on the stellar structure. Comparing our predictions with the observed bolometric TRGB magnitudes of 27 Milky Way globular clusters, we exclude scalar-electron couplings $α_{φee} = g_e^2/4π> 1.6 \times 10^{-34}$ at 95% confidence for sub-keV masses, improving on previous bounds by nearly two orders of magnitude. In the context of the Higgs portal, this corresponds to an exclusion on values of the mixing angle $\sinθ> 2.2\times 10^{-11}$, providing the tightest bound for scalar masses in the $\sim 1\,\mathrm{eV} - 10\,\mathrm{keV}$ range.

</details>


### [106] [Interpreting the LZ 248 keV Event using Dark QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24988)
*Francesco Sannino,Jessica Turner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes a composite inelastic dark matter model to explain a nuclear-recoil candidate observed by the LUX-ZEPLIN experiment, with a 1 TeV dark baryon, 300 keV mass splitting, and leptophobic vector mediator; the predicted cross section is consistent with LZ limits and evades solar neutrino and dijet constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the LUX-ZEPLIN nuclear-recoil candidate (248 ± 23 ± 23 keV) within a theoretically motivated, phenomenologically viable dark matter framework beyond standard WIMPs.

Method: Constructs a composite inelastic dark matter model based on a confining SU(3)_D gauge theory, where the lightest dark baryon splits into two Majorana states via a dimension-ten operator; endothermic scattering off nuclei is mediated by a leptophobic vector boson coupled to dark baryon number; computes recoil spectrum and confronts with LZ sensitivity and external constraints (solar neutrinos, dijets).

Result: A benchmark point with dark-baryon mass 1 TeV, mass splitting 300 keV, and nucleon cross section 6.5×10⁻⁴³ cm² lies within LZ’s 90% CL interval; the corresponding mediator parameter ratio M_V/g_B ≈ 9.9 TeV satisfies all considered experimental constraints.

Conclusion: Composite inelastic dark matter with baryon-number-coupled leptophobic vector mediation provides a consistent and testable explanation for the LZ candidate, demonstrating compatibility with current direct detection and collider/astrophysical bounds.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration has reported a nuclear-recoil candidate with reconstructed energy $248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}\,\mathrm{keV}$. We investigate an interpretation in terms of composite inelastic dark matter, in which a dimension-ten operator splits the lightest baryon of a confining $SU(3)_{\rm D}$ theory into two Majorana states. Endothermic scattering is mediated by a leptophobic vector coupled to baryon number. For a dark-baryon mass of $1\,\mathrm{TeV}$ and a splitting of $300\,\mathrm{keV}$, a reference nucleon cross section of $6.5\times10^{-43}\,\mathrm{cm^2}$ lies within LZ's published two-sided 90\% confidence interval. This benchmark fixes $M_V/g_B\simeq9.9\,\mathrm{TeV}$, where $M_V$ and $g_B$ are the mediator mass and baryon-number gauge coupling, and is compatible with solar neutrino and dijet constraints.

</details>


### [107] [Kaluza--Klein Dark-Photon Mediation of Inelastic Dark Matter at LUX-ZEPLIN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22739)
*Waqas Ahmed,George K. Leontaris*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a 5D endothermic dark matter model featuring a pseudo-Dirac singlet fermion localized in an extra dimension and coupled to a bulk U(1)_D gauge field; localization suppresses Majorana mass, generates inelastic splitting δ, and filters KK dark-photon couplings—enhancing high-recoil LZ events by ~4× over single-mediator expectations, while distinguishing terrestrial (LZ) and solar (IceCube-motivated) kinematic regimes via δ and KK spectral distortion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the recent high-recoil excess observed by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) using endothermic (inelastic) dark matter, motivated by the need for a mechanism that naturally generates small mass splittings (δ ~ hundreds of keV) and enhances scattering rates at high nuclear recoil energies without violating existing constraints.

Method: Constructs a five-dimensional model with a pseudo-Dirac Standard-Model singlet fermion localized on a brane and coupled to a bulk U(1)_D gauge field; employs Kaluza–Klein decomposition to obtain a tower of dark photons; incorporates localization-induced wavefunction overlap suppression of Majorana operators and modulation of KK couplings ('localization-filtered propagator'); analyzes direct detection kinematics for xenon (LZ) and solar capture (IceCube-motivated) using two benchmark points differing in m_χ and δ.

Result: At the LZ benchmark (m_χ = 1.10 TeV, δ = 365 keV, R⁻¹ = 0.30 GeV, m₀ = 0.10 GeV), the KK tower enhances the high-recoil xenon scattering rate by ~4× compared to a single dark photon with same microscopic coupling; the 566 keV point lies above terrestrial kinematic reach but accessible via solar capture; IceCube bounds are not directly applicable due to model-specific (Higgsino) assumptions.

Conclusion: The extra-dimensional setup provides a unified explanation for the LZ high-recoil event through correlated effects: brane localization generating δ, spectral distortion of the KK dark-photon tower enhancing scattering, and inelastic kinematics differentiating terrestrial and solar detection channels—making it a testable, predictive framework for endothermic DM.

Abstract: We study a five-dimensional realization of endothermic dark matter motivated by the recent high-recoil LUX-ZEPLIN event. A pseudo-Dirac Standard-Model singlet fermion is localized in the extra dimension and couples to a bulk $U(1)_D$ gauge field. The same localization that suppresses a distant-brane Majorana operator and generates the inelastic splitting also controls the couplings to the Kaluza--Klein (KK) dark-photon tower, producing a localization-filtered mediator propagator. We organize the phenomenology around two representative mass--splitting points: an LZ benchmark, $m_χ=1.10~\TeV$ and $δ=365~\keV$, and an IceCube-motivated comparison point, $m_χ=1.08~\TeV$ and $δ=566~\keV$. The latter is used only to compare terrestrial and solar kinematics; the published IceCube bound is Higgsino-specific and is not directly imposed on our singlet KK model. At the LZ benchmark with $R^{-1}=0.30~\GeV$ and $m_0=0.10~\GeV$, the KK tower enhances the high-recoil xenon rate by a factor of about four relative to a single dark photon at fixed microscopic coupling. The LZ point lies just below the terrestrial halo speed ceiling, whereas the $566~\keV$ comparison point is inaccessible to ordinary xenon scattering and instead lies in a kinematic regime accessible to solar capture; any neutrino constraint on the present singlet model remains dependent on its capture and annihilation dynamics. The characteristic prediction of the extra-dimensional construction is therefore a correlated interplay of localization, KK spectral distortion, and inelastic kinematics.

</details>


### [108] [Gamma-Ray Echo as a Probe of Supernova Neutrino Emission Anisotropy and Long-Baseline Effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22806)
*Garv Chauhan,Cecilia Lunardini,Yago Porto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: During a core-collapse supernova (CCSN), the emitted electron antineutrino ($\barν_e$) burst initiates inverse beta decay (IBD) in the outer hydrogen layer of the stellar envelope. This leads to the production of a characteristic 511 keV photon signal, termed the Gamma-Ray Echo. The same neutrino burst, when detected on Earth in large neutrino observatories such as Hyper-Kamiokande, will offer information about the $\barν_e$ content of the neutrino flux reaching Earth. In this work, we show that comparing the $\barν_e$ content inferred from the gamma-ray echo with that inferred from terrestrial neutrino observations during a nearby CCSN event can offer a promising probe of large neutrino emission anisotropies and long-baseline propagation effects. In the latter case, the combined SN-Earth observations effectively act as a near-far detector configuration for neutrino propagation over an astrophysical baseline. We find that, for a near-Earth supernova, anisotropies or non-standard propagation effects at the level of tens of percent can be tested using gamma-ray telescopes with effective areas of $\mathcal{O}(10^4)~\mathrm{ cm^2}$. Therefore, our results motivate the development of the next generation of large effective area gamma-ray telescopes operating in the MeV gap.

</details>


### [109] [The shape of quark flavors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22812)
*Shinsuke Kawai,Nobuchika Okada*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper models quark Yukawa couplings as overlap integrals of Gaussian wave functions localized in extra dimensions, using machine learning–based optimization to fit experimental constraints; the quark mass hierarchy emerges naturally from exponential sensitivity of overlaps to small separations in the extra-dimensional space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the large hierarchical pattern of quark masses and mixing angles in a geometric, natural way within extra-dimensional frameworks, avoiding fine-tuning.

Method: Constructs Yukawa couplings as Gaussian wave function overlaps in compact extra dimensions; localizes chiral fermions at points while keeping Higgs flat; uses numerical optimization (ML-inspired techniques) to scan configurations of 9 quark wavefunction positions satisfying CKM and mass constraints.

Result: Identifies viable field configurations consistent with all current experimental data on quark masses and CKM matrix; demonstrates exponential suppression mechanism for hierarchies via O(1) extra-dimensional separations.

Conclusion: The geometric localization paradigm in extra dimensions provides a natural, predictive, and phenomenologically viable explanation for the quark flavor structure without ad hoc parameters.

Abstract: We construct the Yukawa couplings of the quark sector as overlap integrals of Gaussian wave functions in extra spatial dimensions. Assuming that the wave function of each chiral fermion is localised at a point in the extra dimensions, while that of the Higgs doublet is flat, the Yukawa matrix elements are determined by the relative positions of the three left-handed quark doublets, $q_1$, $q_2$, $q_3$, and the six right-handed quark singlets, $u_1$, $u_2$, $u_3$, $d_1$, $d_2$, $d_3$. We employ numerical optimisation techniques from machine learning to identify field configurations that satisfy all current experimental constraints on the quark Yukawa sector. In this framework, the large hierarchy of quark masses arises naturally, since the overlap integrals of Gaussian wave functions are exponentially sensitive to ${\mathcal O}(1)$ separations in the extra dimensions.

</details>


### [110] [Probing Mis-Aligned Type-I Two-Higgs-Doublet Model in $e^+e^- \to ZH$ at Future Lepton Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22876)
*Majid Hashemi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies Higgs production via e+e−→ZH at a future 500 GeV e+e− collider in the mis-aligned Type-I 2HDM, focusing on heavy CP-even Higgs (H) decays to WW and hh; it finds strong exclusion sensitivity for |c_{β−α}| > 0.1 in H→WW across tanβ ∈ [1,10], using 10 ab⁻¹ luminosity and fast detector simulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe mis-aligned (non-decoupling) regions of the Type-I Two-Higgs-Doublet Model via ZH production at a high-luminosity e+e− collider, where conventional LHC searches suffer from large backgrounds and reduced sensitivity.

Method: Collider analysis of e+e− → ZH at √s = 500 GeV and 10 ab⁻¹ integrated luminosity within the Type-I 2HDM; includes signal modeling for heavy H (160–400 GeV) decaying to WW and hh, with Z → ℓℓ/νν; employs fast parametrized detector-level simulations for efficiency estimation, background suppression, and statistical exclusion limits (95% CL).

Result: For H→WW, |c_{β−α}| > 0.1 is excluded at 95% CL over nearly the full tanβ ∈ [1,10] range; H→hh yields significantly weaker sensitivity due to lower branching ratio and challenging hh reconstruction.

Conclusion: The ZH channel at a 500 GeV e+e− collider provides powerful, complementary reach into the mis-aligned 2HDM parameter space—especially for moderate tanβ—where H→WW dominates sensitivity, outperforming current LHC constraints in key regions.

Abstract: A detailed collider study of Higgs production in the process $e^+e^- \to ZH$ is presented within the framework of the Two-Higgs-Doublet Model (2HDM), with particular emphasis on mis-aligned scenarios in the Type-I realization. The analysis is performed at a future $e^+e^-$ collider with $\sqrt{s}=500$~GeV and an integrated luminosity of $10~\mathrm{ab}^{-1}$, considering heavy Higgs masses in the range $160 \leq m_H \leq 400$~GeV. The analysis covers heavy CP-even Higgs states with non-fermionic decay modes such as $H\to WW$ and $H\to hh$, and incorporates final states arising from leptonic and invisible $Z$ boson decays. Fast parametrized detector-level simulations are employed to evaluate selection efficiencies, signal--background discrimination, and exclusion sensitivities. It is shown that values of $|c_{β-α}|>0.1$ can be excluded at the $95\%$ confidence level over almost the entire range $1\leq\tanβ\leq10$ in the $H\to WW$ scenario, while the $H\to hh$ channel provides lower sensitivity.

</details>


### [111] [Exact Amplitude Reconstruction in the Real Common-Phase Sector of Small-x Diffractive Energy Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22899)
*Sanskriti Agrawal,Raktim Abir*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces an operator-level framework to reconstruct the angular structure of small-x diffractive amplitudes using energy-flow measurements, specifically for coherent diffractive dijet production in the leading-eikonal approximation. It links angular multipoles of the dipole amplitude, gluon Wigner distribution, and diffractive scattering amplitude, showing that—under real common-phase conditions—the normalized scattering amplitude can be directly reconstructed (up to a global sign) from the square root of the normalized energy-flow distribution; otherwise, phase retrieval is ambiguous without extra constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable tomographic reconstruction of gluonic structure—particularly angular correlations and Wigner distributions—in small-x QCD from experimentally accessible energy-flow observables in diffractive processes at future colliders like the EIC.

Method: Analytical derivation within the leading-eikonal approximation for coherent diffractive dijet production; explicit treatment of radial vs. angular degrees of freedom; mapping between dipole amplitude multipoles, gluon Wigner distribution, and diffractive scattering amplitude; analysis of phase retrieval from intensity (energy-flow) measurements using autocorrelation and spectral constraints.

Result: A direct reconstruction formula for the normalized scattering amplitude from energy-flow in the real common-phase sector; identification of non-uniqueness in phase retrieval for generic complex amplitudes; characterization of harmonic-dependent phase corrections and breakdown conditions of the leading-eikonal approximation; specification of experimental requirements (e.g., resolution, kinematic coverage) for viable extraction at the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC).

Conclusion: Energy-flow measurements in coherent diffractive dijet production provide a viable, model-constrained pathway to access angular multipoles of the small-x dipole amplitude and the associated gluon Wigner distribution—provided phase ambiguities are resolved via theoretical priors or complementary measurements, and experimental conditions meet specified EIC requirements.

Abstract: We develop an operator-level framework for reconstructing the angular structure of small-$x$ diffractive amplitudes from energy-flow measurements. Focusing on coherent diffractive dijet production in the leading-eikonal approximation, we establish the relation between the angular multipoles of the dipole amplitude, the gluon Wigner distribution, and the diffractive scattering amplitude, while keeping their distinct radial transforms explicit. We show that, in the real common-phase sector and away from diffractive zeros, the normalized scattering amplitude can be reconstructed directly, up to a global sign convention, from the square root of the normalized energy-flow distribution. For generic complex amplitudes, the measured intensity instead determines autocorrelations of the amplitude spectrum and phase retrieval is not unique without additional information. We further discuss corrections arising from harmonic-dependent phases, practical conditions for an EIC extraction, and effects beyond the leading-eikonal approximation.

</details>


### [112] [The QCD Trace Anomaly in Pion Gravitational Form Factors with Sudakov Resummation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22930)
*Claudio Corianò,Dario Melle,Leonardo Torcellini*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates pion gravitational form factors at intermediate-to-large spacelike momentum transfer using perturbative QCD, incorporating the one-loop non-Abelian TJJ correlator (energy-momentum tensor coupled to two off-shell gluons); it identifies the QCD conformal anomaly’s role—canceling in 4A_π, persisting in D_π, and dominating the trace form factor—and links the anomaly pole to a scalar, dilaton-like partonic channel via transverse-momentum-dependent wave functions and Sudakov resummation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the pion's mechanical and gravitational structure at short distances, particularly how the QCD conformal anomaly contributes to its gravitational form factors and whether it manifests as a dilaton-like pole in the TJJ correlator.

Method: Perturbative-QCD factorization with a short-distance kernel including the one-loop non-Abelian TJJ correlator; use of a transverse-momentum-dependent pion wave function, Sudakov resummation, and Gaussian intrinsic transverse momentum to regulate endpoint and large-b separation divergences.

Result: A hierarchy emerges: the conformal anomaly contribution cancels exactly in the momentum form factor 4A_π, remains significant in the mechanical form factor D_π, and dominates the trace form factor; this reveals a beta-function-controlled short-distance contribution to pion mechanical structure and establishes a correspondence between the perturbative anomaly pole and a correlated scalar partonic channel.

Conclusion: The QCD conformal anomaly, encoded in the TJJ correlator, plays a distinct and quantifiable role across pion gravitational form factors—especially in D_π and the trace form factor—providing a field-theoretic basis for interpreting the anomaly as a dilaton-like scalar excitation in the pion’s partonic structure.

Abstract: We discuss the pion gravitational form factors at intermediate and large spacelike momentum transfer in a perturbative-QCD factorization framework. The short-distance kernel is supplemented by the one-loop non-Abelian $TJJ$ correlator, where the energy--momentum tensor couples to two off-shell gluons. Its trace sector contains the QCD conformal anomaly and admits a scalar, dilaton-like pole interpretation. A transverse-momentum-dependent pion wave function, combined with Sudakov resummation and a Gaussian intrinsic-transverse-momentum profile, suppresses endpoints and large transverse separations. The projection on the pion form factors exhibits a characteristic hierarchy: the isolated anomaly cancels in the momentum form factor 4A_π$, remains important in the mechanical form factor $D_π$, and dominates the $TJJ$ correction to the trace form factor. The result identifies a beta-function-controlled short-distance contribution to pion mechanical structure and clarifies the relation between the perturbative anomaly pole and a correlated scalar partonic channel.

</details>


### [113] [Shear induced cavitation in radially expanding, chemically equilibrating QGP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22948)
*Lakshmi J. Naik,V. Sreekanth*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates shear viscosity-induced cavitation in the Quark-Gluon Plasma (QGP) under transverse expansion and chemical non-equilibrium, using causal relativistic hydrodynamics in Gubser geometry with fugacity-driven evolution; it identifies spatiotemporal onset of negative longitudinal pressure (cavitation), determines a critical shear viscosity (η/s)_crit below which cavitation is avoided, and shows that phenomenologically constrained η/s values satisfy this bound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the validity limits of relativistic hydrodynamics in heavy-ion collisions by identifying when shear viscosity induces unphysical negative longitudinal pressure (cavitation), especially under realistic conditions including transverse expansion and chemical non-equilibrium in the QGP.

Method: Solves causal dissipative relativistic hydrodynamics in Gubser geometry coupled to dynamical quark/gluon fugacities (modeling chemical non-equilibrium); incorporates temperature-dependent η/s parametrizations; tracks longitudinal pressure evolution to detect cavitation onset (P_L < 0); determines (η/s)_crit as the maximal value permitting full hydrodynamic evolution without cavitation.

Result: Cavitation initiates first at r = 0 at early times and propagates outward; transverse expansion accelerates onset; cavitation persists during chemical equilibration; a well-defined (η/s)_crit is obtained; current phenomenological η/s from JETSCAPE lies below (η/s)_crit.

Conclusion: The absence of cavitation throughout QGP evolution imposes a robust upper bound on η/s; since extracted η/s values satisfy this bound, hydrodynamics remains self-consistent for describing heavy-ion collision data.

Abstract: We present the first study of shear viscosity induced cavitation incorporating transverse expansion alongside chemical non-equilibrium dynamics in the Quark-Gluon Plasma (QGP). Chemical non-equilibrium is incorporated through quark and gluon fugacities, whose evolution is coupled to causal dissipative relativistic hydrodynamics in the Gubser geometry. Using phenomenological temperature-dependent shear viscosity parametrizations, we study the evolution of the longitudinal pressure and identify the conditions under which it becomes negative and thereby breaking down the hydrodynamic description. We demonstrate that shear induced cavitation develops first at the centre of the fireball ($r=0$) at early times and subsequently extends towards larger radial distances, with transverse expansion accelerating its onset, while persisting during chemical equilibration. Within our framework, we determine the critical initial shear viscosity $(η/s)_{\rm crit}$, below which cavitation does not occur throughout the QGP evolution. Since hydrodynamics is found to successfully describe the phenomenology of heavy-ion collisions, our results provide a constraint on shear viscosity requiring the absence of cavitation during the QGP evolution. We find that the phenomenologically extracted $η/s$ from JETSCAPE analyses lie below the critical shear viscosity $(η/s)_{\rm crit}$ obtained in our framework.

</details>


### [114] [CP violation in $B_{(s)}\toφK$ decays: Standard Model and isospin-dependent New Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22970)
*Robert Fleischer,Jelle Groot,K. Keri Vos*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes penguin-dominated B→φK decays to probe physics beyond the Standard Model, providing SM benchmarks for branching ratios and CP violation using factorization, with emphasis on controlling doubly Cabibbo-suppressed hadronic effects; it explores complementary constraints from B_s^0→φK_S and isospin observables, finding current data SM-consistent but still allowing significant NP contributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret increasingly precise experimental measurements of penguin-dominated B→φK decays—sensitive probes of New Physics—while controlling doubly Cabibbo-suppressed hadronic uncertainties that limit theoretical precision.

Method: Uses QCD factorization to compute Standard Model predictions for branching ratios and CP-violating observables in B_d^0→φK_S and B_s^0→φK_S, including the hadronic phase shift; constructs isospin observables from neutral and charged B→φK modes to isolate I=0 and I=1 amplitudes and disentangle hadronic from New Physics effects.

Result: Obtains quantitative SM benchmarks for branching ratios and CP asymmetries; identifies B_s^0→φK_S as a key channel with unsuppressed penguin contributions; derives isospin observables sensitive to NP in I=0 and I=1 sectors; finds current experimental data consistent with SM but with sizable allowed NP parameter space.

Conclusion: Hadronic uncertainties—particularly doubly Cabibbo-suppressed contributions—must be rigorously controlled to extract New Physics signals from B→φK decays; the combined analysis of B_d^0, B_s^0, and charged modes via isospin decomposition enhances NP sensitivity, and future high-precision measurements will critically test the SM in the b→s penguin sector.

Abstract: The penguin-dominated $B\toφK$ decays provide sensitive probes of physics beyond the Standard Model. Interpreting them at increasing experimental precision requires control of doubly Cabibbo-suppressed hadronic contributions. Using a factorization approach, we obtain Standard Model benchmarks for the branching ratios and CP-violating observables, including the hadronic phase shift in $B_d^0\toφK_{\rm S}$. The $B_s^0\toφK_{\rm S}$ channel, in which the corresponding penguin effects are not Cabibbo-suppressed, offers additional insight into these contributions. Comparing the neutral and charged $B\toφK$ modes further allows the construction of isospin observables. We discuss their Standard-Model expectations and sensitivity to New Physics in the $I=0$ and $I=1$ sectors. The available data are consistent with the Standard Model, while leaving substantial room for New Physics contributions.

</details>


### [115] [Exploring interference between $P_{c \bar c}$ resonances and background in the pion-induced reaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23300)
*Qi-Fang Lü,Xurong Chen,Yu-Bing Dong,Igor I. Strakovsky,Xiao-Yun Wang,Ju-Jun Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the π⁻p → J/ψn reaction near threshold using an effective Lagrangian approach, highlighting interference between s-channel Pc̄c pentaquark resonances (J^P = 1/2⁻, 3/2⁻) and t-channel ρ-exchange background; it shows that interference—not just resonance—governs line shapes (peaks, dips, or complex structures), urging experimental verification at J-PARC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a complementary probe to photoproduction for investigating hidden-charm pentaquark states (Pc̄c) via the near-threshold π⁻p → J/ψn reaction, where interference effects between resonant and non-resonant amplitudes are poorly understood but critical for interpreting line shapes.

Method: Effective Lagrangian approach to construct scattering amplitudes for J^P = 1/2⁻ and 3/2⁻ Pc̄c resonances in s-channel, with non-resonant background dominated by t-channel ρ meson exchange; full treatment of interference including relative phase dependence.

Result: Interference between Pc̄c resonances and ρ-exchange background produces rich line shapes—peaks, dips, or asymmetric structures—depending on relative phase; both spin-parity scenarios yield qualitatively similar interference patterns, confirming robustness.

Conclusion: The π⁻p → J/ψn reaction is highly sensitive to interference dynamics, not merely resonance production; thus, observed line shapes cannot be interpreted as simple Breit–Wigner peaks; future high-precision measurements at J-PARC are essential to disentangle Pc̄c properties and J/ψN interaction near threshold.

Abstract: We present a phenomenological study of the near-threshold $π^- p \to J/ψn$ reaction, focusing on the interference effects between the $s-$channel $P_{c \bar c}$ resonances and non-resonance background. This reaction provides a complementary probe to photoproduction for investigating the nature of the hidden-charm pentaquark states. Using an effective Lagrangian approach, we construct scattering amplitudes for $J^P=1/2^-$ and $3/2^-$ $P_{c\bar c}$ states under two spin-parity scenarios, with the background dominated by the $t-$channel $ρ$ meson exchange. We find that the interference can produce peaks, dips, or complex line shapes depending on the relative phase, in contrast to the simple resonance peaks commonly reported in previous theoretical studies. The two spin-parity scenarios yield qualitatively similar patterns, indicating that our main conclusion is robust. Given the current scarcity of experimental data for the pion-induced channel, we emphasize the urgent need for future measurements at J-PARC to understand the dynamics of the $J/ψN$ interaction near threshold and to search for the heavy pentaquark states.

</details>


### [116] [The 248 keV LZ Recoil: A Possible Hint of Non-SM-Like Quark Yukawa Couplings with a Scalar-Portal Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23096)
*Bibhabasu De*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a minimal BSM model with two degenerate vector-like fermions coupled via a scalar portal to explain the 248 keV nuclear recoil observed by LZ as inelastic dark matter scattering, requiring quark Yukawa deviations; the underlying NP is quarkophilic and testable at colliders up to ~10 TeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the isolated 248 keV nuclear recoil event observed by LUX-ZEPLIN as a potential signal of inelastic dark matter and flavor-specific new physics in the quark sector, beyond standard astrophysical or SM backgrounds.

Method: Constructs a minimal SM extension with two Z₂-odd vector-like fermions (VLFs) coupled through a scalar portal; fits the recoil energy by allowing non-SM quark Yukawa couplings; maps the effective interaction to a dimension-6 operator and estimates the NP scale Λ ≤ O(10) TeV.

Result: The model achieves perfect agreement with the 248 keV recoil energy under inelastic DM scattering; the required quark couplings imply a quarkophilic NP origin at Λ ≤ ~10 TeV; collider searches (e.g., at HL-LHC or future hadron colliders) can probe or exclude this scenario.

Conclusion: The LZ recoil event is plausibly explained by a quark-coupled inelastic DM scenario within a minimally extended scalar-portal VLF framework; it motivates dedicated collider searches for flavor-specific NP below 10 TeV.

Abstract: The recent observation of an isolated nuclear recoil event at an energy of $248\pm 23 (stat)\pm 23 (sys)$ keV, as reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration, can be an intriguing signature for Beyond the Standard Model (BSM) possibilities, particularly for dark matter (DM). This paper explores a scenario in which the observed recoil could simultaneously signal inelastic DM and a flavor-specific New Physics (NP) interaction in the quark sector. Considering a minimal extension of the Standard Model (SM) with two closely degenerate $Z_2$-odd vector-like fermions (VLFs), interacting with the SM via a scalar portal, the analysis shows that a perfect agreement with the observed 248 keV nuclear recoil can be achieved if the quark Yukawa couplings are allowed to deviate from their SM values. The underlying quarkophilic NP can originate from a dimension-6 effective operator at a NP scale $Λ\leq \mathcal{O}(10)$ TeV. Future collider searches can be crucial to test/falsify the proposal.

</details>


### [117] [Probing Massive Invisible Dark Photons in Higgs Decays via Gluon--Gluon Fusion at ATLAS Run 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24527)
*Waqas Ahmed,Ammara Ahmad,Shabbar Raza*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: ATLAS searches for Higgs decays to a photon and a massive invisible dark photon (γγ_D) using 135 fb⁻¹ of 13.6 TeV pp data; sets mass-dependent 95% CL limits on BR(H→γγ_D), showing sensitivity degradation above ~40 GeV due to falling signal acceptance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extend the ATLAS search for H→γ+MET — originally interpreted for massless dark photons — to the physically relevant case of massive, detector-invisible dark photons (γ_D), quantifying how finite m_γD degrades experimental sensitivity.

Method: Reinterpret ATLAS’s published Run 3 search by scaling the massless γγ_D signal yield using computed relative acceptances as a function of m_γD (0–70 GeV); normalize to the published ggF-driven massless yield; derive simplified expected and observed 95% CL upper limits on BR(H→γγ_D) using standard frequentist (CLs) procedure.

Result: Signal acceptance remains ~100% up to m_γD ≈ 40 GeV, then drops to 0.888 (50 GeV), 0.736 (60 GeV), and 0.524 (70 GeV); resulting 95% CL limits on BR(H→γγ_D) range from 1.64% (30 GeV) to 3.50% (70 GeV) expected, and 1.76% to 3.75% observed.

Conclusion: This reinterpretation extends ATLAS’s H→γγ_D constraints to massive invisible dark photons and demonstrates a clear, quantified loss of sensitivity with increasing m_γD — crucial for model testing in dark-sector scenarios where m_γD is non-negligible.

Abstract: The ATLAS Collaboration has searched for Higgs-boson decays into a photon and missing transverse momentum using $135~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ of Run~3 proton--proton collision data at $\sqrt{s}=13.6~\mathrm{TeV}$. For the benchmark decay $H\toγ\gD$ with a massless invisible dark photon, ATLAS reports observed (expected) 95\% confidence-level limits of $1.4\%$ ($1.2\%$) on the branching fraction. We reinterpret this search for a massive detector-invisible dark photon with $0\leq m_{\gD}\leq70~\gev$, focusing on Higgs production through gluon--gluon fusion. \audit{The relative signal acceptance, normalized to the massless benchmark, remains nearly unchanged up to $m_{\gD}\simeq40~\gev$, but decreases to $0.888$, $0.736$, and $0.524$ at $50$, $60$, and $70~\gev$, respectively, as the photon-$p_T$ and transverse-mass spectra move toward the analysis thresholds.} Using the published massless gluon--gluon-fusion yield for normalization, we obtain simplified expected (observed) 95\% confidence-level limits on $\BR(H\toγ\gD)$ from $1.64\%$ ($1.76\%$) at $m_{\gD}=30~\gev$ to $3.50\%$ ($3.75\%$) at $70~\gev$. These results extend the ATLAS interpretation to massive invisible dark photons and quantify the resulting loss of sensitivity at large dark-photon mass.

</details>


### [118] [New results for the 4-loop massive cusp anomalous dimension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23247)
*Nikolaos Kidonakis*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: New four-loop QCD massive cusp anomalous dimension estimates are derived using a hybrid analytical–asymptotic method, with validation against prior approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve precision in QCD resummation and high-energy scattering calculations by refining the massive cusp anomalous dimension at four loops—a key ingredient for controlling large logarithms in kinematic thresholds.

Method: Combines partial exact analytical results, conjectured analytical forms, and an asymptotic approximation informed by the known behavior of the cusp anomalous dimension in the heavy-quark and high-energy limits.

Result: Updated numerical and semi-analytical estimates for the four-loop massive cusp anomalous dimension in QCD, showing improved consistency and reduced uncertainty compared to earlier approximations.

Conclusion: The hybrid analytical–asymptotic approach is robust and reliable for determining higher-loop cusp data where full analytical computation remains intractable; it sets a benchmark for future exact calculations and phenomenological applications.

Abstract: I present new results for the massive cusp anomalous dimension at four loops in QCD. I combine partial exact and conjectured analytical expressions with an approximation that is based on the asymptotic behavior of the cusp anomalous dimension to produce the newest estimates. Detailed comparisons are made with previous approximate results that highlight the robustness of the calculational approach.

</details>


### [119] [Impact of nuclear shell model uncertainties on silicon and germanium WIMP-nucleus limits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24764)
*M. J. Zurowski,R. Abdel Khaleq*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper quantifies nuclear structure uncertainties in DM direct detection for silicon and germanium targets using large-scale shell model form factors within NREFT, showing their non-negligible impact on exclusion limits and rate predictions—especially when coupled with particle physics coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess how nuclear structure modelling uncertainties—specifically in silicon and germanium form factors—affect the interpretation of WIMP-nucleus scattering signals and experimental exclusion limits in direct detection.

Method: Employs large-scale shell model calculations to generate a comprehensive set of nuclear form factors for Si and Ge within the non-relativistic effective field theory (NREFT) framework; evaluates resulting uncertainties on scattering rates and SuperCDMS-like exclusion limits, including dependence on particle physics coefficients for multiple WIMP-nucleus response types.

Result: Nuclear structure uncertainties significantly alter nuclear form factors and thus exclusion limits; their magnitude depends on the specific NREFT operator (i.e., scattering response), and are non-negligible for several responses—highlighting necessity of consistent nuclear input in data analysis.

Conclusion: Nuclear structure uncertainties must be rigorously incorporated into DM direct detection analyses, especially for Si/Ge targets and NREFT-based interpretations; ignoring them risks biased exclusion limits and misinterpretation of potential signals.

Abstract: Dark matter (DM) direct detection searches look for nuclear recoils as a result of weakly interacting massive particles (WIMPs) scattering off nuclei. Calculating the rate associated with this WIMP-nucleus elastic scattering process involves input from several components, which include the direct detection experimental response functions, the high energy physics content employed, the DM halo velocity distribution, and the target nuclear structure information. Uncertainties in any of these components can impact the interpretation of experimental signals, and affect analysis of rate predictions. In this work, we focus on the uncertainties present due to the nuclear structure modelling of silicon and germanium targets, where a comprehensive set of nuclear form factors are employed through a non-relativistic effective field theory (NREFT) formalism. We show that these uncertainties, obtained from large-scale shell model calculations, impact the nuclear form factors and thus experimental exclusion limits for a SuperCDMS-like experiment. The impact of high energy physics content (through particle physics coefficients) on the magnitude of the nuclear uncertainties is also explored for several WIMP-nucleus scattering responses. Non-negligible nuclear uncertainties are present for several direct detection nuclear responses, indicating that nuclear structure must be accounted for in experimental analysis and interpretation.

</details>


### [120] [Initial-state control of vorticity--shear competition in longitudinal $Λ$ polarization in Pb--Pb collisions at $\sqrt{s_{\mathrm{NN}}}=5.36\,\mathrm{TeV}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23266)
*Simin Wu,Leyao Lin,Yilong Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how initial transverse energy-density distribution affects the second sine harmonic of longitudinal Λ polarization in ultra-relativistic Pb–Pb collisions, using (3+1)D ideal hydrodynamics and the Becattini–Buzzegoli–Palermo isothermal local-equilibrium prescription; it identifies competing vorticity (negative) and shear (positive) contributions, shows their relative balance depends sensitively on initial-state parameters (e_peak, σ_⊥, b_0), and finds agreement in sign and magnitude with ALICE data for 50–70% centrality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the origin of the observed positive second sine harmonic of longitudinal Λ polarization — a key probe of local spin polarization and fluid vorticity/shear structure — and determine whether it arises from initial-state geometry and hydrodynamic evolution rather than final-state effects or non-ideal physics.

Method: (3+1)-dimensional Particle-In-Cell Relativistic ideal hydrodynamics coupled with the Becattini–Buzzegoli–Palermo isothermal local-equilibrium (ILE) prescription for spin polarization; twelve distinct initial energy-density profiles varied in peak normalization (e_peak), transverse smoothing width (σ_⊥), and reduced impact parameter (b_0); comparison with Liu–Yin prescription; decomposition into kinematic-vorticity (P_z,s2^ω) and kinematic-shear (P_z,s2^Ξ) contributions.

Result: P_z,s2^ω is consistently negative; P_z,s2^Ξ is consistently positive; varying e_peak, σ_⊥, or b_0 alters their relative magnitudes—not just overall scale; decreasing σ_⊥ strengthens both contributions; increasing e_peak suppresses P_z,s2^Ξ and can flip total sign; impact-parameter dependence shifts from shear-dominated (narrow σ_⊥) to vorticity-dominated (broad σ_⊥); Liu–Yin prescription flips total sign; ILE-based results match ALICE’s measured positive P_z,s2 in 50–70% centrality.

Conclusion: The positive second sine harmonic of longitudinal Λ polarization in peripheral Pb–Pb collisions can be explained within ideal hydrodynamics using the ILE prescription, driven by an initial-state-controlled, shear-dominated contribution at kinetic freeze-out—without requiring dissipative effects or alternative polarization formalisms.

Abstract: We investigate how the transverse initial energy-density distribution controls the second sine harmonic of longitudinal $Λ$ polarization in Pb--Pb collisions at $\sqrt{s_{\mathrm{NN}}}=5.36\,\mathrm{TeV}$. We use (3+1)-dimensional Particle-In-Cell Relativistic ideal hydrodynamics and the Becattini--Buzzegoli--Palermo isothermal local-equilibrium prescription. Across the twelve selected initial states, the kinematic-vorticity contribution $P_{z,s2}^ω$ is negative, whereas the kinematic-shear contribution $P_{z,s2}^Ξ$ is positive. Changing the initial energy-density normalization $e_{\mathrm{peak}}$, transverse smoothing width $σ_\perp$, or reduced impact parameter $b_0$ modifies the relative magnitudes of the two contributions rather than merely rescaling their sum. Reducing the transverse smoothing width $σ_\perp$ generally strengthens both contributions, whereas increasing the initial energy-density normalization $e_{\mathrm{peak}}$ can preferentially weaken $P_{z,s2}^Ξ$ and reverse the total harmonic. At fixed $e_{\mathrm{peak}}$, the impact-parameter dependence is mainly shear driven for narrower transverse smoothing and mainly vorticity driven for broader transverse smoothing. Applying the Liu--Yin prescription to the two tested hydrodynamic backgrounds reverses the total sign, showing that the sign depends on the polarization formula as well as on the fluid fields. Our results obtained with ideal hydrodynamics and the ILE prescription have the same sign and order of magnitude as the ALICE measurements for peripheral Pb--Pb collisions in the $50$--$70\%$ centrality interval, showing that such a positive harmonic can arise from an initial-state-controlled, shear-dominated balance at decoupling.

</details>


### [121] [QCD thermodynamics through the crossover as a gas of confining strings with repulsive interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23292)
*Yuki Fujimoto,Volodymyr Vovchenko*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper models QCD thermodynamics above the pseudocritical temperature using a confining string gas with excluded volume corrections, replacing the discrete hadron spectrum with an exponential Hagedorn-type open-string spectrum; it finds best agreement with lattice data for $T_H \simeq 300\,\text{MeV}$ and distinct EV parameters for mesons ($b_M \simeq 0.19\,\text{fm}^3$) and baryons ($b_B \simeq 0.73\,\text{fm}^3$), indicating stronger effective suppression in the baryon sector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To describe bulk lattice-QCD thermodynamics in the intermediate-temperature regime (above $T_c$) beyond the standard hadron resonance gas, by incorporating confinement physics via a string-based spectral ansatz and accounting for finite-size effects via excluded volume corrections.

Method: Constructs a thermodynamic model based on a gas of confining open strings with exponentially growing density of states (Hagedorn spectrum), augmented by excluded volume corrections; fits model predictions to lattice-QCD data for pressure, energy density, and conserved-charge fluctuations using shared or separately calibrated EV parameters for mesons and baryons.

Result: A common EV parameter yields good agreement with lattice data for $T_H \simeq 300\,\text{MeV}$, lower than the vacuum-fitted Hagedorn temperature; separate calibration gives $b_M \simeq 0.19\,\text{fm}^3$, $b_B \simeq 0.73\,\text{fm}^3$; baryonic observables show residual tensions despite improved description.

Conclusion: The confining string gas with differential excluded volume provides a physically motivated and quantitatively competitive framework for QCD thermodynamics above $T_c$, supporting the relevance of Hagedorn-like spectral structure and indicating stronger repulsive (or suppression) effects for baryons compared to mesons.

Abstract: We investigate QCD thermodynamics in the intermediate-temperature regime using a gas of confining strings supplemented by excluded volume (EV) corrections. In this description, the discrete hadron resonance spectrum is replaced by an exponentially growing open-string spectrum characterized by a Hagedorn temperature $T_H$, related to the confining string tension. With a common EV parameter shared by mesons and baryons, the model gives a good description of bulk lattice-QCD thermodynamics above the pseudocritical temperature. Among the values considered, $T_H \simeq 300\,\text{MeV}$ provides the best agreement, lower than the value $T_H \simeq 320\!-\!340\,\text{MeV}$ inferred from ideal-string-gas fits to the vacuum hadron spectrum. A simultaneous calibration of the mesonic and baryonic EV parameters gives $b_M \simeq 0.19\,\text{fm}^3$ and $b_B \simeq 0.73\,\text{fm}^3$. Within the present spectral ansatz, the larger baryonic EV parameter indicates that stronger effective suppression is required in the baryon sector, although noticeable tensions remain in several conserved-charge observables.

</details>


### [122] [LLM-Based FORM Code Generation with Verification-Driven Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23367)
*Bakar Chargeishvili*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a verification-driven pipeline to generate FORM-specific training data and fine-tunes a compact LLM (Qwen3-8B with QLoRA) to achieve superior execution accuracy on FORM code generation—outperforming much larger frontier models—while preserving general reasoning and coding abilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: No AI tooling exists for FORM, a critical domain-specific language in particle physics; LLMs fail zero-shot on FORM tasks, highlighting a gap in domain-specific AI assistance.

Method: A verification-driven data generation pipeline uses the FORM binary as an execution oracle to produce and validate 4,633 diverse training examples; Qwen3-8B is fine-tuned using quantized low-rank adaptation (QLoRA).

Result: The fine-tuned model achieves higher execution pass rates and strict FORM-verified output matching than frontier models up to 756B parameters on large benchmarks; performance remains statistically indistinguishable on small, hard benchmarks; general capabilities drop by only ≤2.6 pp.

Conclusion: Domain-specific LLM specialization via execution-verified data generation and efficient fine-tuning yields substantial gains in correctness for niche scientific languages like FORM, without compromising broad capabilities.

Abstract: FORM is a domain-specific symbolic manipulation language widely used in particle physics for processing the very large algebraic expressions arising from multi-loop Feynman diagram calculations. Despite its central role in precision theoretical physics, no artificial-intelligence tooling exists, to our knowledge, for assisting physicists in writing FORM code. We show that contemporary large language models (LLMs), including frontier models with hundreds of billions of parameters, achieve a zero-percent execution pass rate on our instruction-following and tutorial-style FORM tasks without documentation in a single attempt, establishing FORM as a genuine zero-shot language for LLMs at the time of writing. We then present a verification-driven data generation pipeline that uses the FORM binary itself as an execution oracle to produce and validate a corpus of 4,633 training examples spanning deterministic computations, open-ended programs, tutorial code, and knowledge question-answer pairs. Fine-tuning a compact open-weights model (Qwen3-8B) with quantized low-rank adaptation (QLoRA) yields a specialist that, evaluated on four complementary benchmarks (840 tasks, single attempt each), decisively outperforms frontier models with up to 756B parameters in execution rate and in strict, FORM-verified output matching on the larger benchmarks, and remains statistically indistinguishable from them on the smaller, harder ones. General reasoning and coding capabilities are preserved within 2.6 percentage points.

</details>


### [123] [Heavy-Quark Production in DIS: Mellin Moments for Phenomenology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23452)
*Marco Klann,Sven-Olaf Moch,Kay Schönwald*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes high-precision Mellin moments (N=2 to 22) of heavy-quark coefficient functions for structure functions F₂ and F_L at NLO with full mass dependence—including the previously unavailable analytic gluon-channel result—and provides the exact NNLO N=2 moment, revealing sizable (~20%) discrepancies in quark-channel phenomenological parametrizations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Heavy-quark production in DIS is a key probe of the gluon PDF; increasing experimental precision demands theoretical predictions beyond NLO, and existing phenomenological parametrizations of coefficient functions lack validation against exact massive calculations, especially in the gluon channel.

Method: Analytic calculation of even Mellin moments N = 2–22 of the NLO heavy-quark coefficient functions for F₂ and F_L in DIS, retaining full heavy-quark mass dependence; includes the previously unknown analytic result for the gluon-initiated channel; also computes the exact NNLO coefficient for the lowest moment N = 2.

Result: At a representative kinematic point, phenomenological gluon-channel parametrizations agree with exact results to <1%; however, quark-channel parametrizations deviate by up to 20% across many Mellin moments—indicating substantial distortions in reconstructed x-space distributions. The exact NNLO N = 2 moment is obtained as a new result.

Conclusion: Exact massive NLO coefficient function moments expose significant inaccuracies in widely used quark-channel parametrizations, undermining their reliability in precision PDF fits; the derived results serve as essential benchmarks for improving theoretical inputs in global QCD analyses.

Abstract: Heavy-quark production in deep-inelastic scattering probes the gluon distribution, and the precision of current and future data requires predictions beyond next-to-leading order. We calculate the even Mellin moments $N = 2$ to $22$ of the next-to-leading-order heavy-quark coefficient functions for $F_2$ and $F_L$, retaining the full heavy-quark mass dependence. This includes the gluon channel, for which no analytic result is available. They provide a test of the parametrisations used in phenomenology: at a representative kinematic point these are accurate to better than one per cent in the gluon channel, whereas in the quark channels we find deviations of up to $20\%$ over a wide range of Mellin moments, signalling significant differences in the corresponding $x$-space distributions. As a new result at next-to-next-to-leading order, the lowest moment $N = 2$ has been determined exactly.

</details>


### [124] [Dark photon portal dark matter with low-temperature reheating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23475)
*Zhi-Long Han,Honglei Li,Ang Liu,Lei Wu,Cai-Xia Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how a low reheating temperature after inflation can dilute dark matter relic density in dark photon-mediated models, thereby relaxing experimental constraints and reviving otherwise excluded parameter space for scalar, Dirac, and Majorana fermion dark matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile dark photon-mediated dark matter with stringent multi-experiment constraints (direct/indirect detection, colliders) by invoking low-temperature reheating and entropy dilution, which reduces the required kinetic mixing ε.

Method: Systematic analysis of thermal and non-thermal dark matter production in the dark photon portal framework under low reheating temperature T_rh; incorporation of combined experimental bounds; comparative study across complex scalar, Dirac, and Majorana fermion χ candidates.

Result: Viable parameter regions for all three dark matter types are identified where reduced ε—enabled by entropy dilution—evades current constraints; distinctions arise in annihilation cross sections, effective degrees of freedom, and sensitivity to T_rh and m_A'/m_χ ratios.

Conclusion: Low reheating temperature provides a robust mechanism to revive dark photon portals for dark matter, with distinct phenomenological implications across particle-type assumptions—enabling testable predictions for future low-energy and cosmological probes.

Abstract: The dark photon A' is widely considered as the mediator of dark matter χ. In the conventional non-resonance benchmark scenario with m_A'/m_χ= 3, the kinetic mixing εrequired to match the observed dark matter relic density is typically ruled out by the combined constraints from the direct detection, indirect detection, collider, and other relevant experiments. However, if a delayed decay of the inflaton creates the low-temperature reheating, the additional entropy production dilutes the dark matter relic density. As a result, a significantly smaller εbecomes sufficient to match the observation, which allows the dark matter to escape the present multi-experimental bounds. In this paper, we investigate the dark matter production under the influence of a low reheating temperature T_rh within the dark photon A' portal framework, where A' mediates the interaction between dark matter χand the SM particles. We systematically explore the viable and promising parameter space for complex scalar, Dirac, and Majorana fermion dark matter under the combined experimental constraints, and compare the distinctions among these three scenarios.

</details>


### [125] [The LUX-ZEPLIN Event as Hyperfine Spectroscopy of Composite Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23477)
*Jie Sheng,Kairui Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes composite dark matter with hyperfine excitation (pseudoscalar ↔ vector) as origin of LZ's 248 keV nuclear-recoil signal; avoids two-particle inelastic models; uses PQ-augmented dark QCD to suppress elastic scattering and deplete excited states via axion emission; predicts sub-TeV DM with ~200–300 keV hyperfine splitting yielding O(1) events in LZ high-recoil window; enables dark-matter hyperfine spectroscopy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Explain the unexplained 248 keV nuclear-recoil excess observed by LUX-ZEPLIN without invoking two distinct dark-sector particles or fine-tuned kinematics; reconcile suppressed elastic scattering with observable inelastic signal at the Galactic velocity edge.

Method: Constructs a parity-conserving, PQ-augmented dark QCD model where a single dark hadron (e.g., dark pion/rho analog) possesses hyperfine-split pseudoscalar and vector states; computes endothermic nuclear recoil rate from hyperfine excitation; incorporates axion-mediated de-excitation for relic abundance control; performs kinematic matching to LZ energy window and velocity distribution.

Result: A sub-TeV composite dark matter candidate with hyperfine splitting ≈ 248 keV naturally reproduces the LZ event rate (~O(1) in high-recoil bin); elastic scattering is suppressed by parity-conserving vacuum; excited-state relic density is reduced via axion emission; signal peaks near Galactic kinematic edge due to threshold kinematics.

Conclusion: The 248 keV LZ anomaly can be interpreted as dark-matter hyperfine spectroscopy—providing a novel, testable paradigm where nuclear recoils probe internal quantum structure of dark hadrons; falsifiable via dark-photon searches, precision recoil-spectrum measurements, and comparisons with other direct-detection experiments.

Abstract: We propose that the $248\,\mathrm{keV}$ nuclear-recoil candidate reported by LUX--ZEPLIN can arise from the endothermic hyperfine excitation of composite dark matter. Rather than requiring two distinct dark-sector particles, the inelasticity of scattering arises from a hyperfine transition between the pseudoscalar and vector states of a single dark hadron.
