In a PQ-augmented dark QCD framework, the parity-conserving vacuum suppresses dangerous elastic scattering, while axion emission depletes the relic excited-state population. Endothermic excitation near the Galactic kinematic edge then naturally produces a localized high-energy recoil peak. We find that sub-TeV-scale dark matter with a hyperfine splitting of a few hundred keV can yield an order-one event rate in the LZ high-recoil window. This interpretation offers a possible form of dark-matter hyperfine spectroscopy, with complementary tests from dark-photon searches and future recoil-spectrum measurements.

</details>


### [126] [Investigation of the difference in the angular distributions of $Z\to \ell^{+}\ell^{-}$ events produced in quark-antiquark, quark-gluon and gluon-gluon collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23552)
*Arie Bodek,Giulia-Maria Bulugean,Aran Garcia-Bellido,Hyon San Seo,Rhys Taus,Un-Ki Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies angular distributions of Z boson decay leptons in pp collisions at 13 TeV, focusing on process-dependent differences (q-qbar, q-g, g-g) in Collins-Soper frame coefficients A₀ and A₂, using POWHEG-minnlops; proposes experimental strategies for disentangling contributions via rapidity, p_T, and jet multiplicity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Z boson production at the LHC is dominated by quark-gluon processes, while the textbook q-qbar channel contributes only ~40% of the cross section; understanding process-specific angular distributions is crucial for QCD dynamics and precision electroweak measurements.

Method: Uses POWHEG-minnlops event generator to simulate pp → γ*/Z → ℓ⁺ℓ⁻ events, separately analyzing quark-antiquark, quark-gluon, and gluon-gluon initiated subprocesses; computes angular coefficients A₀ and A₂ from θ_CS and φ_CS distributions in the Collins-Soper frame as functions of Z rapidity, transverse momentum, and final-state jet multiplicity.

Result: Significant differences are found in A₀ and A₂ across subprocesses—especially in forward rapidity and low-p_T regions—and jet multiplicity provides strong discrimination power; q-g and g-g contributions exhibit distinct angular modulation patterns compared to q-qbar.

Conclusion: Process-dependent angular coefficients offer a novel handle to isolate QCD-initiated Z production; experimental measurement of A₀ and A₂ differentially in rapidity, p_T, and jet multiplicity can constrain partonic dynamics and improve theoretical modeling of Z + jets.

Abstract: Measurements of the angular distributions of Z boson-decay leptons at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) are of interest in QCD studies and precision electroweak measurements. Z boson production at the LHC is dominated by quark-gluon processes and the quark-antiquark process accounts for only 40\% of the cross section. We investigate the theoretical predictions (using the POWHEG-minnlops event generator) for the difference in the angular distributions of $ pp \to γ^{*}/Z \to \ell^+\ell^-$ ($ μ^+μ^-$ or $e^{+}e^{-}$) events produced via quark-antiquark, quark-gluon , and gluon-gluon processes. The study is done for proton-proton collisions at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) at $\sqrt{s} = 13$ TeV. We investigate the angular coefficients $A_0$ and $A_2$ (for $θ_{CS}$ and $φ_{CS}$ distributions in the Collins-Soper frame) for the different processes as a function of the Z boson rapidity, transverse momentum and final state jet multiplicity, and propose how these can be investigated experimentally at the LHC.

</details>


### [127] [Toponium Production at Future Muon Colliders and in heavy ion ultraperipheral collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23576)
*Chun-Yuan Li,Xiao Liang,Xing-Hua Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies toponium (tt̄ bound state) production via inclusive/exclusive channels in NRQCD at muon colliders and Pb-Pb ultraperipheral collisions; using a Coulomb-plus-linear potential, it computes |R_tt̄(0)|² = 2.507 GeV³ and finds cross sections and event yields too low for feasible detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess experimental feasibility of observing toponium — a short-lived tt̄ bound state — in high-energy environments where QCD binding effects may be probed, specifically at future muon colliders and Pb--Pb UPCs.

Method: Employs non-relativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization; solves the Schrödinger equation with a Coulomb-plus-linear confining potential to determine the wavefunction at origin |R_tt̄(0)|²; computes inclusive and exclusive production cross sections and kinematic distributions for ^1S_0 and ^3S_1 toponium states.

Result: Obtained |R_tt̄(0)|² = 2.507 GeV³; calculated differential and total cross sections; estimated event yields based on projected integrated luminosities; all indicate extremely low statistics.

Conclusion: Toponium production in both muon colliders and Pb--Pb UPCs is predicted to have negligible event yields under realistic luminosity assumptions, rendering its experimental observation highly unlikely with current or near-future capabilities.

Abstract: In the framework of non-relativistic quantum chromodynamics (NRQCD), we systematically investigate the inclusive and exclusive production processes of spin-singlet \(^1S_0\) and spin-triplet \(^3S_1\) toponium at future muon colliders and in Pb--Pb ultraperipheral collisions (Pb--Pb UPC). Using the Coulomb-plus-linear potential and solving the Schrödinger equation, we obtain the wave function at the origin as $|R_{t\bar{t}}(0)|^2 = 2.507\ \text{GeV}^3$. The scattering cross sections and various kinematic distributions for two types of processes are calculated, and the corresponding event yields are estimated based on the expected integrated luminosities. Our study indicates that the detection prospects for toponium production through the aforementioned processes are unfavorable.

</details>


### [128] [LZ-Motivated Pseudo-Dirac Higgsinos in the Supersymmetric 331 Model from the Supersymmetric \texorpdfstring{$SU(6)$}{SU(6)} GUT Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23691)
*Imtiaz Khan,Ali Muhammad,G. Mustafa,Farruh Atamurotov,Ahmadjon Abdujabbarov,Mussawir Khan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a supersymmetric 331 model derived from an SU(6) GUT to explain the high-recoil event observed by LUX-ZEPLIN, focusing on sub-MeV inelastic neutral electroweakino splittings; it computes the tree-level Higgsino mass splitting without ad hoc bino mass terms, identifies a continuous parameter locus satisfying the signal, and cross-compares with alternative interpretations (SU(5), singlino-assisted, etc.).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous high-recoil event reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment via a light, inelastically scattering neutral electroweak state—specifically, a neutralino with sub-MeV mass splitting—within a theoretically consistent, UV-complete supersymmetric framework.

Method: Constructs a supersymmetric 331 model embedded in SU(6) grand unification; analyzes the electroweakino sector above the 331-breaking scale where hypercharge is not fundamental; derives gauge-invariant relations (e.g., I_X = −I_L) replacing standard bino–wino cancellation; diagonalizes the full neutral gauge–Higgs fermion mass matrix at tree level; computes Higgsino mass splittings self-consistently without introducing effective bino mass by hand; maps viable parameter space satisfying the LZ recoil constraint.

Result: Identifies a continuous locus in gaugino and 331-breaking parameters yielding the required sub-MeV neutralino splitting; at a reference point, the solution approximates the leading sum rule; variation of the 331-breaking coupling smoothly deforms the locus; predicts ordering of additional neutral states, gauge contributions to scalar masses, and gravitino hierarchy; distinguishes the model from competing explanations (e.g., SU(5), singlino-assisted).

Conclusion: The supersymmetric 331 model from SU(6) GUT provides a natural, calculable, and testable explanation for the LZ high-recoil event via inelastic neutralino scattering, with predictive power over neutralino spectrum, scalar mass corrections, and superpartner hierarchies—offering a distinct alternative to minimal SUSY or singlet-extended scenarios.

Abstract: The high-recoil event reported by LUX--ZEPLIN motivates a neutral electroweak state with an inelastic splitting in the sub-MeV range. We propose supersymmetric 331 model from the supersymmetric $SU(6)$ GUT model, and we study the electroweakino sector relevant to this recoil. Above the $331$-breaking scale hypercharge is not an independent gauge factor, and the leading bino--wino cancellation is replaced by a relation between the physical $U(1)_X$ and $SU(3)_L$ gaugino invariants, $I_X=-I_L$. A singlet--adjoint gauge-kinetic source also correlates the colored invariant. We diagonalize the neutral gauge--Higgs fermion sector of the renormalizable model and determine the tree-level Higgsino splitting without introducing an effective bino mass by hand. The required solutions form a continuous locus in the gaugino and $331$-breaking parameters. At the reference point, the solution lies close to the leading sum rule, while variation of the $331$-breaking coupling moves the locus continuously. The same calculation tests the ordering of the additional neutral states, the gauge/gaugino contribution to the $331$-breaking scalar mass, and the gravitino ordering. We compare this construction with recent $SU(5)$, singlino-assisted, electroweak-multiplet, nuclear-response and solar-capture analyses of the LZ event.

</details>


### [129] [Probing Matter Unification through Higgs Physics at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23708)
*Mark Brettell,Jon Butterworth,Hridoy Debnath,Pavel Fileviez Perez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the Higgs sector of a minimal low-scale quark-lepton unification model, predicting additional CP-even/odd neutral and charged Higgs bosons, heavy right-handed neutrinos, and leptoquarks; it computes LHC production cross sections and branching ratios—dominated by third-generation fermions—and shows that current tau- and top-rich channels constrain neutral heavy Higgses significantly, while charged Higgses remain less constrained; Higgs precision measurements thus offer a novel, complementary probe of quark-lepton unification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore Higgs sector phenomenology as a probe of low-scale quark-lepton unification, beyond direct leptoquark searches, leveraging characteristic couplings tied to matter unification structure.

Method: Analytical and numerical computation of scalar spectrum, Yukawa couplings (for charged-fermion masses), inverse-seesaw neutrino mass mechanism; calculation of LHC production cross sections and branching ratios for new Higgs states using SM-like and extended Higgs couplings; reinterpretation of current LHC constraints (especially tau-, top-, and related final states).

Result: Current LHC data significantly constrain neutral heavy Higgs bosons via tau-lepton- and top-quark-rich decay channels; charged Higgs is less constrained due to suppressed electroweak production; discovery potential remains at high-luminosity LHC.

Conclusion: Higgs precision measurements provide a powerful, complementary avenue to test low-scale quark-lepton unification, with sensitivity already present in existing data and enhanced prospects at HL-LHC.

Abstract: We investigate the Higgs sector phenomenology of the minimal low scale quark-lepton unification theory. In addition to the Standard Model-like Higgs boson, the theory predicts a new CP-even neutral Higgs, a CP-odd neutral Higgs, and two charged Higgses, together with heavy right-handed neutrinos and leptoquarks. We analyze the scalar spectrum, the Yukawa structure responsible for charged-fermion masses, and the neutrino sector realized through an inverse-seesaw mechanism, emphasizing that the couplings of the new Higgs bosons are directly tied to the structure of matter unification and therefore lead to characteristic decay patterns dominated by third-generation fermions. We compute the branching ratios and production cross sections of the new Higgs states at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) and assess their impact on present and future collider measurements. We find that current LHC data probe significant regions of parameter space for the neutral heavy Higgs bosons, with the strongest sensitivity arising from channels involving tau leptons, tops, and related final states, while the charged Higgs remains less constrained because of its electroweak production mechanism. Our results show that Higgs measurements provide a powerful and complementary probe of low-scale matter unification beyond direct leptoquark searches and open a new avenue for testing quark-lepton unification at present and future colliders, with discovery potential in high-luminosity LHC running.

</details>


### [130] [Baryogenesis and dark matter freeze-in from Higgs interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23829)
*Avery Allen,John Brennan Halcomb,Zoe Kane,Nathaniel Kirby,Neil Panth,Brian Shuve,David Tucker-Smith*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a unified freeze-in dark matter and baryogenesis model featuring a TeV-scale vector-like fermion (VLF) and two keV–hundred-keV Majorana DM candidates; CP-violating Yukawa couplings enable simultaneous DM production and baryon asymmetry generation via VLF decays and asymmetry transfer, constrained by LHC and structure formation, with extensions allowing B−L violation and new phenomenology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a minimal, testable framework unifying freeze-in dark matter production and baryogenesis without requiring B−L violation, while accommodating observational constraints on DM abundance and structure formation.

Method: Perturbative calculation of the baryon asymmetry from CP-violating VLF decays and rescattering; analysis of freeze-in DM production kinetics; mapping of viable VLF mass–lifetime parameter space; inclusion of B−L violating Yukawa couplings to extend viability and assess phenomenological signatures (DM decay, LFV, collider limits).

Result: The model generates the observed baryon asymmetry and DM relic density simultaneously; viable parameter space is tightly constrained by current LHC searches (e.g., disappearing tracks, heavy stable charged particles) and small-scale structure bounds (e.g., warm DM suppression); B−L violating extensions relax constraints and introduce observable signals.

Conclusion: A predictive, minimal freeze-in baryogenesis–DM model is viable only in narrow regions of parameter space, already under strong experimental pressure; future colliders and astrophysical probes will decisively test its remaining windows, especially when extended with B−L violation.

Abstract: We present a model of freeze-in dark matter (DM) and baryogenesis in which the new particles are a TeV-scale electroweak-doublet vector-like fermion (VLF) and two gauge-singlet Majorana fermions that constitute the DM, the heavier one with a mass in the tens-to-hundreds of keV range. Feeble Yukawa couplings of the VLF to the DM and the Standard Model Higgs doublet induce freeze-in production of the DM via VLF decays in the early universe. Due to a CP-violating phase in these couplings, DM propagation and rescattering induce opposite charge asymmetries in the VLF and SM sectors. Even in the absence of $B-L$ violation, the VLF asymmetry guarantees a baryon asymmetry once in-equilibrium SM interactions are taken into account. A relatively small conversion factor from VLF asymmetry to baryon asymmetry is balanced by a more direct asymmetry generation mechanism than in other minimal models of freeze-in baryogenesis. The observed DM energy density requires the heavier DM particle to remain well out of equilibrium throughout production. This allows us to map out the VLF mass-lifetime space using a novel perturbative calculation of the baryon asymmetry. We find that the model is already significantly constrained by LHC searches and structure formation probes, which will continue to test interesting parameter space in the coming years. The addition of VLF Yukawa couplings not involving the DM breaks $B-L$ and greatly expands the viable parameter space, while inducing potential signatures from dark matter decay and lepton flavor violation.

</details>


### [131] [Non-standard cosmic strings with amazing profiles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23920)
*Wolfgang Bietenholz,José Antonio García-Hernández,Victor Muñoz-Vitelly*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a minimal extension of the Standard Model by gauging the B−L symmetry, introducing right-handed neutrinos and a new complex scalar to generate their mass; it numerically solves the gauge-Higgs field equations in cylindrical geometry to study cosmic string solutions, finding sub-femtometer-scale strings with novel profile features (e.g., sign-changing Higgs profiles and co-axial/overshooting behavior) consistent with current cosmological constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The accidental global B−L symmetry in the Standard Model is unnatural; promoting it to a local (gauged) symmetry provides theoretical motivation, anomaly cancellation requires right-handed neutrinos, and the absence of a Majorana mass necessitates a new Higgs scalar — yielding a well-motivated, minimal extension.

Method: The authors construct a U(1)_{B−L} gauge extension of the SM with two Higgs fields (SM Higgs and a new 1-component complex scalar), derive the coupled non-linear field equations for the gauge and Higgs sectors in cylindrical coordinates, and solve them numerically to obtain cosmic string solutions (profiles and energy densities).

Result: Numerical solutions reveal stable cosmic string configurations with radii < 10^{-3} fm and tension compatible with CMB and gravitational-wave bounds; novel features include 'overshooting' and 'co-axial' profiles, where the SM Higgs field changes sign near the string core for high winding numbers.

Conclusion: Gauging B−L with minimal particle content yields viable, ultra-thin cosmic strings exhibiting unexpected topological and field-profile phenomena, supporting the model’s theoretical consistency and potential phenomenological relevance.

Abstract: It is an unnatural feature of the Standard Model that the difference between the baryon number $B$ and the lepton number $L$ is conserved, which implies an exact, global symmetry. We promote it to a naturally exact, local symmetry by coupling the difference $B-L$ to an Abelian gauge field. Gauge anomalies are cancelled by adding right-handed neutrinos $ν_{R}$. They are not sterile in this case, which forbids the Majorana term, but we arrange for a $ν_{R}$-mass by adding a Higgs-type 1-component complex scalar field. Thus we arrive at a well-motivated, modest extension of the Standard Model (SM).
  We investigate the field equations in the corresponding gauge-Higgs sector, involving both Higgs fields and the non-standard U(1) gauge field. This leads to a set of coupled, non-linear differential equations, which we solve numerically. We use cylindrical coordinates, thus focusing on the structures of cosmic strings. For a large variety of parameters, we identify the string profiles and energy densities. These profiles depend on the winding number of each of the Higgs fields. For our parameters, the characteristic string radii are below $10^{-3}~{\rm fm}$, and the string tension is not ruled out by observations of the Cosmic Microwave Background or gravitational waves. As an amazing peculiarity, we discover - for high winding numbers - ``overshooting'' and ``co-axial'' profile functions. In the latter case, the profile of the standard Higgs field changes its sign near the core of the cosmic string.

</details>


### [132] [Three-Body Holonomy as a Toy-Model Mechanism for Family Triplication and Mass Hierarchy in (1+1) Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24022)
*Tadashi YOSHIKAWA*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how a genuine three-body holonomy in a 1D relativistic Dirac system induces nontrivial U(1) phase winding around a triple-coincidence point in relative configuration space, leading to a cyclic three-state mass structure; it demonstrates how the holonomy θ₃ lifts degeneracies, generates light mass branches, and leaves infrared memory—encoded in rephasing-invariant loop products—of the full three-state topology, offering a minimal phenomenological model for fermion triplication and hierarchy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a low-dimensional, phenomenologically viable mechanism for fermion family triplication, mass splitting, and infrared memory—without invoking high-energy microphysics—by leveraging topological holonomy arising from genuine three-body quantum statistics in a relativistic setting.

Method: Analyzes a 1D relativistic Dirac system with three identical particles; removes center-of-mass coordinate to obtain 2D relative configuration space punctured at triple-coincidence point; identifies nontrivial U(1) holonomy θ₃; constructs C₃-symmetric Hermitian effective mass operator (Peierls-type) on a 3-cycle graph; computes eigenvalues analytically; generalizes to symmetry-broken case using Schur complement to integrate out heavy states and extract low-energy corrections; identifies rephasing-invariant real and imaginary parts of the cyclic product t₁₂t₂₃t₃₁ as cos θ₃ and sin θ₃.

Result: Derives exact mass eigenvalues M^[k] = M₀ + 2Δ cos((θ₃ + 2πk)/3), showing holonomy-induced lifting of conjugate-channel degeneracy and parametric lightness; proves that low-energy effective mass retains cos θ₃ via Re(t₁₂t₂₃t₃₁), constituting topological infrared memory; shows Im(t₁₂t₂₃t₃₁) ∝ sin θ₃ is phase-sensitive but insufficient for CP violation alone.

Conclusion: A genuine three-body holonomy in a minimal 1D Dirac system suffices to generate family-like triplication, hierarchical mass spectrum, and robust infrared memory encoded in gauge-invariant loop phases—establishing a proof-of-concept that topological many-body structure, rather than fundamental replication, can underlie observed fermion generation patterns.

Abstract: We investigate the phenomenological consequences of a genuine three-body holonomy in a relativistic Dirac system in one spatial dimension. After removing the center-of-mass coordinate, the triple-coincidence point punctures the two-dimensional relative configuration space and permits a nontrivial $U(1)$ winding phase, whereas the pairwise Sakamoto--Munakata--Ino contact interactions give trivial net matching around this point.
  At fixed intrinsic parity, the six particle-ordering sectors reduce to a three-dimensional cyclic space. A Hermitian $C_3$-invariant effective mass operator admits a Peierls-type realization in which the gauge-invariant phase around the three links equals the three-body holonomy $θ_3$. Its eigenvalues are
  $$ M^{[k]}=M_0+2Δ\cos\!\left(\frac{θ_3+2πk}{3}\right), \qquad k=0,1,2. $$
  The holonomy lifts the conjugate-channel degeneracy and can generate a parametrically light branch through cancellation between the common mass and the holonomy-induced shift.
  We further allow cyclic-symmetry breaking and consider a general Hermitian three-state mass matrix. Exact elimination of two heavy states by the Schur complement yields a low-energy correction containing the rephasing-invariant loop product $\mathrm{Re}(t_{12}t_{23}t_{31})\propto\cosθ_3$. Thus, even when only one branch is kinematically accessible, its effective mass can retain finite memory of the complete three-state loop. The complementary invariant $\mathrm{Im}(t_{12}t_{23}t_{31})\propto\sinθ_3$ is phase sensitive but does not alone imply CP violation. The construction provides a low-dimensional phenomenological proof of concept for family-like triplication, mass hierarchy, and infrared memory, rather than a microscopic theory of Standard Model fermion generations.

</details>


### [133] [Deciphering the production mechanism of the LHCb $P_c$ states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24231)
*Wen-Jia Wang,Meng-Lin Du,Feng-Kun Guo,Bing Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel box-diagram-based production mechanism to explain why only three narrow pentaquark states ($P_c(4312)$, $P_c(4440)$, $P_c(4457)$) are observed in $Λ_b^0$ decays, attributing their formation to $Σ_c\bar{D}^{(*)}$ rescattering and invoking box singularities (for $P_c(4457)$) and suppressed $Λ_c(2625)$ production to account for the absence of predicted heavy-quark spin partners.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between the molecular interpretation of LHCb's $P_c$ states and the non-observation of expected heavy-quark spin partners; specifically, to explain why only three $P_c$ states appear prominently in $Λ_b^0 \to J/ψp K^-$ decays.

Method: Constructs a novel production mechanism involving $D_{s1}^*(2860)Λ_c(2595)\bar{D}π$ and $D_{s0}(2590)Λ_c(2595)\bar{D}^*π$ box diagrams that feed into $K^-Σ_c\bar{D}^{(*)}$, followed by $S$-wave rescattering to $J/ψp$; incorporates box singularity analysis and uses lattice QCD–informed suppression of $Λ_c(2625)$ to suppress $Σ_c^*\bar{D}^{(*)}$ partners.

Result: Reproduces LHCb data with only two production parameters (vs. 7 previously), naturally explains the absence of $Σ_c^*\bar{D}^{(*)}$ states, and yields quantitative, falsifiable branching ratio predictions for $Λ_b^0 \to P_c K \to J/ψp K$ relative to specific intermediate charmed baryon–meson channels.

Conclusion: The proposed box-diagram + rescattering mechanism provides a unified, economical, and predictive explanation for the observed $P_c$ spectrum and its apparent incompleteness under heavy-quark spin symmetry, strongly supporting their hadronic molecular nature while clarifying production dynamics in weak $Λ_b^0$ decays.

Abstract: The three narrow pentaquarks $P_c(4312)$, $P_c(4440)$, and $P_c(4457)$ observed by LHCb are widely interpreted as hadronic molecules owing to their proximity to the $Σ_c\bar{D}^{(*)}$ thresholds. However, heavy-quark spin symmetry predicts additional partner states that have not been seen experimentally, making the production mechanism in $Λ_b^0$ decays particularly important in understanding their nature. We propose a novel production mechanism, which can naturally explain why only these three LHCb $P_c$ states have been clearly observed. The $P_c(4440)$ and $P_c(4457)$ are produced via the $D_{s1}^*(2860)Λ_c(2595)\bar{D}π$ box diagram leading to $K^-Σ_c\bar{D}^{*}$, followed by rescattering into $K^- J/ψp$. The box diagram develops a box singularity in the vicinity of the $P_c(4457)$, as all four states in the box can go on shell. The $P_c$ states themselves are dynamically generated through the rescattering of the $S$-wave $Σ_c\bar{D}^{(*)}$ to $J/ψp$. The $P_c(4312)$ proceeds analogously via the $D_{s0}(2590)Λ_c(2595)\bar{D}^*π$ box diagram but without a box singularity. The near-invisibility of the $Σ_c^*\bar{D}^{(*)}$ molecular states is attributed to the suppressed production of the $Λ_c(2625)$ in $Λ_b$ weak decays, as established by the lattice QCD calculations. This mechanism naturally reproduces the data with only two production parameters, whereas previous works required 7 parameters and a finely tuned background, and provides a natural explanation for why the $Σ_c^*\bar{D}^{(*)}$ states are barely visible. It yields unique, falsifiable predictions, $Γ(Λ_b^0\to P_c(4440/4457) K\to J/ψp K)/Γ(Λ_b^0\to\bar D_{s1}^*(2860)Λ_c(2595))\approx (0.2-0.4)\%$, $Γ(Λ_b^0\to P_c(4312) K\to J/ψp K)/Γ(Λ_b^0\to \bar D_{s0}(2590)Λ_c(2595))\approx (0.2-0.8)\%$.

</details>


### [134] [Holonomic techniques for massive 3-loop form factors: the gluonic case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24279)
*J. Blümlein,A. De Freitas,P. Marquard,J. Obrovsky,C. Schneider*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes massive three-loop form factors for various currents (vector, axial-vector, scalar, pseudoscalar) in the gluonic sector—extending prior quarkonic results—using large-moment expansions, advanced symbolic computation, and PSLQ-based constant recognition; it delivers exact analytic expansions around s=0 (in MZVs) and the first complete s→±∞ expansions (in MZVs plus three higher-weight constants), with validated analytic continuation across the full real s-axis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precision collider phenomenology requires massive three-loop form factors beyond the quark-initiated case; the gluonic contributions are significantly more complex and had remained uncomputed in full analytic form.

Method: Large-moment method combined with automated recurrence solving, high-precision numerical evaluation, PSLQ integer-relation searches, and differential equation techniques; symbolic reconstruction of analytic series expansions around s=0 and s=±∞.

Result: Exact analytic expansion around s=0 in terms of multiple zeta values (MZVs); first complete analytic series at s→±∞ involving MZVs and three constants from higher-weight number spaces; validated analytic continuation over the entire real s-axis.

Conclusion: The gluonic three-loop form factors are now fully characterized analytically across kinematic regimes, establishing a landmark benchmark for symbolic computation and enabling high-precision QCD predictions for LHC and future colliders.

Abstract: Massive three-loop form factors for vector, axial-vector, scalar, and pseudoscalar currents are vital for precision collider phenomenology. Extending the quarkonic framework to the more complex gluonic case, in Ref. (arXiv:2609.22034 [hep-ph]) we computed these contributions widely using advanced computer algebra, automated guessing, and large-scale PSLQ searches. Here, we outline the core strategy of the large-moment method and illustrate its main challenges, including solving record-sized recurrences and differential equations. Our high-precision evaluation provides an exact analytic representation around $s=0$ in terms of MZVs. We achieved a complete analytic series expansion around $s=\pm\infty$ for the first time, which depends on MZVs and three constants out of higher number spaces, along with reliable analytic continuations across $s \in (-\infty, \infty)$, serving as a landmark benchmark for modern symbolic computation.

</details>


### [135] [Multitask Jet Analysis with Vision-Language Models: A Physics-Informed Four-Panel Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24334)
*Lu Zhang,Rachik Soualah,Abbes Amira*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores using Vision-Language Models (VLMs) — adapted via LoRA — on physics-informed jet images for unified jet tagging (QCD, Higgs, W/Z, top), attribute prediction, and consistency localization; achieves strong performance (e.g., 75.05% Macro recall), highlights lifetime and jet-charge signatures as key discriminators, and demonstrates robust transfer to broader topologies and real-data domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The growing volume of high-energy physics collider data makes precise jet identification a bottleneck; conventional ML approaches are often task-specific, motivating a unified, physics-informed, multimodal interface leveraging VLMs for structured jet analysis.

Method: Converts JetClass jets into 224×224 RGB 'physics images' with four panels encoding: (1) p_T flow, (2) constituent composition (charged/neutral hadrons, EM), (3) p_T-weighted impact-parameter significances + displaced-track multiplicity (lifetime signature), and (4) signed-track p_T densities + local p_T^k-weighted jet-charge asymmetry. Four open VLMs (including Gemma4-E4B) are fine-tuned using low-rank adaptation (LoRA) on three tasks: (1) 4-class jet classification, (2) six-field attribute regression/prediction, and (3) cross-panel consistency via replaced-panel localization. Evaluation uses 24,000 balanced JetClass test jets; ablations and budget reallocation (e.g., oversampling W/Z) are performed; transfer tested on JetClass-II and real-data Aspen Open Jets.

Result: Gemma4-E4B achieves 75.05% Task 1 Macro recall (74.87% F₁), 93.31% Task 2 field-mean accuracy, and 99.88%/99.75% binary/localization recall for Task 3. Ablations confirm impact-parameter lifetime dominates heavy-flavor discrimination; jet charge separates W/Z. Zero-shot recall is near chance (3.33–10.57%). Oversampling W/Z boosts Zqq recall by up to +9.83 points without degrading Macro recall. Transfer to JetClass-II and Aspen Open Jets with only 3,000 target jets yields >70% Task 1 recall and >92% on all Task 2/3 metrics.

Conclusion: Rasterizing jet observables — energy flow, species, displacement, and jet charge — into structured images and adapting open VLMs via parameter-efficient LoRA enables a single instruction-conditioned interface for robust, generalizable, and interpretable jet analysis, bridging simulation-to-data gaps and scaling to future collider data.

Abstract: The enormous recorded data at high-energy physics (HEP) colliders make the accurate identification of physics objects a bottleneck in disentangling event topologies, where machine learning has become a standard tool for jet tagging. In this work, we examine whether Vision-Language Models (VLMs) can provide a common interface for structured jet analysis from a single physics-informed image. Each JetClass jet becomes a 224x224 RGB image with four panels encoding p_T flow of all constituents; charged-hadron, neutral-hadron, and electromagnetic composition; p_T-weighted impact-parameter significances with displaced-track multiplicity; and signed-track p_T densities with local p_T^k-weighted jet-charge asymmetry. Four open VLMs are adapted with low-rank adaptation (LoRA) for class classification across QCD, Higgs, W/Z, and top jets, six-field attribute prediction, and cross-panel consistency with replaced-panel localization, evaluated on 24000 balanced JetClass test jets. Ablations identify the impact-parameter lifetime signature of heavy-flavor tagging as dominant, with jet charge separating the nearly mass-degenerate hadronic W and Z. Zero-shot recall stays near chance (3.33%-10.57%), once adapted, Task 1 Macro recall grows monotonically with training size, and Gemma4-E4B reaches 75.05% (74.87% F_1), 93.31% Task 2 field-mean, and 99.88%/99.75% binary/localization recall. Reallocating budget toward W/Z jets raises Zqq recall by up to 9.83 points at near-constant Macro recall. Transfer to broader-topology JetClass-II and real-data Aspen Open Jets (probing the simulation-to-data gap) is retained: with 3000 target jets, Task 1 recall exceeds 70% on JetClass-II and every Task 2/3 metric exceeds 92%. Thus rasterizing energy flow, species, displacement, and jet charge, with parameter-efficient adaptation, supports jet analysis through one instruction-conditioned interface for future collider data.

</details>


### [136] [Probing axion-like particle couplings to leptons via the $γγjj$ signal at the FCC-ee](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24566)
*Hua-Ying Zhang,Chong-Xing Yue*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the FCC-ee's sensitivity to axion-like particle (ALP) couplings with leptons via e⁺e⁻ → γγjj at √s = 91 GeV and 150 ab⁻¹ luminosity, comparing unpolarized and polarized (80% e⁻, −80% e⁺) beams across three coupling scenarios; polarized beams yield stronger limits—down to ~0.00394 GeV⁻¹—for ALP masses spanning 10–80 GeV, complementing other future collider probes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe axion-like particle (ALP) couplings to leptons—specifically to neutrinos and charged leptons—using the high-luminosity, precision environment of FCC-ee, where existing constraints are weak and complementary coverage to other collider channels is needed.

Method: Simulated analysis of e⁺e⁻ → γγjj at √s = 91 GeV with ℒ = 150 ab⁻¹; considered three ALP coupling scenarios (c_R = 0, c_L = 0, c_R = c_L), both for unpolarized and longitudinally polarized beams (P_e⁻ = +80%, P_e⁺ = −80%); derived projected 95% CL lower limits on effective ALP-lepton couplings g_aℓℓ/f_a and g_aνν/f_a using kinematic cuts and background estimation.

Result: Polarized beams significantly improve sensitivity: for c_R = 0 (ALP–neutrino coupling), limit ≈ 0.0105 GeV⁻¹ (10–24 GeV m_a); for c_L = 0 (ALP–charged lepton), ≈ 0.0174 GeV⁻¹ (10–27 GeV); for c_R = c_L (ALP–neutrino), ≈ 0.00394 GeV⁻¹ (45–80 GeV); all surpass unpolarized case and fill gaps relative to other future collider searches.

Conclusion: FCC-ee with polarized beams offers uniquely competitive and complementary sensitivity to ALP–lepton couplings in the 10–80 GeV mass range, especially for neutrino-coupled ALPs, reinforcing its role as a precision frontier for light BSM physics.

Abstract: We study the sensitivity of the Future Circular Collider in electron-positron mode (FCC-ee) to the axion-like particle (ALP) couplings with leptons via the process $e^{+}e^{-} \rightarrow γγjj$ at the center-of-mass energy $\sqrt{s}=91$ GeV with the integrated luminosity $\mathcal{L}=150~\text{ab}^{-1}$. We analyze three scenarios, $\mathbf{c}_R=0$, $\mathbf{c}_L=0$ and $\mathbf{c}_R=\mathbf{c}_L$, for both unpolarized and polarized beams with $(P_{e^-}, P_{e^+}) = (+80\%, -80\%)$, and derive projected lower limits on the effective coupling strengths. The polarized case provides higher sensitivity than the unpolarized case. Specifically, for the $\mathbf{c}_R=0$ scenario (probing the ALP-neutrino coupling $\text{Tr}(g_{aνν})/f_a$), the projected sensitivity in the polarized case reaches approximately $0.0105~\text{GeV}^{-1}$ for the ALP mass in the range $10$--$24$ GeV; for $\mathbf{c}_L=0$ (probing the ALP-charged lepton coupling $\text{Tr}(g_{a\ell\ell})/f_a$), it reaches $0.0174~\text{GeV}^{-1}$ in the $10$--$27$ GeV range; and for $\mathbf{c}_R=\mathbf{c}_L$ (probing the ALP-neutrino coupling $\text{Tr}(g_{aνν})/f_a$), it reaches $0.00394~\text{GeV}^{-1}$ in the $45$--$80$ GeV range. In these ALP mass ranges, our projected sensitivities are complementary to the projected sensitivities from other search channels at future colliders.

</details>


### [137] [Resolving the universality and polarization puzzles in $J/ψ$ hadroproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24674)
*An-Ping Chen,Jun Guo,Yan-Qing Ma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper resolves two longstanding puzzles in prompt J/ψ hadroproduction—LDME universality tension between hadronic and e+e− data, and simultaneous description of yield and weak polarization—by applying threshold-resummed fragmentation-function formalisms in both NRQCD and SGF frameworks; it identifies a common LDME region where 3S1[8] and 3PJ[8] dominate the yield, their transverse contributions cancel, and a natural v⁴ spin-flip correction in 3S1[8] accounts for both observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve two interrelated puzzles in prompt J/ψ hadroproduction: (i) incompatible constraints on the same color-octet LDME combination from fixed-order hadronic vs. e+e− analyses (universality tension), and (ii) inability to simultaneously describe the measured cross section and weak transverse polarization.

Method: Applies leading-logarithmic threshold resummation within the fragmentation-function formalism to high-pT J/ψ production in both NRQCD and soft-gluon factorization (SGF); performs global fits to resummed hadroproduction data and combines with resummed e+e− bounds to constrain LDMEs; analyzes spin structure via a one-parameter spin-flip ansatz in the 3S1[8] channel.

Result: Threshold resummation eliminates the LDME universality tension, yielding a common allowed region in both NRQCD and SGF; the 1S0[8] contribution is suppressed; yield is dominated by 3S1[8] and 3PJ[8], whose leading transverse amplitudes largely cancel; this cancellation enhances sensitivity to spin-symmetry-breaking effects, enabling a natural O(v⁴) spin-flip term in 3S1[8] to simultaneously fit yield and weak polarization; analogous behavior holds for ψ(2S).

Conclusion: Threshold resummation is essential for restoring LDME universality across processes and for reconciling J/ψ yield and polarization; the observed weak polarization arises not from suppression of transverse production, but from near-cancellation of dominant transverse amplitudes, making subleading spin-flip dynamics observable and physically significant.

Abstract: Prompt $J/ψ$ hadroproduction poses two related puzzles: fixed-order hadronic and $e^+e^-$ analyses give incompatible constraints on the same color-octet (CO) long-distance matrix element (LDME) combination, and the weak observed polarization is difficult to reproduce together with the yield. We analyze high-$p_T$ production in the fragmentation-function formalism with leading-logarithmic threshold resummation in both nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) and soft-gluon factorization (SGF). Combining the resummed hadroproduction fit with bounds from resummed $e^+e^-$ production, we find in both frameworks a common LDME region satisfying the two constraints simultaneously: threshold resummation resolves the universality tension present at fixed order. There, the $^{1}S_{0}^{[8]}$ contribution is small and the yield is governed by the $^{3}S_{1}^{[8]}$ and $^{3}P_{J}^{[8]}$ channels, whose leading transverse contributions largely cancel. The cancellation amplifies spin-symmetry-breaking transitions in the $^{3}S_{1}^{[8]}$ channel, so that within a one-parameter spin-flip ansatz a contribution of natural order $v^4$ accommodates both the measured yields and the weak polarization. A similar sensitivity holds for $ψ(2S)$ production.

</details>


### [138] [The phenomenology of Axion Relic Pockets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24714)
*Wafaa Khater,M. C. David Marsh,Charalampos Nikolis*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies axion relic pockets—phase-transition remnants stabilized by hot axion gas—as dark matter candidates, computes axion-to-photon conversion in their compact spherical geometry (including atomic electric fields), identifies isotropic high-energy electromagnetic cascades (especially upward-going) as a smoking-gun signature, sets first terrestrial limits using IceCube data, and outlines detection prospects via fluorescence, radio Cherenkov, and direct detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Axion relic pockets are theoretically plausible dark matter components, but their phenomenology remains largely unexplored; understanding their electromagnetic signatures is essential for experimental testability.

Method: Derives the axion-to-photon conversion rate within spherically symmetric, compact axion relic pockets, incorporating effects of geometry and coupling to atomic electric fields; models resulting electromagnetic cascades; interprets IceCube neutrino data to set exclusion limits; surveys complementary detection channels (fluorescence, radio Cherenkov, direct detection).

Result: Identifies isotropic, upward-going electromagnetic showers as a distinctive signature; obtains first experimental constraints on axion relic pockets from IceCube; finds only a narrow region of parameter space is currently testable; outlines viable pathways for future detection across multiple experimental platforms.

Conclusion: Axion relic pockets are phenomenologically accessible dark matter candidates; this work establishes foundational tools and signatures for their systematic experimental investigation, bridging theoretical cosmology and multi-messenger particle astrophysics.

Abstract: Axion relic pockets are phase-transition remnants consisting of regions of false vacuum stabilised from collapse by a hot axion gas. Axion relic pockets can comprise dark matter, but little is known about their phenomenology. We consider trapped axions coupled to electromagnetism and derive the axion to photon conversion rate accounting for the pocket's compactness and spherical geometry. As a case study, we show that axion-photon conversion occurs in atomic electric fields, leading to high-energy electromagnetic cascades that may be detectable by neutrino, cosmic-ray, and dark-matter experiments. The distribution of electromagnetic showers is inherently isotropic, and upward-going showers provide a smoking-gun signal for the model. Using recent data from the IceCube neutrino telescope, we derive the first limits from terrestrial experiments on axion relic pockets as dark matter. However, for the simplest realisation of the model, only a small part of the parameter space can give signals strong enough to be constrained by current data. We also comment on the prospects for discovering axion relic pockets using fluorescence and radio Cherenkov detection by atmospheric instruments and balloon-borne payloads, as well as traditional dark matter direct detection experiments. These results provide the necessary tools to initiate phenomenological studies of axion relic pockets across different environments.

</details>


### [139] [Comparative Study of Color Glass Condensate and Collinear Frameworks for Large-Transverse-Momentum Semi-Inclusive Deep Inelastic Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24783)
*Swagato Mukherjee,Björn Schenke,Shaswat Tiwari*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares collinear and Color Glass Condensate (CGC) factorization frameworks for Semi-Inclusive Deep Inelastic Scattering (SIDIS) at large hadron transverse momentum (Pt ≳ Q), using COMPASS, HERA, and projected Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) data; it finds limited discriminative power between the two schemes for nuclear SIDIS at EIC and highlights sensitivity of LO CGC predictions to the gluon longitudinal momentum fraction xg, indicating need for beyond-eikonal corrections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve theoretical ambiguity in describing large-Pt SIDIS: whether collinear factorization (PDFs + FFs) or CGC-based eikonal dipole formalism is more appropriate—especially in small-x, high-energy regimes relevant to current (COMPASS, HERA) and future (EIC) experiments.

Method: Systematic comparison of leading-order (LO) and next-to-leading-order (NLO) collinear factorization versus LO CGC factorization for SIDIS differential cross-sections; applied to existing COMPASS and HERA data, and extrapolated to EIC kinematics for both nucleon and nuclear targets; assessed theoretical uncertainties and xg-dependence in CGC predictions.

Result: Within current theoretical uncertainties, collinear and LO CGC descriptions are difficult to distinguish for nuclear SIDIS at EIC; LO CGC predictions show strong sensitivity to the choice of gluon longitudinal momentum fraction xg, signaling substantial beyond-eikonal corrections required in EIC kinematics.

Conclusion: The study underscores limitations of pure eikonal CGC at EIC energies and suggests that higher-order (beyond-LO) CGC calculations—including finite-xg and non-eikonal effects—are essential for robust interpretation of large-Pt SIDIS in small-x nuclear collisions; collinear factorization remains viable but may require careful matching with saturation physics in transitional regimes.

Abstract: We study the Semi-Inclusive Deep Inelastic Scattering (SIDIS) cross-section at large values of hadron transverse momentum $P_t \gtrsim Q$, with $Q^2$ being the photon virtuality. This kinematic regime of SIDIS allows for a collinear factorization in terms of parton distribution functions (PDFs) and collinear fragmentation functions (FFs). On the other hand, at high energies, i.e., at small Bjorken $x$, the same SIDIS process can also be factorized within the Color Glass Condensate (CGC) framework in terms of eikonal dipole amplitudes. In this work, we perform a systematic comparison of the leading-order (LO) and next-to-leading-order (NLO) collinear and the LO CGC factorizations of this process based on the COMPASS and HERA data. We further provide comparisons of these two factorization frameworks for nucleon and nuclear large-$P_t$ SIDIS in the expected Electron Ion Collider (EIC) kinematics. We find, within the present theoretical uncertainties, that it might be difficult to distinguish between these two factorization schemes for nuclear SIDIS at the EIC. We also observe a significant dependence of the LO eikonal CGC predictions on the choice of longitudinal momentum fraction $x_g$, suggesting significant beyond-eikonal corrections for the EIC kinematics.

</details>


### [140] [A New Probe of Strongly-Interacting Dark Sector using Neutrino Telescopes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24887)
*Jose A. Macias Cruz,Christopher V. Cappiello,P. S. Bhupal Dev,Suchita Kulkarni*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes using cosmic neutrino background scattering to detect dark vector mesons from a confining dark sector via resonant absorption features in the neutrino spectrum—enabling spectroscopy of dark-sector resonances with future neutrino telescopes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe strongly-interacting dark matter via spectroscopic signatures from a QCD-like confining dark sector, leveraging the cosmic neutrino background as a target.

Method: Analyzes resonant production of dark vector mesons in high-energy neutrino–cosmic neutrino background scattering, computing resulting absorption features in the cosmic neutrino energy spectrum.

Result: Identifies a distinctive, mass-dependent absorption feature; finds broad regions of dark-sector parameter space (e.g., dark confinement scale Λ_D ∼ 10 MeV–1 GeV, coupling g_D ∼ 0.1–1) accessible to IceCube-Gen2 Radio and other next-generation neutrino telescopes.

Conclusion: Cosmic neutrino background scattering provides a novel, spectroscopic probe of dark-sector resonances and neutrino–dark-sector interactions, turning neutrino telescopes into discovery instruments for strongly-interacting dark sectors.

Abstract: A confining dark sector modeled after quantum chromodynamics provides a well-motivated framework for strongly-interacting dark matter. We show that dark vector mesons in a confining dark sector can be resonantly produced in high-energy neutrino scattering on the cosmic neutrino background, generating a distinctive absorption feature in the cosmic neutrino energy spectrum. Unlike conventional attenuation effects, this feature directly reflects the mass spectrum of dark resonances and can therefore serve as a spectroscopic signature of a strongly-interacting dark sector. We find that a broad range of dark-sector parameter space is within the reach of future neutrino telescopes, including IceCube-Gen2 Radio. Our proposal thus turns the cosmic neutrino background into a target for discovering dark-sector resonances, while simultaneously providing a novel probe of dark sector interactions with neutrinos.

</details>


### [141] [Two-loop amplitude for the associated production of a top-anti-top pair and a $W$ boson at hadron colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24910)
*Mattia Pozzoli*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the NNLO QCD inclusive cross-section for tt̄W production using the first exact leading-colour two-loop amplitude, bypassing elliptic functions via rational-coefficient special functions evaluated with differential equations and finite field methods; the double-virtual correction is obtained via 5D grid interpolation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve a precise theoretical prediction for the tt̄W process—a key background in Higgs and new physics searches—by computing the full NNLO QCD correction, specifically overcoming the mathematical obstacle of elliptic functions appearing in the two-loop amplitude.

Method: Exact leading-colour two-loop QCD amplitude computation; representation of the amplitude in terms of special functions (with rational coefficients) instead of elliptic functions; evaluation of special functions via canonical differential equations; rational coefficient determination using finite field arithmetic; double-virtual correction computed by interpolation over a five-dimensional phase-space grid.

Result: First complete NNLO QCD inclusive cross-section for pp → tt̄W; successful circumvention of elliptic integrals through rational-function decomposition; validated numerical evaluation of the double-virtual contribution via 5D grid interpolation.

Conclusion: The calculation establishes a robust framework for high-precision tt̄W predictions at hadron colliders, demonstrating that elliptic complications in multi-scale two-loop amplitudes can be systematically avoided using modern analytic and computational techniques.

Abstract: In this contribution I present a calculation of the inclusive cross-section for the associated production of a top-anti-top pair and a W boson at next-to-next-to-leading order in QCD, based on the first exact computation of the two-loop QCD amplitude at leading colour. I discuss a strategy to express the amplitude in terms of a set of special functions with rational coefficients, overcoming the obstacle posed by the presence of elliptic functions. The special functions are evaluated through the method of differential equations, while the rational coefficients are computed using finite field techniques. For the computation of the inclusive cross-section, the double-virtual correction is evaluated by interpolating a five-dimensional grid.

</details>


### [142] [On the failure of the $12\%$ rule in $J/ψ$ and $ψ(2S)$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24961)
*Arthur Vereijken,Francesco Giacosa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the violation of the 12% rule in J/ψ and ψ(2S) → VP hadronic decays using a chiral effective framework with vector meson dominance; it finds that J/ψ decays require additional SU(3) breaking and electromagnetic effects for agreement with data, while ψ(2S) decays are well-described minimally; the analysis shows extended fits are indistinguishable from models with J/ψ–glueball mixing, implying the ρπ anomaly likely stems from extra J/ψ decay amplitudes rather than ψ(2S)-specific suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the strong violation of the 12% rule—particularly in J/ψ → ρπ—by systematically assessing contributions from chiral dynamics, electromagnetic interactions (via vector meson dominance), SU(3) flavor breaking, and possible vector glueball mixing.

Method: Effective chiral Lagrangian framework incorporating strong and electromagnetic interactions (the latter via vector meson dominance); fits to J/ψ and ψ(2S) → VP decay widths using minimal and extended parameter sets; χ² analysis to quantify goodness-of-fit and compare model variants including glueball mixing.

Result: ψ(2S) → VP decays fit well with three parameters (χ²_red ≈ 1.12); J/ψ → VP requires three additional parameters (SU(3) breaking + EM corrections) to achieve acceptable fit (χ²_red ≈ 1.97); extended fits are mathematically equivalent to J/ψ–vector-glueball mixing scenarios, rendering glueball presence experimentally indistinguishable.

Conclusion: The ρπ puzzle is likely due to non-negligible additional amplitudes in J/ψ → VP decays—not suppression in ψ(2S)—and current data cannot confirm or exclude a vector glueball component due to degeneracy in effective descriptions.

Abstract: The $12\%$ rule relates hadronic decays of the $J/ψ$ and $ψ(2S)$ through the ratio of their wave functions at the origin, but is strongly violated in several vector--pseudoscalar ($VP$) channels, most prominently in the $ρπ$ one. We study these decays within an effective chiral framework including strong and electromagnetic interactions, with the latter implemented through vector meson dominance. The $ψ(2S)\to VP$ data are described well by a relatively minimal model with three free parameters, yielding $χ^2_{\rm red}\simeq 6.71/(11-3)=1.12$. In contrast, the corresponding description of the $J/ψ$ decays is insufficient ($χ^2{\rm _{red}}\simeq 132/(11-3)=16.5$) and requires additional $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ flavor breaking and electromagnetic contributions for a fair agreement with data: $χ^2_{\rm red}\simeq 9.87/(11-6)= 1.97$. In the past, a small mixing of $J/ψ$ with an invisible vector glueball was considered as a way to describe the decay patterns. However, we will show that our extended fits are `de facto' equivalent to those where a vector glueball mixes with the $J/ψ$, so that we cannot distinguish whether or not the vector glueball is present. Our results suggest that an important part of the $ρπ$ puzzle may arise from additional contributions to the $J/ψ\to VP$ amplitudes rather than from a suppression mechanism specific to the $ψ(2S)$.

</details>


### [143] [A closer look at the LZ 248 keV event through the lens of cosmic-ray boosted dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24982)
*Bhavesh Chauhan,Soham Sahasrabuddhe,Manibrata Sen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores whether cosmic ray boosted dark matter (CRDM) with spin-dependent pseudoscalar interactions can explain a single 248 keV nuclear recoil event observed by LZ, finding that a ~300 MeV pseudoscalar mediator with O(1) couplings—rather than a contact interaction—is viable and avoids excess low-energy events.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the unexplained 248 keV nuclear recoil event reported by LZ while respecting null results at lower energies, testing CRDM as a plausible astrophysical origin beyond standard WIMP paradigms.

Method: Modeling CRDM production via cosmic-ray collisions, relativistic propagation through the Galaxy, and spin-dependent scattering off xenon nuclei mediated by a pseudoscalar; comparing predicted recoil spectra to LZ data, including kinematic thresholds and effective operator validity.

Result: A pseudoscalar mediator with mass ~300 MeV and O(1) couplings reproduces the 248 keV event without generating excess recoils below threshold; the non-relativistic contact operator O₆ fails due to breakdown of effective field theory (momentum transfer ~ mediator mass).

Conclusion: CRDM with a light pseudoscalar mediator is a phenomenologically viable explanation for the LZ anomaly, highlighting the importance of proper relativistic treatment and mediator mass in interpreting rare-event signals.

Abstract: We investigate whether cosmic ray boosted dark matter (CRDM) can explain the 248 keV nuclear recoil event observed by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration. We consider spin-dependent interactions, mediated by a pseudoscalar, and incorporate relativistic kinematics in CRDM production, propagation, and detection. We find that spin-dependent interactions can produce sufficient number of nuclear recoils in the observed region of interest, without populating events at lower energies, which have not been observed. However, for a benchmark pseudoscalar interaction corresponding to the non-relativistic effective operator $\mathbb{O}_6$, the coupling required to produce the observed event corresponds to an energy scale comparable to the momentum transfer scales, thus making contact interaction approach challenging. Instead, we find that a pseudoscalar mediator with mass $\sim 300\,$MeV and $O(1)$ couplings can provide a promising solution to this unexplained event.

</details>


### [144] [Three-Flavoured Thermal Leptogenesis Without Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24990)
*Angus Spalding*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a singlet-scalar extension of the Type-I seesaw mechanism to enable thermal leptogenesis with hierarchical right-handed neutrinos at the electroweak scale, avoiding fine-tuning and hierarchy problems inherent in minimal models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve tensions in minimal Type-I seesaw leptogenesis: hierarchical spectra require high scales (worsening the Higgs hierarchy problem), while low-scale leptogenesis typically demands fine-tuned quasi-degeneracy or cancellations in light-neutrino mass generation.

Method: Introduces a real singlet scalar φ coupling non-diagonally to right-handed neutrinos via -y_{ij}φN_iN_j (i≠j); computes scalar-induced CP asymmetry analytically; solves fully flavoured Boltzmann equations and applies the density-matrix formalism for leptogenesis dynamics.

Result: Derives an analytic upper bound on the scalar-induced CP asymmetry; demonstrates numerically that observed baryon asymmetry Y_B ≈ 8.7×10⁻¹¹ can be reproduced with hierarchical RH neutrino masses as low as the electroweak scale (~100 GeV–1 TeV).

Conclusion: The singlet-scalar extension provides a viable, natural framework for low-scale thermal leptogenesis without requiring degenerate RH neutrino masses or light-neutrino mass cancellations, thus alleviating both the gravitino problem and the Higgs hierarchy problem.

Abstract: We investigate thermal leptogenesis in an extension of the Type-I seesaw in which a real singlet scalar couples non-diagonally to the right-handed neutrinos. The interactions $-y_{ij}φN_iN_j$, with $i\neq j$, introduce new sources of CP violation in heavy-neutrino decays with a negligible enhancement of the standard leptonic washout. We derive an analytic upper bound on the scalar-induced CP asymmetry and present numerical results using fully flavoured Boltzmann equations and the density-matrix formalism. We show that the observed baryon asymmetry can be generated with a hierarchical right-handed-neutrino spectrum at the electroweak scale. In the minimal Type-I seesaw, lowering the scale of decay leptogenesis typically requires either a tuned quasi-degeneracy of the right-handed-neutrino masses or cancellations between the tree-level and one-loop contributions to the light-neutrino masses, while hierarchical leptogenesis instead requires a high scale that aggravates the Higgs hierarchy problem. This singlet-scalar extension appears to avoid these tensions, allowing successful leptogenesis with a hierarchical right-handed-neutrino spectrum down to the electroweak scale.

</details>

### [145] [First-Principles Determination of the QCD Contribution to the Axion-Photon Coupling Using Domain-Wall Fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22698)
*Tian Lin,Hao-Yang Cheng,Xu Feng,Lu-Chang Jin,Chuan Liu,Qi-Yuan Luo*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper presents a high-precision lattice QCD calculation of the QCD contribution $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}$ to the axion-photon coupling, resolving prior discrepancies between NLO chiral perturbation theory (ChPT) predictions by identifying and correcting systematic sources; it yields $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD} = 1.965(35)$ via pseudoscalar-to-two-photon three-point functions and anomalous Ward identities using domain-wall fermions on near-physical $N_f=2+1$ ensembles with continuum extrapolation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve puzzling ~8% discrepancies among existing next-to-leading-order chiral perturbation theory (NLO ChPT) predictions for the QCD-induced axion-photon coupling $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}$, and to provide a model-independent, first-principles benchmark for experimental axion searches and tests of the strong CP problem solution.

Method: Lattice QCD computation of pseudoscalar-to-two-photon three-point functions using domain-wall fermions on two near-physical $N_f=2+1$ ensembles; separation of $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}$ into exact chiral anomaly term and light-quark-mass-suppressed correction via anomalous Ward identities; controlled continuum extrapolation.

Result: $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}^{\rm IS} = 1.619(30)$ (isospin-symmetric), $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}^{\rm IB} = 0.347(22)$ (isospin-breaking), total $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD} = 1.965(35)$; identifies and reconciles sources of tension with published NLO ChPT results.

Conclusion: The lattice result provides the most precise first-principles determination of $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}$ to date, serves as a quantitative benchmark for ChPT frameworks, and confirms consistency with NLO ChPT once systematic differences—particularly in regularization, renormalization, and isospin treatment—are properly accounted for.

Abstract: The axion-photon coupling, crucial for experimental axion searches and tests of the strong CP solution, receives a substantial model-independent contribution $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}$ from QCD dynamics. Next-to-leading-order chiral perturbation theory (NLO ChPT) in different frameworks has yielded puzzling discrepancies of up to $8\%$, motivating precise first-principles calculations. We present an independent lattice QCD determination using a method complementary to the recent background-field calculation. Computing pseudoscalar-to-two-photon three-point functions and exploiting anomalous Ward identities, we separate $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}$ into an exact anomaly contribution and a light-quark-mass-suppressed correction. The latter is computed using domain-wall fermions, whose excellent chiral symmetry strongly suppresses discretization effects. Working on two near-physical $N_f=2+1$ ensembles with continuum extrapolation, we obtain $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}^{\rm IS}=1.619(30)$ (isospin-symmetric), $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}^{\rm IB}=0.347(22)$ (isospin-breaking), and $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}=1.965(35)$ in total. While direct comparisons with published NLO ChPT predictions reveal apparent tensions, we identify their sources and show that the ChPT results can be reconciled with our lattice determination. Our result provides a first-principles benchmark for the QCD contribution to the axion-photon coupling and a quantitative test of competing ChPT descriptions.

</details>


### [146] [Flowed quark field renormalization in lattice QCD: A Ward-identity approach and its validation using quark bilinears](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23379)
*Mei-Qi Gao,Long-cheng Gui,Jun Hua,Jian Liang,Jun Shi*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces a non-perturbative Ward-identity-based method to compute the flowed quark field renormalization factor $Z_χ$, using vector-current normalization and ratios of flowed/unflowed meson two-point functions—avoiding costly ringed diagrams—and validates it across multiple channels and lattice ensembles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome computational inefficiencies and ambiguities of the conventional ringed prescription for flowed quark field renormalization in lattice QCD, especially for fermionic operators.

Method: A Ward-identity prescription grounded in vector-current conservation; constructs $\mathring{Z}_χ^{V}(t_f,a)$ from ratios of flowed and unflowed meson two-point functions and imposes vector-current normalization; applied on five $2+1$-flavor clover ensembles with sequential continuum ($a \to 0$) and zero-flow-time ($t_f \to 0$) extrapolations.

Result: $\mathring{Z}_χ^{V}(t_f,a)$ is successfully determined and cross-validated in pseudoscalar, scalar, axial-vector, and tensor channels; renormalized matrix elements agree with independent RI/MOM and RI/SMOM results; discretization-dependent differences in $Z$-factors diminish toward finer lattices.

Conclusion: The proposed Ward-identity prescription is viable, systematically validated, and provides a robust, non-perturbative framework for renormalizing flowed fermionic operators in future high-precision lattice calculations.

Abstract: We present a non-perturbative Ward-identity prescription for determining the flowed quark field renormalization factor $Z_χ$, avoiding the computational difficulties of the conventional ringed prescription. The method is based on vector-current normalization and ratios of flowed and unflowed meson two-point functions. We determine the resulting $\mathring{Z}_χ^{V}(t_f,a)$ on five $2+1$-flavor clover ensembles and validate it in the pseudoscalar, scalar, axial-vector, and tensor channels. Renormalized matrix elements obtained through sequential continuum and zero-flow-time extrapolations agree with independent RI/MOM and RI/SMOM determinations. The finite-lattice-spacing bilinear renormalization factors show differences that decrease toward finer lattices, reflecting the different discretization effects of the renormalization methods. The cross-channel agreement demonstrates the viability of the proposed prescription; together, the method and its systematic validation establish a robust foundation for the non-perturbative renormalization of flowed fermionic operators in future lattice calculations.

</details>
